# gEdit: implementation plan for Phase 4

Scope: the roadmap's Phase 4, **"Comfort and geometry"** (`docs/planning/roadmap.md`): translate, rotate and mirror a program's path, expand drilling cycles into plain moves, report path lengths and an estimated run time, and give the editor the comfort features long-time users of NC editors expect (split view, printing, finding files in folders, typing help, named bookmarks, block ranges, inserting files, file associations, renumbering options, the side-by-side channel view, word-level compare marks, shortcut and colour settings, script packages, a form editor for profiles). Phase 4 is finished when the exit criteria in §2.2 pass.

Status: **plan of record since 2026-10-12, the owner's answers to §9 are in; P4a is on `main` (v1.1.0, draft built); P4e is built, reviewed and fixed (2026-10-17) and lands next**. The owner answered all 25 questions of §9 on 2026-10-12: 20 as proposed and 5 changed (Q4-2, Q4-9, Q4-14, Q4-23 and Q4-24), each marked under its question. The plan splits the work into eight milestones, in order of value; since 2026-10-17 P4e (the side-by-side channel view) comes right after P4a, then P4b, P4c, P4d and the rest (§9, "Channel view and order"):

| Milestone | What it delivers | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **P4a "Move the path"** | translate, rotate and mirror as bundled scripts on the Python modal interpreter, with a shared path tracker taken out of the extents script; on a lathe, translate and a Z mirror for the counter spindle; what cannot be judged is refused and listed, and a refused block means nothing is written | on `main` (`733f2e1`), released as v1.1.0 |
| **P4b "Drilling cycles and time"** | expand drilling cycles into rapid and feed moves; path lengths and an estimated time per tool; a machine's motion data (rapid rates, tool-change time, top feed) and limits (top spindle speed, travel) as a new, optional block of the machine configuration, also read by the program checks | built, reviewed and fixed on `v12/wave` (2026-10-22); waiting for the wave's runs |
| **P4c "Editor comfort"** | auto-space and an automatic block number on Enter; bookmark names, the bookmark panel and "bookmark all matches"; select or delete a block range; insert file and append file; printing | built, reviewed and fixed on `v12/wave` (2026-10-22); waiting for the wave's runs |
| **P4d "Renumber, all of it"** | the advanced renumber options; `M99 P` rewritten instead of reported, `M98 P… Q…` followed into the called program and reported; references from other open documents listed and, on request, updated | built, reviewed and fixed on `v12/wave` (2026-10-22); waiting for the wave's runs |
| **P4e "Side by side"** | split view (two editor groups on one model); the channel view (one channel pane per channel, up to four) and merging split channel documents back; word-level marks in the comparison; the aligned NC-aware diff with merge | built, reviewed and fixed (2026-10-17); lands next |
| **P4f "Folders and channels"** | find files in folders; OS file associations with a single running instance; folder-to-machine rules; the folder-aware sibling lookup, writing split channels to disk, channel-aware compare and search, and a wait-code check that follows jumps | planned |
| **P4g "Settings, keys and packages"** | user keyboard shortcuts with conflict warnings, a key per script, script packages that can be switched on and off, export and import of the whole configuration, a settings search box, and the switch for a second interface language | planned |
| **P4h "Profiles and machines"** | the form-generated profile editor with a live preview, JSON Schemas for profiles and machines, per-profile colours and the role colour editor, the remaining machine parameters (block-aware units for the `G76` thread height, the per-class number input in the dialog), and the small per-profile options the owner keeps | planned |

It **reuses the Phase 2 execution protocol** (`phase-2-implementation.md` §5, cited as **P2 §5**, which reuses P1 §4), as amended by the Phase 3 plan (`phase-3-implementation.md` §4, cited as **P3 §4**), and the contracts of P2 §7 and P3 §6. They stay binding unless §4 or §6 of this document changes them. Numbering continues Phase 3: verified facts **F110+**, architecture decisions **AD-41+**, exit criteria **X19+**, contract deviations **#292+** in §7 (continuing P3 §7), work packages **P4.1+**, owner questions **Q4-1+** (§9).

Path shorthands as in P2 and P3: `SP` = a scratch directory outside the repo; `P2`, `P3` = the Phase 2 and Phase 3 plans. All other paths are relative to the repository root (branch `p4a/base` from `main` @ `e344d61`, the plan of record with the owner's answers). The repository is public: this plan names no local machine paths, and no real program, file name of a real program or content derived from one appears in it.

---

## 0. Where this plan comes from

- **The roadmap's Phase 4 table** (the old Phase 3 before the re-cut of 2026-09-30, D69) and its exit criteria: "geometry transforms and cycle expansion work reliably on the fixture set, and the editor has the comfort features long-time users expect". Every row is in §2.1 with a decision; the rows the roadmap marked "if asked for", "only if review mode proves too limited" or "or cut" were settled by the owner on 2026-10-12 (§9).
- **The deferrals of Phase 2** that the roadmap lists in Phase 4, with their reasons in [P2 §11](phase-2-implementation.md#11-deferred-or-cut-items-phase-2--phase-3-or-backlog): items 2, 4, 5, 6, 8, 14, 15, 16, 17, 21, 26, 27 and 28. Item 27 (the side-by-side channel view) is the reason split view comes first in P4e: its groundwork is a second editor and a model per pane, which split view builds.
- **What bug-fix round 1 and the TODO left in Phase 4 areas:** the `G76` second-block `P` (thread height) shown as written because the unit readers are not block-aware (TODO, "Two-block lathe cycles, what is left"); the Fanuc lathe `G83`–`G89`, the Okuma `G181`–`G189` and the Klartext pattern cycles without position roles (TODO, "Code-database gaps"); two instances of the app overwriting each other's state (TODO, "Data safety"), which file associations would make common; the extents' unresolved cycle depths and Okuma `L` radii (TODO, "M10 data gaps").
- **The owner's standing rules** (the memory notes, P2 §10, the handover): CAM output first and keep it simple; no backplot, no simulation, no DNC, no program management; machine-dependent readings are machine configurations with documented defaults; tapping speeds are left and reported, never scaled; the commercial reference editor is never named; manuals are sources of facts in our own words; an independent corpus of programs may be read for facts only, is never named or quoted, and evidence from it stays in `docs/planning/`; the owner's real programs stay local and are reported only as counts; a draft release follows each milestone that lands (D70; superseded on 2026-10-17, one release v1.2.0 with everything in TODO.md), and v1.0.0 stays unpublished (Q4-24); model and effort are chosen per task and shown per work package.
- **What the code says** (§1): the Python layer has modes and frames but no geometry model; the only position tracker and arc geometry are inside `extents.py`; the database has no flag that says "a drilling cycle with an R plane, a depth and a peck", no arc direction and no compensation side; the machine configuration has no rates or limits; the app has one code editor, no directory listing, no file associations and no print path. P4a and P4b therefore begin with data and a shared module, not with scripts.

## 1. Verified facts this plan relies on (at `f119784`)

- **F110. The Python modal state has no geometry.** `ModalInterpreter` (`src-tauri/resources/scripts/_nc_modal.py:487`, state at 1308–1332) tracks the plane (`sets.plane`, Klartext through `planeFromAxisWord`), absolute or incremental, the motion group as the last modal code, units, the feed unit (per minute, per revolution, per tooth, inverse time, travel time), the speed unit and clamp, diameter or radius (`diameter_reading`, 1448), open frames as a list of `{code, line, group}` without their values (1004–1069), tool centre point control and the rotary axes' state. The active cycle is `{code, line, pitchFeed}` only (937): **no cycle parameter is kept**. Sinumerik `TRANS`/`ATRANS` are a shift, not a frame; Klartext cycle 7 is an offset; Fanuc `G15`/`G16` are a frame (`polarCommand`); Sinumerik `AP=`, `RP=` and `G110`–`G112` are not in the database.
- **F111. The only position tracker and arc geometry live in `extents.py`.** `resolve_moves` (1043) resolves incremental moves from the last known position; `Extents.arc` (1110–1197), `centre_from_radius` (1522: a negative `R` is more than 180°, chord slack 0.002), `extremes` (1206) and `polar_move` (1230) do the geometry, from a vocabulary table at 93–121 (`G2`/`G3`, the profile's `arcCenter` and `arcCenterMode`, `R` and `CR`, Klartext `DR±`, `PR`/`PA`, `TURN=`, `AC()`/`IC()`). `CT`, `CTP` and `CIP` are always unresolved, and so are Okuma arcs with an `L` radius and `G102`/`G103`. The `R` plane of a drilling cycle is not computed and `G98`/`G99` are not read (they are a group without `sets`).
- **F112. Address arithmetic is the precedent for refusals.** `address_arithmetic.py` refuses a block per reason (`BLOCK_REASONS`, 245; `block_refusal`, 1380): an unknown code, a machine position (`G53`, `G28`, `M91`, `SUPA`), any open frame, a turned rotary axis without TCP, a call or modal macro with a chosen address, a cycle not reviewed or with a variable position, a cycle outside the XY plane, a pole it cannot move. It skips incremental words on add and subtract, variables, expressions, data words (`G92`, `G52`, `G10`) and FK points; it moves a cycle and the blocks that run it "in one piece, or not at all"; it reads every number on the machine and writes it back in its own form, rounding half away from zero only where the word's form forces it and reporting it ([user guide](../user/scripts.md#address-arithmetic)). A refused block is left and **the other blocks are changed**.
- **F113. The arc-centre convention is profile data.** `addresses.arcCenter` and `arcCenterMode: "incremental"` are in `fanuc-gcode.json`, `okuma-osp.json` and `sinumerik.json` (the lathe inherits); `AC()`/`IC()` override it per word; Klartext writes its centre as the pole `CC` (`pole: set`, used by `C`, `CR`, `CT`, `LP`, `CP`, `CTP`).
- **F114. The database has no arc direction and no compensation side.** `CodeSets` (`src/lib/core/codes/types.ts`) has `motion`, `path: 'arc' | 'cycle'` (read by the motion colours only), `radiusComp: 'on' | 'off'`, but nothing that says `G2` is clockwise or `G41` is left. Mirroring needs both, as data.
- **F115. There is no drilling-cycle flag.** Cycles are told by `group: 'cycle'`, `sets.cycle`, `modal`, `pitchFeed`/`tapping`, `blocks: 2`, `axisWords` and `CodeParam.position` (`'tool-axis' | 'none' | 'other' | 'mode'`, types.ts:77). Fanuc mill `G73`, `G74`, `G76`, `G81`–`G89`: `R` is tool-axis, `Q` a length, `P` a count, `K` a count. Fanuc lathe `G83`–`G89` and Okuma `G181`–`G189`: no position roles. Okuma `G80`–`G83` are contour (LAP) codes. Klartext `CYCL DEF 200`–`209`, `240`, `25x`, `262`: `Q203` tool-axis, the rest none; `CYCL CALL POS`/`PAT` positions `other`. Sinumerik `CYCLE81`–`89`, `CYCLE840`: `RTP`, `RFP`, `DP` (and `FDEP` of `CYCLE83`) tool-axis, `SDIS` and `DPR` none, `DTB` a dwell.
- **F116. A machine has no rates and no limits.** `MachineParams` (`src/lib/core/machines/types.ts:108`) has `numberInput`, `units`, `diameter`, `variants`, `modalInitial`; `channels` is stored in `MachineConfig.params` only and kept out of the effective key (P2 §7.16 #149). No rapid rate, top feed, tool-change time, top spindle speed, travel or acceleration exists anywhere; nothing converts a feed per revolution into a feed per minute or a surface speed into revolutions. Scale Feed's limits are form values. `MACHINES_VERSION` is 1 (Rust and TypeScript).
- **F117. The script contract.** Header fields `name`, `description`, `profiles`, `input`, `output`, `timeout`, `envelope`, `documents`, `params` (`src-tauri/src/scripts/meta.rs:57-67`); parameter types `number`, `integer`, `text`, `bool`, `choice`, `file`, `folder`, `address-list` (76–85); outputs `panel`, `replace`, `new-document` (one untitled document, `src/lib/app/scripts.ts:640-645`) and `report` (at most 5,000 rows and 1,000 findings). `documents` other than `active` is accepted with a warning and reset to `active` (`meta.rs:365-371`): **no script can read two documents today**. Every script is a command `script.run:<id>` without a key (`src/lib/contrib/scripts.ts:87`).
- **F118. One code editor.** `editorService.ts` drives one `IStandaloneCodeEditor` with one model per document; the compare diff editor is the only other widget, and its header records the two traps of any further instance (every editor option lands in the shared configuration service; a `theme` option sets the global theme; `src/lib/monaco/diff.ts:18-29`). No editor groups exist. View zones and scroll sync are public API (P2 F55); `setHiddenAreas` is not and fails over 300,000 lines (P2 F54).
- **F119. Keys.** `CommandDef.keys` is one key or `{mac, other}`; `findConflicts` (`src/lib/core/keys/keySpec.ts:340-357`) checks both platforms; `tests/unit/commandPins.test.ts` pins every shipped key against `tests/runtime/lib/shortcuts.js`. There is no user override file.
- **F120. Settings and texts.** Settings are one flat schema with `SETTINGS_VERSION` 1 (`src/lib/core/settings/schema.ts:26`); the dialog has five schema pages plus Machines and Profiles and no search. `t()` (`src/lib/i18n/index.ts`) has plurals through `Intl.PluralRules` and only English message files. The config folder is fixed (`src-tauri/src/paths.rs:407-416`); there is no zip support and no JSON Schema file for profiles or machines. M13 imports and exports single profile, code and machine files.
- **F121. Bookmarks** are line numbers without names, stored per file in `state.json` (at most 200 per file, 500 files; `src/lib/stores/fileMemory.ts`); there is no panel and no ribbon entry.
- **F122. Typing** reads `editing.forceUppercase` and `editing.preventLineJoin` only (`src/lib/contrib/typing.ts`); nothing handles Enter.
- **F123. Renumbering** implements `start`, `step`, `digits`, `max`, `onOverflow`, `spacesAfter`, `skipStartingWith`, `skipEmpty`, `restartAtProgramStart`, `onlyNumbered` and `altPrefixes`; `fitToMax`, `alignColumn`, `everyNth`, `skipContaining`, `skipFirst`/`skipLast`, `startTrigger` and `autoNumberOnEnter` are design-note names only. Reference rules (`src/lib/core/transforms/references.ts`): on Fanuc `M99 P` and `M98 P… Q…` are **reported**, `M98 Q` without `P` and `GOTO` are rewritten, `G70`–`G73 P/Q` are reported on the mill and rewritten on the lathe; Okuma `GOTO`, `IF` and `G85`–`G88 N…`; Sinumerik `GOTOF`/`GOTOB`/`GOTOC`. No rule for `G65`.
- **F124. The shell.** Tauri plugins: dialog, fs, window-state (`src-tauri/Cargo.toml:31-33`). No single-instance plugin (`src-tauri/src/recovery.rs:27-31` explains the heartbeat that replaces it), no `fileAssociations` in `tauri.conf.json`, `on_run_event` handles only `Exit` (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs:87-91`), no print path, and no command that lists a directory for the webview (`channels.rs` reads the metadata of named siblings only).
- **F125. Channels.** "Split into channel documents" makes untitled documents with a proposed name `<stem> <channel><ext>` (`src/lib/contrib/channels.ts:503-525`) and is a one-way copy; nothing shows two channels side by side.
- **F126. Compare** has review mode with six ignore options (`src/lib/core/compare/types.ts:76-81`); inside a changed line only Monaco's own character marks show.
- **F127. Tests.** A test that times work over 100,000 lines or more, or simulates typing, against a wall clock lives in `<name>.slow.test.ts` (`vitest.config.ts:8-36`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` "Tests"); the evidence gate exempts `docs/planning/` (`tests/unit/evidenceGate.test.ts`); no test checks the links of the Markdown documents.

### 2.2 Phase 4 exit criteria (testable)

Each criterion is proven by its milestone's tests and runtime scenarios on committed fixtures (synthetic, or the owner-public programs under `tests/fixtures/nc/owner-public/`); the owner's local programs are checked by G11 on their machine only, as counts. "Refused" always means: nothing in the document changes, and every reason is a row in Results with its line.

| # | Criterion | Proven by |
|---|---|---|
| X19 | **Translate.** On synthetic goldens for every built-in profile (`tests/fixtures/scripts/translate/<case>/`): a shift by dX, dY, dZ moves every absolute point, absolute arc centre (`AC()`, an absolute `arcCenterMode`), Klartext pole `CC`, cycle position and tool-axis cycle position (`R` under `G90`, `Q203`, `RTP`/`RFP`/`DP`), and leaves incremental words, incremental centres, radii and polar radii and angles as written; a lathe's dX is read as the machine reads `X` (diameter or radius, AD-43); each word is written back in its own form. A shift of 0, 0, 0 gives the program back **byte for byte** on every owner-public program it does not refuse; a shift by d and then by −d gives every word back its value. The extents of the result equal the extents of the original moved by the shift on every golden and every owner-public program it does not refuse (the differential oracle, §4 rule 7). | `test_translate.py`, `test_geometry_oracle.py`, `p4-geometry` |
| X20 | **Rotate.** In the XY plane of a milling profile, about a point by an angle: every point, arc centre vector and incremental move is rotated; `G2`/`G3` stay; `X` and `Y` are both written on every moved block (a block that wrote only one gets the other); Klartext `PA` turns by the angle, `IPA` stays; a rotation by 90°, 180° or 270° is exact; any other angle rounds each word to its resolution (AD-44) and every arc keeps its start and end radius within the tolerance of Q4-4, or the run is refused. Rotating by 360° gives the program back byte for byte; four rotations by 90° give every value back. | `test_rotate.py`, the oracle |
| X21 | **Mirror.** About the X or the Y axis through a point, or a line at an angle, in the XY plane of a milling profile: points and arc vectors mirror, `G2`↔`G3` and `G41`↔`G42` swap (Klartext `DR+`↔`DR-`, `RL`↔`RR`) **from data** (AD-42), and the result carries one warning that climb milling became conventional milling; mirroring twice gives every value back. **Lathe Z mirror** (the counter spindle; ZX plane, `G18`): Z mirrors about the Z the user gives (default Z0), the arc centre vector along Z (`K`) changes sign, an incremental Z and `W` change sign, `X` and `U` and `I` stay, `G2`↔`G3` and `G41`↔`G42` swap from data, and the result carries a warning that the tool nose direction in the offset must be set for the counter spindle. Goldens on `fanuc-lathe` system A and system B, the Okuma lathe and Sinumerik turning; mirroring twice gives every value back. | `test_mirror.py`, the oracle |
| X22 | **Refused, not guessed.** Each of these refuses the whole run with its reason and its line, and writes nothing (AD-41, Q4-1): a frame or shift in force (`G68`, `G51.1`, `G16`, Sinumerik `ROT`/`MIRROR`/`SCALE`/`TRANS`, Klartext cycles 7, 8, 10, 26, `PLANE`, cycle 19); tool centre point control with a rotary word in scope (rotate, mirror); a variable or expression in a moved word; a subprogram or macro call (`M98`, `G65`, `G66`, `CALL LBL`, `CALL PGM`, `L…`, a Sinumerik name call); a coordinate setting (`G92` as a setting, `G52`, `G10`); a cycle with a parameter whose role is `other` or not reviewed (pockets, patterns, `PATTERN DEF`); an FK contour; `CT`, `CTP`, `CIP` under rotate and mirror; Okuma `L`-radius arcs and `G102`/`G103`; a plane other than XY (rotate, mirror; the lathe Z mirror needs `G18`); a rotate on a lathe; a mirror of X on a lathe; under the lathe Z mirror: a thread cycle (`G76`, `G92`, `G32`), a roughing cycle `G71`–`G73` (refused in the first version), Sinumerik `DIAMON`/turning cycles (the list of the P4a prelude decides, item 5: the turning cycles are refused, `DIAMON` refuses nothing, §7 #295; the reason ids are §6.1's); a point-less word whose reading depends on the machine with no machine chosen (AD-31, D57); a distance mode that is only assumed (Q4-5); an axis the shift must move that the program never writes before its first moved block; a selection that starts or ends inside a cycle or a Klartext multi-line block. `G53`, `M91`, `SUPA` and incremental `G28`/`G30` returns are left and listed as information; an absolute intermediate point that the transform would change refuses the run (Q4-6). | the goldens' `refused` cases, `p4-geometry` |
| X23 | **Expand drilling cycles.** Each cycle of the expansion schema (Q4-8) at the positions of its modal block and of the blocks that repeat it becomes the rapid and feed moves the manual describes, with `G98`/`G99` (or the dialect's return), the `R` plane or set-up clearance, the depth, absolute or incremental, the peck, the dwell copied as the same word, the repeat count, and the motion mode after the cycle restored; the `G73` retract and the `G83` clearance are asked in the form on each run, prefilled from the machine's motion block when it sets them, and the run does not start without a value in the form when the program contains either cycle (Q4-9); a tapping cycle, an oriented or back boring cycle, a cycle under radius compensation, and a cycle the database gives no schema are refused with the reason. Every golden's expansion has the same extents as the cycle (the depth reached, the return level) as `extents.py` reads them. | `test_expand_cycles.py`, `p4-cycles` |
| X24 | **Statistics and time.** Per tool and in total: feed length, rapid length, arc length included, the number of tool changes, and with the machine's motion data the feed time, rapid time, dwell time and tool-change time; feeds per revolution use the speed in force, constant surface speed uses the diameter along the move and the clamp in force (or the machine's top speed); expanded drilling cycles counted; inverse-time feeds counted; a multi-pass lathe cycle, a thread cycle, a contour cycle, a pocket or pattern cycle, a feed per tooth, a call and a rotary move without a rotary rate are counted as "not estimated" with their lines; so is a `G73` or `G83` while the statistics form has no retract or clearance for it (the form asks the same two fields as the expansion, prefilled from the machine; a report is still given for the rest, Q4-9). Without motion data the lengths are given and the time columns say what to set. The goldens hold hand-computed values to 0.01 mm and 0.1 s. | `test_statistics.py`, `p4-cycles` |
| X25 | **Machine motion and limits.** A machine stores an optional `motion` block (rapid rate per axis, rapid kind, top feed, tool-change time, rotary rapid, the `G73` retract and `G83` clearance as the prefill of the expand form, Q4-9) and a `limits` block (top spindle speed, travel per axis), edited on a page of its own in the machine dialog, validated with JSON paths, exported and imported with the machine, outside the effective key (AD-46); the program checks report a speed above the top speed and a position outside the travel (in machine coordinates only where the program says so, otherwise information), and nothing when the block is absent. | `motion.test.ts`, `test_program_checks.py`, `p4-machines` |
| X26 | **Typing help.** With the profile's `editing.autoSpace` on, typing `G1X` gives `G1 X`, and nothing changes inside a comment, a string, a variable expression, a multi-letter keyword or a Sinumerik `NAME=` word; with `editing.autoNumber` on, Enter after `N120 G1 X5.` starts `N130 ` (the profile's step), Enter between `N120` and `N125` gives a number between them, with no gap the line stays unnumbered and the status bar says so, and on Klartext the blocks below are renumbered in the same undo step. Each is one undo step with the typed text. | `typing.test.ts`, `autoNumber.test.ts`, `p4-comfort` |
| X27 | **Bookmarks, ranges, files, print.** A bookmark can carry a name; the bookmark panel lists every bookmark of every open document with file, line, name and text, jumps, deletes one and clears all; "Bookmark All Matches" marks every line the current search finds; names persist per file. "Select Block Range" and "Delete Block Range" take line or block numbers and refuse a block number that does not exist or exists twice. "Insert File" and "Append File" read a file the way Open reads it (encoding, line endings normalized to the document's), as one undo step; appending after a program end warns; on Klartext the inserted blocks are renumbered and a second `BEGIN PGM` is refused. "Print" prints the document or the selection with colours or in black, line numbers optional, and a header with name, date and page. | unit tests, `p4-comfort` |
| X28 | **Renumber, all of it.** The renumber form offers fit to maximum, align to a column, every Nth line, skip lines containing a text, skip the first and last lines, and a start trigger; each has goldens on Fanuc mill and lathe, Okuma and Sinumerik; `M99 P` inside the same file is rewritten when exactly one program of the file calls its program, and `M98 P… Q…` is followed into the called program and the `Q` is reported (owner, 2026-10-19); a reference from another **open** document to a renumbered number is listed and, when the user confirms, updated in that document as its own undo step; a reference whose target is not in the file stays reported. | `renumber.test.ts`, `references.test.ts`, `p4-renumber` |
| X29 | **Split view and channels side by side.** "Split Editor" shows the document in two panes on one model (edits, undo, bookmarks, the inspector and the motion colours follow the focused pane); on a program with two to four channels (single file and one file per channel), "Channels Side by Side" opens one editable pane per channel, aligned at the wait codes with filler space, matched and unmatched marks marked differently, always scrolling together (the owner, 2026-10-17); an edit in a pane is one undo step of the document, and with one file per channel the view has one undo history (§9); "Merge Channels Back" writes the edited channel documents of a split into their source as one undo step; a program over 300,000 lines works the same with two panes (no hidden areas are used; waived for three and four panes, the owner, 2026-10-17, §9). | `editorGroups.test.ts`, `panes.test.ts`, `viewUndo.test.ts`, `channelPanes.test.ts`, `merge.test.ts`, `p4-split`, `p4-channels` |
| X30 | **Compare: word marks and the aligned diff.** In review mode and the raw view, a changed line marks the whole words that differ (`F1200` → `F1000` marks both words, not two digits), read with each side's tokenizer; a word that only moved is not marked. The **aligned NC-aware diff with merge** ([design](file-compare.md#aligned-nc-aware-diff-with-merge)) shows the two programs side by side lined up on their blocks, with the word marks inside a changed block, and merges a block or a word from either side into the other as one undo step of the target document; the review-mode ignore options apply to it. | `wordDiff.test.ts`, `alignedDiff.test.ts`, `p4-compare` |
| X31 | **Folders and files.** "Find in Folder" searches a folder the user picked (name pattern, text or regular expression, modified dates), streams the results into the Results panel, can be stopped, never follows a link out of the folder, and skips binary and oversized files with a count; a double-click on a `.nc` (or another registered extension) opens it in the running gEdit, as a tab, on the three platforms; a folder rule assigns a machine to the files of a folder; the sibling lookup finds the other channel file in the document's folder; the split channels can be saved to disk with the names of the pattern (each save asks before it overwrites); compare and Find All can be limited to a channel; the wait-code check follows a `GOTO` inside a channel. | Rust tests, unit tests, `p4-folders` |
| X32 | **Settings, keys and packages.** A user key for any command, stored in `keybindings.json`, wins over the default, warns on a conflict on either platform, and can be reset; a script can have a key; a package folder under `<config>/packages/` adds its scripts, profiles, code files and templates, and switching it off removes them without a restart; "Export Configuration" writes one zip with the settings, keys, profiles, code files, machines, scripts and packages, and "Import Configuration" shows what it will replace before it does; the configuration folder stays where it is (no relocation, no pointer file, Q4-23); the settings search box finds a setting by its label or help; the interface language follows the setting and falls back to English for a missing text. | unit tests, Rust tests, `p4-settings` |
| X33 | **Profiles and machines.** The profile form edits a user profile section by section, generated from the profile's JSON Schema, with help per field and a live preview on a sample program; every built-in profile and every user profile fixture validates the same under the schema and the hand-written validator; a profile's `colors` override role colours for that profile only; the role colour editor changes a role with a live preview and refuses a colour below the contrast limit; the `G76` thread height `P` is read as the machine reads a length in block 2; the per-class number input is editable in the machine dialog. | `schema.test.ts`, `profileForm.test.ts`, `p4-profiles` |
| X34 | **No regression, within the budgets.** Every earlier scenario passes (the cumulative suite `m0`–`m13`, `rp`, `p3a`, `p3b`, `b1`), changed only for the listed intentional changes; CI green on the three platforms. The new budgets: a geometry script and the expansion on 300,000 lines within the 300 s the M10 scripts allow (the same `timeout`), measured in `p4-perf`; auto-space and auto-number add ≤ 2 ms per keystroke at 300,000 lines; a second editor pane keeps the typing budget of P1 (split view, and the channel view with two panes; three and four panes have no budget, the owner, 2026-10-17, §9); "Find in Folder" yields its first result within 1 s on a folder of 10,000 files and can be stopped within 200 ms; the bookmark panel updates ≤ 16 ms with 1,000 bookmarks. | unit budgets, `*.slow.test.ts`, `p4-perf`, CI |

**Phase 4 is done when** X19–X34 pass (the rows the owner cut on 2026-10-12 are not part of it); the cumulative runtime suite passes on the GitHub macOS runner; G10 has signed off the new data (the drilling-cycle schemas, the arc-direction and compensation-side flags, the motion defaults) and the script texts; and the owner has done the manual checks of §10 that only a real window or a machine can do.

## 3. Architecture decisions

AD-19 (one modal interpreter, both languages), AD-31 (machine configurations, "no machine, no guess") and AD-32 (channels) of P2 §3, and AD-33–AD-40 of P3 §3 stay binding as written; the decisions below add to them.

**AD-41: The geometry transforms are scripts on one shared module, and a refusal means nothing is written.**
- Three bundled scripts, `translate.py`, `rotate.py`, `mirror.py` (the Tools tab's Built-in Scripts, each its own command), on a new helper module `_nc_geom.py`: the classification of every word of a block into a geometry role (absolute point, incremental point, arc centre absolute or incremental, radius, polar radius, polar angle absolute or incremental, pole, cycle position, tool-axis cycle position, data, untouched), the transform of a role, the write-back through `_nc_machine.py` in the word's own form, and the refusal reasons.
- **Refusal is all-or-nothing** (Q4-1, as the owner confirmed on 2026-10-12): address arithmetic changes the blocks it can judge and lists the rest (F112), which is right for a value change; for a path it is not — a contour moved except for one block is a broken contour. A geometry run that meets one refused block writes nothing and lists every refused block, so the user sees all of them at once.
- **Why scripts:** the roadmap's delivery column says Script; the work is a batch computation over the text on the Python interpreter, which is the oracle of the TypeScript one (AD-19), and users can read and adapt it (README principle 3). **Why not one script with an operation choice:** each transform has its own form (a shift, a centre and angle, an axis), and three commands are what a user looks for.
- **Why not a frame in the program** (inserting `G68`, `ROT`, cycle 10 or a datum shift instead of rewriting coordinates): that changes what the control does, needs the control's option, and leaves the path unreadable for the inspector and the checks; rewriting is what the design note asks for.

**AD-42: Mirroring swaps what the data says to swap.**
- New database flags, set by the P4a prelude and checked by G10: `CodeSets.arcDirection: 'cw' | 'ccw'` on the arc codes (`G2`/`G3` and their dialect forms) and `CodeSets.compSide: 'left' | 'right'` on the radius-compensation codes (`G41`/`G42`, Klartext `RL`/`RR` where they are entries); Klartext `DR+`/`DR-` read through a profile member that names the direction words of an arc (`addresses.arcDirectionWords`, the prelude's contract). `mirror.py` swaps a code for the code of the same group with the other value, so no code list lives in the script ("no consumer names a profile", P2 §8.8).
- A mirror also reverses the cutting direction: climb milling becomes conventional milling. The run says so in one warning; reversing the path to restore it is not offered (the design note keeps it for later, and it needs every block's order reversed).

**AD-43: Every number is read on the machine, and a lathe's X is a diameter when the machine says so.**
- The transforms read each word with `_nc_machine.py` (AD-31): a point-less word under "none" whose reading differs between the presets is refused, as in address arithmetic (D57).
- On a lathe, translate's dX is asked as the machine reads `X` (the form says "diameter" or "radius" and follows the document's machine; Sinumerik `DIAMON`/`DIAMOF`/`DIAM90` switches are followed per word, as `diameter_reading` does); `U` and `W` are incremental and stay.
- **The lathe Z mirror (Q4-2, decided 2026-10-12)** turns a main-spindle program into one for the counter spindle: Z mirrors about a Z the user gives (default Z0; `Z' = 2·Z0 − Z`), an incremental Z and `W` change sign, the arc centre vector along Z (`K`) changes sign, `X`, `U` and `I` stay (X is untouched, so no diameter reading is needed), and `G2`↔`G3` and `G41`↔`G42` swap **from the data of AD-42**, never from a code list. It works in the ZX plane (`G18`) only: this is the one lathe exception to the XY-only rule of Q4-3. **No rotation on a lathe**, and a mirror of X on a lathe is refused. Thread cycles, roughing cycles and Sinumerik turning cycles are refused in the first version (the prelude's case list decides the exact rule, P4a item 5). The result carries one warning that the tool nose direction (a number in the tool offset, not in the program) has to be set for the counter spindle.
- The distance mode at the first moved block comes from the program, or from the machine's power-on mode (`modalInitial`); a mode only assumed from the profile's default refuses the run (Q4-5). The design note's per-run options "arc-centre convention" and "distance mode to assume" are dropped: both are facts of the control (F113, the machine's power-on mode), and a run option would let a guess in (§7 records this).

**AD-44: Exact where it can be, rounded where it must be, and the arcs checked after rounding.**
- Translate, a mirror about an axis-parallel line through a decimal point, and a rotation by a multiple of 90° are computed exactly on decimal text (the `Decimal` rules of `_nc_machine.py`): no word is rounded.
- Any other rotation or mirror line computes in double precision and rounds each word half away from zero to its resolution: a point-less word to whole increments of the machine (IS-B 0.001 mm, an Okuma unit), a word with a point to the decimals the form asks (default: the word's own decimals, and at least three in mm, four in inch; Q4-4). Every rounded word is counted in the message.
- After rounding, every arc is checked: the distance from the centre to the start and to the end may differ by at most the tolerance of Q4-4, otherwise the run is refused at that arc (a control rejects or misreads such an arc). A radius arc keeps its radius; its chord is checked against it.
- Rotate writes `X` and `Y` on every moved block (a block that wrote only one now moves in both), from the position the path tracker knows; translate and the axis mirrors write only the words that were there. A block whose start position is unknown (no point written yet on an axis the transform needs) refuses the run.

**AD-45: Drilling cycles expand from a schema in the database, never from a code list.**
- A new member `CodeEntry.expand` (the P4b prelude's contract) says what a cycle does in steps the script knows: the approach (rapid to the position, rapid to the `R` plane or the set-up clearance above the surface), the feed in (in one, or in pecks with a retract to the `R` plane or by a distance), a dwell at the bottom, the way out (rapid, feed, feed with dwell, spindle stop and rapid), the return level (`G98`/`G99`, Klartext `Q204`, Sinumerik `RTP`), and which parameter is which (`CodeParam.expandRole`: position, r-plane, surface, depth, depth-relative, clearance, peck, dwell, return, feed, repeat). The expansion script reads only that.
- Only a cycle with a schema is expanded; the NC architect writes each schema from the manual, with a golden per cycle. Tapping cycles have no schema (a tap needs the spindle and the feed synchronized; plain moves break it, and tapping speeds are left as written by the owner's rule); nor do oriented boring (`G76`, the shift direction is a machine parameter), back boring (`G87`), manual-return boring (`G88`), the lathe cycles and Okuma's contour codes.
- **Machine-dependent steps are asked on each run** (Q4-9, decided 2026-10-12): the retract of a high-speed peck (`G73`) and the clearance of a deep peck (`G83`) are parameters of the control. The expansion form asks both **on each run**, prefilled from the machine's motion block (AD-46) when it sets them, and the field is required for a program that contains either cycle: without a value the two cycles cannot run, and the form says why. The motion mode after the cycle is the one the manual gives for the dialect, written on the next motion block when that block has no motion code of its own.
- The same expansion is used inside `time_estimate.py` (a module `_nc_cycles.py`), so the time estimate counts what the expansion would write. The statistics form asks the same two peck fields, prefilled the same way; it is a report, so it does not stop without them: a `G73` or `G83` without a value is counted as "not estimated" with its lines and the rest of the report is given (one rule for both scripts: the same fields and prefill, a refusal only where something would be written).

**AD-46: A machine's motion data and limits are an optional block of its configuration, outside the effective key.**
- `MachineConfig.params.motion` (rapid rate per axis in mm/min or in/min and the rotary rapid in degrees per minute, the rapid kind: `straight` (all axes on one line at the rapid rate) or `per-axis` (each axis at its own rate), the top cutting feed, the tool-change time in seconds, and the peck values of AD-45, which are only the prefill of the expand form) and `MachineConfig.params.limits` (top spindle speed per spindle, travel per axis in machine coordinates). Both optional, both additive: `MACHINES_VERSION` stays 1, as M12's `channels` did (D59).
- **Not in `EffectiveMachine.params` and not in `effectiveKey`** (the M12 pattern, P2 §7.16 #149): they do not change how a program is read, so changing a rate rebuilds no modal index. The script context gains `machine.motion` and `machine.limits` (additive, `contract` stays 2), absent when the machine has none.
- **Documented defaults:** none is a fact about any machine. A missing value is "not set" and the feature that needs it says so (the time columns, the peck prefill, the limit checks), instead of a number that looks like a machine's (Q4-10, Q4-11).

**AD-47: The time estimate is a lower bound and says so.**
- Time = length ÷ feed for every move, at 100 % override, with no acceleration, no block processing time and no look-ahead, so the machine is always slower; the report says this in one plain line. A feed per revolution is multiplied by the speed in force; under constant surface speed the speed follows the diameter along the move, held by the clamp in force or, without one, the machine's top speed (AD-46), and a move is split where the clamp starts to hold. Rapid moves use the machine's rapid kind and rates; the top cutting feed clamps a programmed feed above it. A dwell counts as written (the dwell class of `_nc_machine.py`). An inverse-time block takes 1 ÷ F minutes.
- What cannot be estimated is counted, never guessed: a multi-pass lathe cycle (`G71`–`G73`, `G76`), a thread or contour cycle, a pocket or pattern cycle, an Okuma LAP cycle, a feed per tooth (no tooth count is known), a subprogram call, a rotary move without a rotary rate.

**AD-48: The path tracker is taken out of `extents.py` before anything else uses it.**
- P4.1 moves the position tracker and the arc and polar geometry of `extents.py` (F111) into `_nc_path.py` without changing a result: every extents golden and every owner-public run stays byte for byte the same (the guard). The geometry scripts, the expansion and the statistics use the module; `extents.py` imports it.
- Why not a second tracker in `_nc_geom.py`: two trackers drift, and the TODO already lists duplicated script code as debt. Why not a TypeScript tracker: these are scripts (AD-41); the modal parity of AD-19 covers the modal state only, and the path tracker has no TypeScript reader.

**AD-49: Renumbering rewrites `M99 P` and `M98 Q` inside the file, reports `M98 P… Q…`, and lists what points in from outside.**
- A reference into another program of the **same file** (`M99 P40` in a subprogram whose single caller is in the file; `M98 Q` as a local call) is rewritten with the map of the program it points into, which the renumber builds per program (`restartAtProgramStart`). `M98 P1000 Q30` is **report-only** (owner, 2026-10-19): the called program is followed and renumbered and the `Q` is reported. A target that is not in the file stays reported, as today.
- **Other documents:** after a renumber, references in the other **open** documents that point at a renumbered program of this file (`M98 P<this program>`, `CALL` and the like, from the profile's reference rules) are listed in Results; "Update the other documents" rewrites them, each document as its own undo step, after one confirmation; a file that is not open is never read or written (proposed, Q4-17).

**AD-50: Editor groups: one model per document, the focused pane is the editor; split view has two panes, the channel view one per channel (up to four).**
- `EditorService` gains groups: two for split view (side by side or stacked), and for the channel view of AD-51 one per channel, up to four (the owner, 2026-10-17: a three-channel program must show all three channels at once); each group is one `IStandaloneCodeEditor`, created with the shared instance options (`instanceOptions.ts`) and never a `theme` option (F118). The same document can be in both, sharing its model; each group keeps its own view state. `editorInstance()` answers the focused group's editor, so every command, decoration and panel follows the focus; the decoration owners (bookmarks, motion colours, compare marks, the inspector's highlight) register per editor.
- Why the cap of four: the owner's machines run up to three channels, and each pane adds the view-model cost of P2 F56; split view keeps two, because the header-while-editing case needs no more. The prelude measures the typing budget with four panes on a 300,000-line program (X29, X34). *Changed 2026-10-17 from "at most two": the plan had read the roadmap's channel view as two-channel only.*

**AD-51: A channel pane is a model of its own with a two-way mapping into the document.**
- **One file with channel sections:** each pane shows a model built from that channel's lines; an edit in a pane is applied to the document's model as the minimal edit of the mapped lines (one undo step of the document), and a change of the document (a transform, a reload, a machine switch) re-projects the panes. **One file per channel:** each pane is that channel's own document (its own dirty state and save).
- Alignment at the wait codes with view zones and locked scrolling are public API (P2 F55); no hidden areas are used, so a program over 300,000 lines works the same (P2 F54).
- Merging split channel documents back into their source uses the same mapping: the split records where each channel came from, and "Merge Channels Back" writes the channel documents' current text into those ranges as one undo step of the source, refused when the source changed in those ranges since the split.

**AD-52: Word marks come from the tokenizer of each side.**
- A changed line pair is tokenized with each side's profile; words are aligned by a small diff over the word sequence; a word that differs is marked whole, as a decoration on top of Monaco's own diff. In review mode the marks are computed on the normalized text and shown on it.

**AD-53: One read-only folder walk in Rust serves every folder feature.**
- A command `folder_search` (streaming, cancellable, a folder the user picked in the dialog, read only, no link followed out of it, a cap on files and on file size, binary files skipped and counted, the name pattern and dates checked before a file is read) serves Find in Folder; a narrower `folder_siblings` (one folder, names only) serves the channel sibling lookup. Both are reviewed under G8's security part (a new command that takes a path).
- Why not the fs plugin's directory scope: it would grant the webview a directory, which P2 §4 kept closed; a command that only answers what the search asked keeps the grant narrow.

**AD-54: File associations need one running instance.**
- The installer registers the extensions the owner confirms (Q4-21); macOS sends `RunEvent::Opened` to the running app, Windows and Linux start a second process with the path. A second process must hand its paths to the first and end, or the two overwrite each other's state (TODO, "Data safety"). The single-instance plugin of Tauri does this. P2 D42 deferred file associations for three reasons: the plugin had to be fetched over the network, several extensions are shared with other programs, and the installers could not be tested here. P4f answers them in turn: the dependency is added in the P4f prelude with the owner's OK and its licence checked by `licenses:check`; the extensions are the owner's list (Q4-21); the installers are a manual check (§10).

**AD-55: User keys are one file of overrides, checked like the defaults.**
- `<config>/keybindings.json`: command id → key (or `null` to remove a key), per platform where needed; read and validated like the other user files (bounded, JSON paths in problems); applied over the defaults at start and on save; `findConflicts` runs on the merged set for both platforms, and a conflict is shown on the Keyboard page and never silently resolved. A script's key binds `script.run:<id>`. The Keyboard page edits the file; the shortcut reference (View ▸ Keyboard Shortcuts) shows the keys in force.

**AD-56: A script package is a folder that adds user files, and switching it off removes them.**
- `<config>/packages/<name>/` with `scripts/`, `profiles/`, `codes/` (templates included), and a small `package.json` (name, version, description). Its files are read with the user files' rules (limits, validation, the M13 merge), as a layer between the built-ins and the user's own files; a setting `packages.disabled` lists the switched-off ones; a reload follows a switch without a restart. A package is as trusted as a user script: it is the user's folder.

**AD-57: The JSON Schemas drive the profile form and are held to the hand-written validators.**
- `schemas/profile.schema.json` and `schemas/machines.schema.json` are written by hand from the types (draft 2020-12), shipped and referenced with `$schema` in exported files; the form editor generates its sections and help from them. The hand-written validators stay the gate (P2 F6): a test holds both to the same answer on every built-in profile, every fixture profile and a set of broken ones, so a schema that drifts fails CI.

## 4. Execution protocol: deltas to P2 §5 and P3 §4

Everything in [P2 §5](phase-2-implementation.md#5-execution-protocol-deltas-to-p1-4) and [P3 §4](phase-3-implementation.md#4-execution-protocol-deltas-to-p2-5) applies: a prelude with contracts and stubs, work packages in their own worktrees with disjoint `Owns` lines, Wave A from `p4x/base`, Wave B from the Wave A integration, integration, gates G0–G11, reviews, fixes, documentation, one squash commit on `main` per milestone. The changes:

1. **Branches.** Milestone `x` (`a`…`h`): `p4x/base` (the prelude) from `main`, work-package branches `p4x/<wp>`, Wave A integration `p4x/intA` where a milestone has two waves, integration `p4x/int`. Before landing: fetch, merge `origin/main` (the second session on the owner's Windows machine may have pushed; its commits are reviewed like any other change), run the gates again, then one squash commit on `main`. No push, tag or publication without the owner's say.
2. **A draft release after each milestone that lands — superseded on 2026-10-17 (the owner): no incremental minor releases; the next public release is v1.2.0 with everything in TODO.md, see [§9, Release scope and the waves](#9-owner-decisions-and-defaults). The text below is the history.** (D70, [P2 §5.3](phase-2-implementation.md#53-releases-after-each-milestone-accepted-2026-10-01-d70)): the version is bumped in the milestone's commit, the tag is pushed with the owner's OK, the workflow builds the draft, and only the owner publishes. Versions (Q4-24, decided 2026-10-12): **v1.0.0 is never published** (its draft was deleted on 2026-10-12, its tag stays as history). The first 1.x release is **v1.1.0, cut after P4a**; it carries bug-fix round 1 (CHANGELOG "Unreleased") and P4a, and its entry starts with one line saying that it includes v1.0.0, which was not published as a release. Each later milestone that lands is the next minor version. The Phase 1 exit check (the bundles on Windows and Linux) is part of the v1.1.0 release checklist.
3. **Model and effort per work package** (the owner's instruction; the agent types of the project): **Haiku, low** (`gedit-mech`) for exact mechanical recipes only, its result checked by the orchestrator; **Sonnet, medium** (`gedit-docs`) for narrow rules and documentation; **Sonnet, high** (`gedit-impl`) for well-scoped implementation, UI, integration, the harness and the code review; **Opus, high** (`gedit-nc-architect`) for NC-critical logic, NC data, the preludes of NC milestones and the NC review; **Opus, extra high** (`gedit-judge`) for the skeptic pass and exit-criteria verdicts. Every work package below names its model; the integrator of every milestone is Sonnet, high.
4. **Reviews.** After integration, the NC review (Opus, high; ids `NC-…`) and the code review (Sonnet, high; ids `CODE-…`, with the security part of G8 where a milestone adds a Rust command or a capability) run **in parallel and read-only**. The skeptic (Opus, extra high) verifies every finding and confirms, partly confirms or rejects it with the reason. Fixers get **only verified findings**, and every fix comes with a regression test that fails without it. A milestone without NC content (P4e, P4g) has no NC review unless it touches `src/lib/data/**`.
5. **Light test runs** (the owner, 2026-10-10; `CONTRIBUTING.md`, "Running the tests lightly"): `taskpolicy -b` for tests and builds; vitest on the related files with few workers; the full suite once at integration, at normal priority, on a quiet machine; Python locally on the newest interpreter only (CI runs 3.9 and 3.12); the runtime harness only on the GitHub runner, never locally (it drives the real mouse and keyboard). Long commands run in the background and are polled briefly; every wait loop is stopped before a package hands back.
6. **Slow tests.** A wall-clock test over 100,000 lines or more, or a typing simulation, goes into `<name>.slow.test.ts` next to its `<name>.test.ts` (F127); a property is pinned by a count where a count can replace a clock; a heavy measurement yields to the event loop between rounds (the CI "Timeout calling onTaskUpdate" lesson).
7. **The differential oracle for scripts** (P4a, P4b). A transform is checked against code that did not compute it: the extents of the transformed program against the transformed extents of the original (translate: the ranges shifted; axis mirror: the ranges reflected; rotation by 90°: the ranges turned), and the expansion's extents against the cycle's. After the reviews, an adversarial fuzz review (Opus, high, as the P3b fuzz review) runs the transforms on random programs from the six profiles and holds identity and round-trip properties (§2.2 X19–X21); its findings go through the skeptic like the others. **Done for P4a in v1.2.0 (BL-NC-fuzz, [§7 #501–#504](#7-where-phase-4-deviated-from-the-contracts)):** `tests/python/test_geometry_fuzz.py`, seven code sets, 2,642 programs in the review run; its open findings FZ-5 and FZ-8 go to BL-NC-geometry in Wave Y.
8. **G10** covers the new data (the arc-direction and compensation-side flags, the expansion schemas, the motion and limits defaults and their texts) and every golden of an NC fixture; **G11** on the owner's machine runs the three transforms (identity and round trip), the expansion and the statistics over the local programs and reports counts only (transformed, refused per reason, identity kept).
9. **The runtime harness.** Each milestone has its suite (`tests/runtime/suites/p4a.txt` …); scenarios that pin behaviour a milestone changes on purpose are found by grep and updated first; the cumulative suite runs on `spike/harness-ci` before landing only when the owner allows the force-push in that session, and on `main` after the landing in any case.
10. **Evidence and names.** No evidence about real programs outside `docs/planning/` (the evidence gate); no name of the commercial reference editor anywhere; manuals in our own words. Each milestone runs `tests/unit/evidenceGate.test.ts`.
11. **Contract deviations** are recorded in §7, numbered from #292.

## 5. Milestones

Each milestone below has a prelude, waves of work packages with disjoint `Owns`, an integration, the reviews of §4 rule 4, the gates and a commit. The status lines and "as built" notes are filled in as the milestones run.

### P4a: Move the path

**Goal:** a user moves a milling program by a shift, turns it about a point or mirrors it, on any built-in milling profile, moves a lathe program along X and Z, and mirrors a lathe program in Z for the counter spindle; what gEdit cannot judge is refused with every reason listed, and the document is not touched.

**Status:** prelude done on `p4a/base` (2026-10-12): the contracts §6.1 and §6.2, the stubs `_nc_path.py` and `_nc_geom.py` with their pending tests, the data flags on the six databases, the golden format with four example cases, the settled defaults in the stubs and the case list below (§7 #292–#297). Wave A (P4.1, P4.2) and its integration done on `p4a/intA` (2026-10-13); Wave B (P4.3–P4.5) and its integration fix batch on `p4a/intB` (2026-10-15, §7 #299–#310); the reviews and the fix batches B1–B5 with their follow-ups on `p4a/intC` (2026-10-16, §7 #311–#360, §9 "Review answers"); the performance batch (B6), the test batch (B7) and these documents (B8) run in parallel, then the runtime suite (G6) and the landing.

**What the prelude decides:**

1. The contracts of `_nc_path.py` (the tracker's API: the position before and after each block, the arc of a block with its centre, start and end, the polar move, the reasons a position is not known) and `_nc_geom.py` (the word roles of AD-41, `transform(role, value, op)`, the write-back, the refusal reasons with their plain texts), as stubs with docstrings and failing tests marked pending.
2. The data flags of AD-42 (`CodeSets.arcDirection`, `CodeSets.compSide`, the profile's arc direction words): types, the loader, `flagsContract.test.ts`, the Python reader; the flags are written on the six databases by the prelude itself (a short, exact list, checked by G10).
3. The golden format of `tests/fixtures/scripts/{translate,rotate,mirror}/<case>/` (`input`, `params.json`, `machine.json` optional, `expected` or `refused.json` with the reasons and lines), as the address arithmetic fixtures are.
4. The answers of §9 that the scripts need (Q4-1, Q4-2, Q4-4, Q4-5, Q4-6; all decided 2026-10-12), each in the scripts' header comments.
5. The list of cases the NC architect has to decide from the manuals, so the review decides and does not discover: Klartext `APPR`/`DEP` forms under rotate and mirror (proposed: points transformed, the angle words `CCA` and the arc direction follow the rules of `C`; any form not covered refused); Sinumerik `AR=` (an opening angle stays under rotation and changes sign under a mirror); Fanuc helical arcs (the third axis stays); `G2`/`G3` with `R` under a mirror (the radius stays, the direction swaps); the effect of the **lathe Z mirror** on thread cycles (`G76`, `G92`, `G32`: proposed refused), on the roughing cycles `G71`–`G73` (`W` and the `P`/`Q` contour: refused in the first version; the prelude says whether a later version can allow a cycle whose whole contour is in the selection) and on Sinumerik `DIAMON` and the turning cycles (proposed refused), and the wording of the tool-nose-direction warning.

**Decided by the prelude** (2026-10-12; the sources are the owner's control manuals, named by title, facts in our own words):

1. **Contracts** in §6.1 (the tracker and the transform rules) and §6.2 (the flags), with the stubs `src-tauri/resources/scripts/_nc_path.py` and `_nc_geom.py` and the pending tests `tests/python/test_nc_path.py` (P4.1) and `tests/python/test_nc_geom.py` (P4.2). The final parts of the stubs run now: the tracker's data types and number helpers (copied from `extents.py`, held to it by a test), the roles, operations, refusal reasons, warnings and tolerances of `_nc_geom.py`.
2. **The flags** on the six databases, 16 entries, each marked `review: "pending"`: `arcDirection` on `G2` (`cw`) and `G3` (`ccw`) of Fanuc (the lathe and system B inherit them), Okuma and Sinumerik; `compSide` on `G41` (`left`) and `G42` (`right`) of Fanuc, the Fanuc lathe (its own entries), Okuma and Sinumerik, and on Klartext `RL`/`RR`; the Klartext profile's `addresses.arcDirectionWords` `{ "DR+": "ccw", "DR-": "cw" }`. Left without a flag on purpose (§7 #297), so a mirror refuses them: Fanuc `G41.2`–`G42.6` and `G41.3` (five-axis and leading-edge compensation), Okuma `G102`/`G103`/`G132`/`G133` (C-axis contour arcs), Sinumerik `CIP`/`CT` and the Klartext tangential arcs (their direction follows from the path), Klartext `C`/`CR`/`CP` (direction by the `DR` word). Nothing was left undecided by the manuals: every flagged code's direction or side is stated in its manual.
3. **The golden format** in `tests/fixtures/scripts/README.md` ("The geometry goldens"), read by `helpers.geometry_cases` and `helpers.geometry_context`, with one example case per script (`translate/fanuc-mill-shift`, `rotate/fanuc-mill-90`, `mirror/lathe-z-counter-spindle`) and one refused case (`mirror/lathe-thread-refused`); the runners in `test_translate.py`, `test_rotate.py`, `test_mirror.py` are skipped until P4.3 and P4.4 land (§7 #293).
4. **The settled defaults** Q4-1 to Q4-6 (Q4-3 with the lathe Z mirror as its one exception) are the header of `_nc_geom.py`.
5. **The case list**, decided from the manuals:
   - **Klartext `APPR`/`DEP` under rotate and mirror: allowed.** The first contour point of `APPR LT`, `LN`, `CT`, `LCT` and the end point of `DEP LCT` (and their `Z`) are points; `LEN`, the centre angle `CCA` (entered positive, at most 360°) and the radius `R` keep their values: the direction of the approach arc follows from the first (or last) contour element, and the sign of `R` says on which side of the compensation the tool comes in, so swapping `RL`↔`RR` from data keeps it right. This differs from the proposal ("`CCA` follows the rules of `C`"): `CCA` has no sign to follow. The polar forms (`APPR PLT`, `PLN`, `PCT`, `PLCT`, `DEP PLCT`) are not in the database and are refused like any form not covered. Source: HEIDENHAIN TNC 640 Klartext user's manual, approaching and departing the contour. (`CT`, `CTP` and `CIP` stay refused under rotate and mirror as X22 says; a later version can allow them under the exact operations, because their arcs follow from the transformed path.)
   - **Sinumerik `AR=`: stays under rotate and mirror.** The opening angle is unsigned (0 to 360°) and the direction is the code's, which the mirror swaps; the plan's proposal (a sign change under a mirror) would make a mirrored arc invalid. An arc by its centre and `AR=` with no end point leaves the position after it not known (§7 #294), so the next block that needs it refuses a rotate (`position-unknown`). The arc check does not apply to `AR=` arcs: the control computes the missing point, so no radius mismatch can arise. Source: SINUMERIK 840D sl programming manual, fundamentals, circular interpolation with opening angle.
   - **Fanuc helical arcs: the axes beyond the plane stay.** Up to two further axes move linearly with the arc; translate shifts their absolute values, rotate and mirror in XY leave them. A mirror turns a helix the other way, so a thread milled with it changes hand: the result carries the warning `helix-hand` (the Klartext manual's helix table shows the same: the hand follows from the turning direction and the direction along the tool axis). Sources: FANUC Series 30i/300i/300is Model A operator's manual, common to lathe and machining centre (helical interpolation); TNC 640 Klartext user's manual (helix).
   - **`G2`/`G3` with `R` under a mirror: the radius stays, sign included, and the direction swaps.** A negative radius means the arc over 180°, and a mirror keeps the angle an arc spans. The same holds for Sinumerik `CR=` (negative over 180°), Klartext `CR … R±` (the sign picks the larger or smaller arc, `DR` swaps) and Okuma `L` with `CALRG` (Okuma `L` arcs stay refused by X22; a later version can allow them under the exact operations). Sources: FANUC 30i operator's manual, common part (circular interpolation); SINUMERIK 840D sl programming manual; TNC 640 user's manual; Okuma OSP-P200L programming manual (direct radius command).
   - **The lathe Z mirror and threads: refused (`thread`).** Every block whose motion code or cycle has `pitchFeed` or `tapping`: `G32`/`G33`, `G76`, `G92` (system A), `G78` (system B), Okuma `G31`–`G37`, `G71`, `G72`, `G112`/`G113`, Sinumerik `G33`–`G35`, `G331`/`G332`; the Sinumerik thread cycles `CYCLE97`, `CYCLE98`, `CYCLE99` are refused as turning cycles. The hand of a thread follows from the direction of travel along Z together with the spindle's direction; the mirror reverses the travel, and whether the counter spindle's `M3` turns the same way seen from the part is a fact of the machine the program does not state. Sources: FANUC 30i lathe operator's manual (thread cutting, multiple threading cycle); Okuma OSP-P200L (thread cutting).
   - **The lathe Z mirror and `G71`–`G73`: refused in the first version (`roughing-cycle`).** A later version **can** allow a roughing cycle whose whole contour (`P` to `Q`) is inside the scope: the contour blocks mirror as points, the finishing allowance along Z (`W` of the second block of `G71` and `G72`) and the relief along Z of `G73` (`W` of its first block) change sign, and the depth of cut and the retract stay — the manual's four contour patterns give the signs of the allowances by the direction of the contour, which the mirror reverses in Z. The Okuma LAP (`G85`–`G88` with the shape codes) is refused the same way; its allowances are unsigned (the direction comes from the shape), so a later version would mirror the shape only. Sources: FANUC 30i lathe operator's manual (stock removal in turning, its patterns); Okuma OSP-P200L (LAP).
   - **The lathe Z mirror and Sinumerik `DIAMON`, `DIAMOF`, `DIAM90`: not refused.** The Z mirror leaves `X` and `I` as written, so how `X` is read does not matter to it. This differs from the proposal ("refused"). The Sinumerik turning cycles (`CYCLE92`–`CYCLE99`, `CYCLE930`, `CYCLE940`–`CYCLE952`) are refused (`lathe-cycle`): their parameters mix positions along Z with the side and direction of the machining. Source: SINUMERIK 840D sl programming manual (diameter programming); SINUMERIK cycles manual (turning cycles).
   - **The lathe Z mirror and the other cycles:** a single turning or facing pass (system A `G90`, `G94`; B `G77`, `G79`) without a taper is points and is mirrored; with its taper `R` it is refused (`single-pass-taper`), because the taper of `G90` is a difference in X (it would stay) and that of `G94` a difference in Z (it would change sign), which the data does not tell apart (FANUC 30i lathe manual, canned cycles). Every other cycle (`G70`, `G74`, `G75`, `G83`–`G89`, Okuma `G73`, `G74`, `G181`–`G189`, the Sinumerik drilling cycles) is refused (`lathe-cycle`). A C-axis word under the Z mirror is refused (`rotary-word`): seen from the counter spindle the C axis turns the other way.
   - **The tool-nose warning (`tool-nose`):** "Mirrored for the counter spindle: the tool nose direction (the cutting edge position in each tool's offset) is not in the program. Set it for the counter spindle before this program runs." Sources: FANUC 30i lathe manual (the direction of the imaginary tool nose is part of the offset); Okuma OSP-P200L (nose radius compensation through the T word).

**Prelude P4a:** this plan's P4a sections, the stubs and contracts above, the data flags with their tests, the roadmap's P4a rows (the scripts carry their own texts, so no i18n file changes). **Model:** Opus, high.

**Intentional behaviour changes when P4a lands:** the Tools tab gains three built-in scripts (a scenario that counts the bundled scripts or lists the Built-in Scripts menu changes; H4a greps `tests/runtime/scenarios` for the menu's count first); `extents.py` imports `_nc_path.py` with no change of output.

**Wave A**

#### P4.1 The path tracker (`_nc_path.py`)

- **Model:** Opus, high.
- **Owns:** `src-tauri/resources/scripts/_nc_path.py` (the prelude's stub: its data types and helpers are final), `src-tauri/resources/scripts/extents.py` (only the code that moves out and its imports), `tests/python/test_nc_path.py` (the prelude's pending cases: un-skip them and add).
- **Depends on:** the prelude.
- **Deliver:** AD-48: the tracker and the arc and polar geometry moved out of `extents.py`, behind the API of §6.1, with the block's start and end position, the arc's centre in absolute coordinates whatever the convention, and the reason when a position is not known; the arc direction read from the flags of §6.2 instead of `ARC_CODES`/`DIRECTION_WORD`; `extents.py` uses it, and its copies of the number helpers go; the `AR=` reading of §7 #294.
- **Tests:** every extents golden and the owner-public extents run byte for byte unchanged; the tracker on hand-written blocks for every arc form F111 lists (centre incremental and absolute, `R` above and below 180°, `CR=`, Klartext `CC` with `C`, `CR`, `LP`, `CP`, `AC()`/`IC()`), each unknown-position reason.
- **Covers:** the groundwork of X19–X24.

#### P4.2 The transform rules (`_nc_geom.py`)

- **Model:** Opus, high.
- **Owns:** `src-tauri/resources/scripts/_nc_geom.py` (the prelude's stub: its vocabulary is final), `tests/python/test_nc_geom.py` (the prelude's pending cases: un-skip `ExactnessTest`, `TransformTest`, `SwapTest`, `ArcCheckTest` and add; `RolesTest`, `ReasonsTest` and `WriteBackTest` need the real tracker and are un-skipped by the Wave A integration).
- **Depends on:** the prelude (the tracker's API as a stub; the tests build positions by hand).
- **Deliver:** AD-41–AD-44: the role of every word of a block in every built-in dialect (from the profile, the database and the modal state, never from a code list), the three operations on each role (translate, rotate, mirror; and the lathe Z mirror of AD-43) with exact decimal arithmetic where AD-44 says so and rounding elsewhere, the arc check after rounding, the swaps of AD-42, the write-back in the word's own form through `_nc_machine.py` (a point stays a point, a point-less word stays whole increments, the sign and leading zeros kept, Klartext's `+`), the lathe's diameter reading, and every refusal reason of X22 as a stable id with a plain text.
- **Tests:** each role in each dialect; each operation on each role; exactness (translate by `0.1` then `-0.1`; mirror about `X12.5`; rotate by 90°); rounding to IS-B increments and to decimals; the arc check refusing a rounded arc; every refusal reason.
- **Covers:** X19–X22 (the rules).

**Integration of Wave A (`p4a/intA`):** merge P4.1 and P4.2; `_nc_geom.py` runs on the real tracker: un-skip the three tracker-bound classes of `test_nc_geom.py` and make them pass (a fix to `_nc_geom.py` goes back through P4.2's model); the extents goldens again.

*As built (2026-10-13):* P4.1 (`7a25faa`) and P4.2 (`3c2d6ec`) merged on `p4a/intA` without a conflict; the three tracker-bound classes of `test_nc_geom.py` pass on the real tracker with no change to `_nc_geom.py`. P4.1's guard: the extents output byte for byte the same on 2,814 runs (every extents golden, every NC fixture under every effective reading of its profile, every script golden input, and the owner's local programs, counted only); the `AR=` change of #294 occurs in none of them and is held by its regression test.

**Wave B**

#### P4.3 Translate

- **Model:** Opus, high.

- **Model:** Opus, high.

Rotate is refused on a lathe profile; mirror on a lathe offers only the **Z mirror for the counter spindle** (the Z to mirror about, default Z0, in the ZX plane), with the swaps of AD-42 and the tool-nose warning, and refuses a lathe X mirror; on a mill both are refused outside the XY plane (Q4-2, Q4-3). The climb-milling warning of AD-42 on a mill.

- **Model:** Sonnet, high.

**Integration I4a:** merge Wave B; the full unit suite, Python and the extents goldens; the runtime suite on the runner.

*As built, Wave B and its integration (2026-10-15):* P4.3 (`36db95c`), P4.5 (`4f458fc`) and P4.4 (`7912743`) merged on `p4a/intB` without a conflict (`bf22095`). The baseline there: the three script test files, `test_nc_geom`, `test_nc_path` and `test_extents` passed with the eight expected failures the work packages left (`ModuleGapTest` in `test_translate.py`, `KnownGapTest` in `test_mirror.py`); the oracle failed three goldens on mirror twice, two of them because a word keeps the form it is written in (the oracle's assumption, fixed, §7 #310) and one for the Klartext `+` (§7 #308). The integration's fix batch closed every gap the work packages named, each with a regression test that replaces its expected failure: the resets (§7 #299), the rotary rule (#300), `PATTERN DEF` and `CYCL CALL PAT` (#301), `I=AC()` under `DIAMON` (#302), `axis-unwritten` (#303), the codeless `D` (#304), the roles of the Fanuc lathe's `G70`–`G76` and of `CYCLE95` (#305), whole turns of `PA` (#306), the class of `CIP I1=` (#307), the Klartext `+` (#308) and `selection-mode` (#309). The modal change of #299 is in both languages; the TS/Python modal parity holds on every modal golden, the new `heidenhain-klartext/frame-resets` included. Goldens changed on purpose: `translate/fanuc-mill-frames-settings` (`G52 X0. Y0.` is a reset), `translate/klartext-refused` (the `CYCL DEF 10.0` line names the cycle and opens no frame), `translate/lathe-a-refused` (`G71`/`G70` move), `translate/sinumerik-turn-refused` (`CYCLE95` is `cycle-position`), `rotate/sinumerik-mill-refused` (bare `ROT` refuses nothing), `rotate/` and `mirror/fanuc-mill-refused` and `mirror/fanuc-lathe-cycles-refused` (the rotary rule; their inputs bring the axis back to 0 so every reason they held is still held), `mirror/okuma-lathe-refused` (`C0.` refuses nothing, the move after `C90.` does), `mirror/fanuc-mill-selection-under-comp` (`selection-mode`), `address_arithmetic/lathe-z-shift` (the `W` of `G71` is a value of the cycle, no longer an unreviewed cycle). Over the first 1,500 lines of each of the 20 owner-public programs the transforms wrote: translate 2 → 5, rotate 1 → 2 (7 turning programs n/a), mirror 1 → 3; the blocks refused for `frame` fell from 854 to 853 (translate), 730 to 712 (rotate), 2,342 to 2,306 (mirror), for `coordinate-shift` from 168 to 93, 303 to 186, 606 to 372, `cycle-not-reviewed` (translate) from 31 to 28; `rotary-word` rose (every move while an axis stands turned is now listed). What is left for the NC review: the Klartext `BLK FORM` (its tool axis letter without a value is `word-unknown` under rotate and mirror), Klartext cycles 11 and 26 (their reset is a factor of 1, which no data says yet), `CYCLE95` with a contour that is a label range in the same program (refused like a subprogram), and the Fanuc lathe drilling cycles `G83`–`G89` (no roles, by the P10 decision).

*As built, the reviews and the fix batches (2026-10-16):* the NC review found 19 findings and the code review 13; the skeptic confirmed 27 and 5 in part and rejected none (15 critical: every one of them a run that wrote a wrong program without refusing). The fixes landed as five batches on `p4a/intC` (`226bfca` with the integrations): B1 the transform rules and the data (NC-2, NC-4 to NC-9, NC-11, NC-12, NC-15, NC-17 to NC-19, CODE-11, CODE-13; §7 #311–#323), B2 the scripts (NC-1, CODE-2, CODE-4, CODE-5, CODE-8; #330–#335), B3 translate (NC-3, NC-10, CODE-3; #340–#342), B4 address arithmetic (NC-16; #345, #346) and B5 the harness and the budget (CODE-1, CODE-7; #350–#353), each with regression tests that fail without the fix. The owner's four answers (§9, "Review answers") decided NC-13, NC-14, CODE-1 and NC-12. Open: the performance batch B6 (CODE-1's optimisation), the tests batch B7 (CODE-10, CODE-12, CODE-13), the documents B8, then the runtime suite on the runner and the landing. CODE-6 (the shared engine copy of rotate and mirror, a third engine in translate) is debt, listed in `TODO.md`.

**Reviews:** NC (the transform rules, the refusals, the data flags, every golden; `NC-…`) and code (the scripts' structure, the module boundaries, the selection priming, the performance; `CODE-…`) in parallel; the skeptic; the fix batches; then the fuzz review of §4 rule 7.

**Gates:** G0–G11 (G6 on the runner); G7: `p4-perf`; G10: the flags and the goldens; G11: the transforms over the owner's local programs (counts).

**Commit:** `P4a: Move the path — translate, rotate, mirror on a shared path tracker`

### P4b: Drilling cycles and time

**Goal:** a user replaces the drilling cycles of a program with the moves they make, reads how long each tool runs and how far it travels, and gives a machine its rapid rates, tool-change time and limits once, so the time estimate and the program checks can use them.

**Status:** built, reviewed and fixed on `v12/wave` (2026-10-22): the prelude, the work packages, the NC and code reviews with the skeptic, the fix batches and the owner's answers (§7 #518–537); waiting for the full local run and one GitHub run, then the landing. Fix batch 7 (a Fanuc `K0` block without a position kept as written) is in.

**Scope added (the owner, 2026-10-17, v1.2.0 triage A9):** the real conversion of a Sinumerik `G931` travel time in the statistics (P4.9) and the checks (P4.10) (T085), and a depth role "relative to the surface" for Klartext `Q201` and Sinumerik `DPR`, which the extents equality of X23 needs for Klartext 200/201/203 and `CYCLE81`–`83` (T108; P4.6, P4.7). The prelude branches from `main` after the NC fix round (A1) and starts with `_nc_run.py` (T014).

**What the prelude decides:**

1. `CodeEntry.expand` and `CodeParam.expandRole` (AD-45): types, the loader, the flags contract test, the Python reader; the step vocabulary and its rules (which word of a following block runs the cycle again, the motion mode after the cycle, `G91` forms, the repeat count) written as a contract the data and the script share.
2. `MachineConfig.params.motion` and `.limits` (AD-46): types, validation with JSON paths and bounds, the Rust side's shape (additive, version 1), the context members, the defaults "not set".
3. The golden formats `tests/fixtures/scripts/expand_cycles/<case>/` and `tests/fixtures/scripts/statistics/<case>/` (the expected table with values to 0.01 mm and 0.1 s).
4. The answers of Q4-8 to Q4-11 the scripts need (decided 2026-10-12): the plain drill/peck/bore list; the `G73` retract and `G83` clearance asked in the form on each run, prefilled from the motion block (the form field required for a program that contains them, and the statistics form asks the same fields, AD-45).

**Prelude P4b:** the above with stubs and pending tests, the roadmap's P4b rows. **Model:** Opus, high.

**Intentional behaviour changes when P4b lands:** two new built-in scripts; the machine dialog gains a page; the program checks gain the limit checks (silent without a `limits` block, so every existing golden stays).

**Wave A**
