### P4c: Editor comfort

**Goal:** typing a block needs fewer keystrokes, bookmarks have names and a list, a range of blocks is one command away, a file can be inserted or appended, and a program can be printed.

**Status:** built, reviewed and fixed on `v12/wave` (2026-10-22): the prelude, the work packages, the NC and code reviews with the skeptic, the fix batches and the owner's answers (§7 #538–544); waiting for the full local run and one GitHub run, then the landing.

**Profile members (the owner, 2026-10-17, v1.2.0 triage A13):** `editing.autoSpace` and `editing.autoNumber` are read with "off" as the loader default, and **P4c writes no built-in profile file**: P4d is the sole writer of `src/lib/data/profiles/*.json` (P4.19), and Q4-12 already says off in every built-in profile. Where the prelude below says the members are "off in every built-in profile", that is the loader default; a profile that wants them on sets them itself.

**What the prelude decides:** the profile members `editing.autoSpace` and `editing.autoNumber` (both off in every built-in profile, Q4-12); the bookmark record with a name (`state.json`, per file: `{ line, name? }`, read as untrusted, the old `number[]` read as before); the command ids, keys (none by default except what Q4-12 settles) and test ids; the print view's contract (a print-only HTML built from the tokens and the theme's role colours). **Model:** Sonnet, high, with the typing rules read by the NC architect.

**Wave A**

- **P4.12 Auto-space and automatic block number** (Opus, high; owns `src/lib/core/nc/autoSpace.ts`, `src/lib/core/nc/autoNumber.ts` and their tests, `src/lib/contrib/typing.ts` (the two handlers), the `.slow.test.ts` of the keystroke budget). The rules of X26 from the tokenizer of the profile: no space inside a comment, string, variable expression, `NAME=` word or multi-letter keyword; the next number from the block above and the step, a number between two numbered lines, none when there is no gap, Klartext renumbering the blocks below in the same edit, a line that continues a block (`syntax.continuationStart`) never numbered.
- **P4.13 Bookmarks** (Sonnet, high; owns `src/lib/core/nav/bookmarks.ts`, `src/lib/contrib/bookmarks.ts`, `src/lib/monaco/bookmarks.ts`, `src/lib/components/panels/BookmarksPanel.svelte` (new), `src/lib/stores/fileMemory.ts` (the record), their tests, `src/lib/i18n/en/bookmarks.ts`). Names, the panel (left region, beside the Program Map), "Bookmark All Matches" from the find widget's current search, persistence, a ribbon entry on the Edit tab.
- **P4.14 Block range, insert and append file** (Sonnet, high; owns `src/lib/contrib/ranges.ts`, `src/lib/contrib/insertFile.ts` (new) and their tests, `src/lib/i18n/en/ranges.ts`). The block range dialog (line or block numbers, select or delete, refuse a missing or duplicate number); insert and append through the file-read path (encoding, line endings), one undo step, the warning after a program end from the profile's `programEnd`, Klartext renumbering and a refused second `BEGIN PGM`.

**Wave B**

- **P4.15 Printing** (Sonnet, high; owns `src/lib/app/print.ts` (new), `src/lib/components/print/**` (new), the Rust print call if the webview needs one, their tests). The print view (colours or black, line numbers, header with name, date and page), the document or the selection, the webview's print dialog; a spike in the prelude settles whether the macOS, Windows and Linux webviews print from Tauri; where one does not, "Export as HTML" (Q4-22).

### P4e: Side by side

**Goal:** a user sees two places of one program at once, edits the channels of a multi-channel program side by side lined up at their wait codes, sees in a comparison which words changed, and compares and merges two programs in an aligned side-by-side view.

**Status:** built, reviewed and fixed (2026-10-17); lands next. The prelude (contracts §6.7 and §6.11, stubs, pins, §7 #380–#392) was done on `p4e/base`, then Wave A (P4.21 editor groups and split view, P4.22a channel mapping, P4.24 word marks), Wave B (P4.22b channel panes, P4.23 merge back, P4.25 aligned diff with merge), P4.D (the user guide) and H4e (the scenarios `p4-split`, `p4-channels`, `p4-compare`, suite `p4e`, never run locally: GitHub only). The reviews found 14 NC and 13 code findings; the skeptic confirmed 18, held 8 partly and rejected 1 (NC-6, by design); the fix batches FA, FB1, FB2, FB3, FC, FE, FT and the follow-up FA2 fixed them (§7 #451–#472); the first runtime-suite run on GitHub found four more bugs and stale scenario numbers, fixed by GA, GB and GC (§7 #473–#479), with the owner's answers of 2026-10-17 (§9 "P4e review answers"). Left for later: the P4e backlog in TODO.md (CODE-4, CODE-5, NC-6, NC-8, the rest of CODE-11, a no-op redo after a refused edit once the view is closed, the jump-line re-read on 300,000-line programs, the runtime scenario as the only cover of the typing wiring in `AlignedDiff.svelte`).

**Prelude** (Opus, high — an exception to the rule of §4 rule 3 that the owner can veto: the editor-group contract is the base every editor feature then depends on, and the traps of F118 are where a second instance has gone wrong before; the owner agreed on 2026-10-12, Q4-25): AD-50's `EditorService` groups, the per-editor decoration registration, the commands (`view.splitEditor`, `view.splitEditorDown`, `view.closeSplit`, `view.focusNextPane`, `view.focusPreviousPane`, `channels.sideBySide`, `channels.mergeBack`), the test ids, the mapping contract of AD-51.

**Decided by the prelude** (2026-10-17; each with its reason; the owner's answers of the same day are in §9 and marked "owner" here):

1. **Undo with panes of one document is the document's history.** A pane of a program with channel sections has no history of its own that the user can reach: its edits are applied to the document as mapped edits that join the document's open undo element, and every boundary Monaco sets on the pane model (its `pushStackElement`, wrapped while the view is open) is set on the document too; the focus moving to another pane is one more boundary. Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Shift+Z (Ctrl+Y on Windows and Linux) in a pane and Edit ▸ Undo/Redo run the document's undo; the pane where the change is takes the focus and shows it. *Why:* one program, one history (AD-51, X29); mirroring Monaco's own boundaries gives the same steps as typing in the program itself, without guessing where a word ends.
2. **One undo history with one file per channel (owner).** While the view is open a `ViewUndoStack` (§6.7 rule 15) orders the documents' own histories: an entry is a run of edits in one document between two boundaries, undo takes the newest entry back wherever it was made and focuses its pane. When the view closes the order is dropped and every document keeps its whole Monaco history; Save stays per file. *Why:* the owner asked for one history; storing only an order over the documents' own undo stacks keeps every document's dirty state, save and later undo exactly as they are without the view, and needs no text copies. *Limit, said in the user guide:* the order covers only edits made while the view is open (its history starts when the view opens, §7 #461). A file reloaded from disk is **one** entry of the view's history (`replaceAll` on the same model; Undo takes the reload back); only a document whose model was replaced drops its entries (§7 #469).
3. **Scrolling: always together (owner), no leading pane.** All panes share one vertical scroll position; the pane the wheel or the keyboard scrolled sets it for the others (horizontal scrolling stays per pane). *Why:* the filler zones make every aligned wait the same height in every pane and the trailing zones make the panes equally tall, so one scroll position is right for all of them; there is nothing to choose.
4. **The inspector, the program map and the status bar follow the active channel (owner).** The active pane is the one with the keyboard focus; `EditorService.cursor()` answers its cursor in **document** lines (the coordinate rule, §6.7 rule 3), so the inspector, the program map, the Channel and the cursor items of the status bar need no change. The active pane has a 2 px border in `--accent` (both themes; P4.21 holds it at ≥ 3:1 against the editor background, as the motion colours are held), the others a transparent one of the same width, so nothing moves when the focus does.
5. **Every pane has its own minimap and overview ruler (owner).** The minimap follows the setting "Show minimap" like the main editor; the overview ruler always shows the pane's wait marks (matched and unmatched), its bookmarks and its find results.
6. **Wait codes pair by the owner's rule (owner, 2026-10-17).** The same code with the same `P` set meets in exactly the channels the `P` names, the n-th occurrence in one with the n-th in each other (`waitKey`, `alignPanes`, §6.7 rule 12). A channel the `P` does not name gets nothing for it; a shown channel the `P` names that has no n-th occurrence (or has the code with another `P`) makes every shown mark of the occurrence unmatched; a named channel that is not shown is not judged. Two waits written in a different order in two channels: with two panes the longest run of occurrences that is in the same order in both is kept (a longest increasing subsequence, as Check Wait Codes' `out-of-order`) and the others are unmatched; with three or four panes the occurrence with the most crossings is dropped first, ties by pane and then line, capped (amended by review NC-5, §7 #457; the prelude's "the crossing occurrence of the lowest pane is dropped" gave poor results). The key above is the rule for every wait written with a `P` word, also for a rule built on the machine page; a wait whose partners are a list (`kind: 'line'`, the Sinumerik `WAITM`) pairs channel by channel instead, and a `SETM` answers it (review NC-4, §7 #456). *Why:* it is how the control reads the `P` word (paths as digits, the presets of the Fanuc lathe already say so); it generalises Go to the Matching Mark from two channels to N, and P4.23 moves `findPartner` onto the same key so both agree. Check Wait Codes keeps its own pairwise rule: for a rule with `samePartners` (the Fanuc presets) it reports the same situations as `partners-differ`, `missing` and `count-mismatch`.
7. **A pane's lines.** The header (the lines before the first section) is at the top of every pane, as Split into Channel Documents copies it; an edit in it shows in every pane. Lines after the header that belong to no channel are in no pane: the bar above the panes says how many, and Close Split shows them again. A document line no pane shows cannot be revealed in the view: the status bar says so and nothing moves. *Why:* the same projection as the split, so the merge and the view share one mapping; the header holds the program number every pane needs; inter-section lines are rare and editing them belongs to the program, not to a channel.
8. **More than four channels.** The view shows the first four channels in declared order and names the others in its bar; the Channel status item still reaches them. *Why:* AD-50 caps the panes at four; the owner's machines have at most three channels, so this is a rule for other machines, and a quick pick of four out of N would ask every time.
9. **Opening with one file per channel.** The channel documents that are open become the panes; when the sibling lookup (`channels.siblings`) finds a channel file on disk that is not open, the command opens the file dialog in the document's folder with multi-select, as "Open channel 2…" does, and goes on with what is open afterwards. Fewer than two open channel documents: refused in plain words. *Why:* standing rule 14 (P2 §4): the webview reads no file the user did not pick or drop, and the sibling lookup answers existence only.
10. **A selection across a junction.** In a channel pane, a selection whose lines are not one run of document lines (it spans the end of one section and the start of the next, over another channel's lines) is reported with `crossesJunction`, and the commands that act on the selected lines (transforms, the clean-up commands, scripts with a selection scope) refuse with "The selection runs over the lines of another channel. Select within one section." *Why:* acting on the document lines in between would change another channel the user cannot see in that pane; clamping would act on less than what is selected.
11. **Hover, folding and the outline in a pane.** The hover of a pane reads its modal context through the pane's mapping (P4.22b); folding and the breadcrumbs answer nothing in a pane in the first version (their providers find no document for a pane model). *Why:* a fold or a symbol computed on the program's line numbers would be at the wrong lines in a pane; mapping them is work no exit criterion asks for.
12. **Split view and the channel view exclude each other.** Split Editor is not offered while the channel view is open; Close Split closes either; a tab of another document in the channel view closes the view and shows that document. *Why:* four groups of two kinds at once would need a layout editor, which gEdit leaves out on purpose (no editor management beyond what the features need).
13. **Keys:** `F6` / `Shift+F6` move to the next and previous pane (free in gEdit and in Monaco 0.55.1; the F-key row is where gEdit's navigation keys are); no key for the split and channel commands (the usual split key needs AltGr on the owner's Swiss German keyboard; the `Mod+Alt` letters left are on the AltGr check). P4g's user keys can bind them.
14. **The typing budget (owner).** Split view (two groups on one 300,000-line document) and the channel view with two panes on a 300,000-line two-channel program keep the P1 budget, proved by `editorGroups.typing.slow.test.ts` (P4.21) and `channelPanes.typing.slow.test.ts` (P4.22b) on the pattern of `p3Typing.budget.slow.test.ts` (the code's work per keystroke: listeners, mapping, re-projection, alignment, motion colours per group), and by `p4-perf` (H4e) on the hosted runner for the WebKit part (each editor's view model, P2 F56). Three and four panes on 300,000 lines have no budget and no proof: such programs come from 3D and 5-axis milling, not from multi-channel lathes. The single-editor budget (`p3Typing.budget.slow.test.ts`) must not move.
15. **The merge's record and refusals** (§6.7 rule 16): kept in memory for the session; a new split of the same program replaces it; a merge is refused when the program changed in the lines of a channel it merges (or in the header), when a channel document's header was changed, and when a merged edit would move lines between channels (a section or wait mark typed into the wrong channel). The merge is one undo step of the program and asks no confirmation; the status bar says what was merged and how to undo it. *Why:* each refusal is a case where writing back would silently lose or move someone's lines; one undo step makes the rest safe to do without asking.

**Wave A**

- **P4.21 Editor groups and split view** (Sonnet, high; owns `src/lib/monaco/editorService.ts`, `src/lib/components/editor/EditorHost.svelte`, `src/lib/components/editor/EditorGroup.svelte` (new), `src/lib/contrib/splitView.ts` (new) with its README row, `src/lib/i18n/en/splitView.ts`, the decoration owners' registration in `src/lib/monaco/{bookmarks,motionColors}.ts`, the callers of `editorInstance()` that address lines (`src/lib/contrib/segments.ts` → `selectLines`, the two helpers of `src/lib/app/session.ts` → `topLine`/`scrollToTop`, `ensureHook` of `src/lib/contrib/typing.ts` → the focused group), the `F6` rows of `tests/runtime/lib/shortcuts.js`, their tests, `editorGroups.test.ts` and `editorGroups.typing.slow.test.ts`).

## 6. Shared contracts (binding once the preludes write them)

This section is written by each milestone's prelude; until then the architecture decisions of §3 are the contract. The headings are reserved so links stay stable. §6.1 and §6.2 are written (the P4a prelude, 2026-10-12); §6.7 and the P4e rows of §6.11 (the P4e prelude, 2026-10-17).

### 6.1 The path tracker and the transform rules (`_nc_path.py`, `_nc_geom.py`; P4a)

Written by the P4a prelude; binding for P4.1–P4.5. The stubs carry the same text in their docstrings; where the two differ, this section wins and the stub is fixed.

**Where they sit.** Both are helper modules **above** `gedit_nc`: they import it and it never imports them, so the `_nc_*` internals `gedit_nc` re-exports stay as they are. A bundled script imports them by name (`import _nc_path`, `import _nc_geom`); the scripts README says they are internal (§7 #292). Standard library only, Python 3.9.

**The tracker (`_nc_path.py`, P4.1):**

```python
CENTRE_AXIS, RADIUS_WORDS, POLAR_RADIUS, POLAR_ANGLE, UNRESOLVED_ARCS, TURNS_WORD,
ANGLE_WORD = "AR", VALUE_FUNCTIONS, PLANE_AXES, PLANE_NORMAL, CHORD_SLACK, INCH, COMPUTED
UNKNOWN_KINDS = ("machine-dependent", "expression", "distance-unknown", "diameter-unknown",
  "incremental-start", "called-program", "frame", "unknown-code", "multi-pass", "cycle",
  "cycle-position", "arc", "polar", "data")                   # = extents.KINDS minus rotary, selection
STEP_KINDS = ("none", "move", "machine", "multi-pass", "data", "pole", "frame", "unknown-code")
class Unknown(NamedTuple): kind; axes: Tuple[str, ...]; line; detail = ""; key = ""; extra = None
class Reach(NamedTuple): axis; value: Decimal; line          # a point the path reaches (column value)
class WordRead(NamedTuple): axis; token; address; incremental: Optional[bool]; function;
  value: Optional[Decimal]; reading: Optional[str]; unknown: Optional[str]
class Arc(NamedTuple): line; plane: (a, b); direction: 'cw' | 'ccw'; form: 'centre' | 'radius' | 'pole' | 'polar';
  centre; start; end                                          # absolute coordinates of the plane
  full: bool; sweep: Optional[float]; helix: Dict[axis, (start, end)]
class Polar(NamedTuple): line; plane; pole; radius; angle; arc: bool
class Step: line, kind, tokens, masked, entries, unknown_codes, calls, moves, words, reads,
  before, after, pole, arc, polar, cycle, units, events: List[Unknown | Reach]
class PathAxes: twins, order, known, angular, diameter_words, lathe_column, centre_words,
  centre_incremental, direction_words; axis_of(), label(), geo(), col()   # = extents.Axes + direction_words
class PathTracker:
  def __init__(self, context, cp)                             # profile, cp, codes, interp, machine, axes,
                                                              # pos, pole, last_polar, report_units,
                                                              # units_switched, called, unstated
  def line(self, text, number, state, record) -> (state, Step)
  def value(self, token, units, entries, number_class=None) -> (Optional[Decimal], readings)
  def geo_pos(self, axis) -> Optional[Decimal]
  def forget_all(self) -> None
literal_of, dec, sqrt, rounded, distance, angle_of, polar_point, within, centre_from_radius   # final
def arc_extremes(arc) -> List[(axis, Decimal)]                # P4.1
def arc_radii(arc) -> (Decimal, Decimal)                      # P4.1
```

1. **The line between the tracker and `extents.py`.** The tracker is everything of `Extents.line`/`Extents.block` that decides where the tool is: the interpreter update, a program start after the first (`called`, `unstated`), the written codes (`Extents.written`), the moves, the machine positions, the multi-pass cycles, the data blocks and the pole, a frame in force, unknown codes, the positions of a called cycle, `resolve_moves`, the polar move, the arc, what a cycle in force leaves unknown, and `report_units`/`units_switched` (which `value()` needs). `extents.py` keeps its ranges, scopes, work-offset and shift groups, the held rotary positions (`track_rotary`, `settle_held`; it reads `tracker.interp`), the diameter switches, the tool words, the machine rows (from `Step.moves` and `tracker.value`), the findings and every text.
2. **Events in the old order.** `Step.events` holds the `Unknown`s and `Reach`es in the order `Extents.block` raised and noted them: the resolve misses, the polar move (its miss or its extremes), the arc (its miss or its extremes), the block's end points, what the cycle in force leaves unknown; a data block's information row is an `Unknown` of kind `'data'` with no axes, raised only when the block moves something. `extents.py` replays them through `miss_axes` and `note` after its own work for the line (offsets, shifts, rotary state, tool words), which is the order it has today. That order is the guard of AD-48: every extents golden and every owner-public extents run stays byte for byte.
3. **Units and columns.** Positions are column values (a lathe's `X` a diameter where `PathAxes.lathe_column`), in `report_units`, fixed by the first **recorded** line that states `mm` or `inch`; a priming line (`record=False`) moves the path and fixes nothing. Arcs and polar moves are in coordinates (`PathAxes.geo`).
4. **The arc direction is data** (§6.2): `gedit_nc.arc_direction_of` of the motion code in force, or `PathAxes.direction_words` (`gedit_nc.arc_direction_words_of`) for a word written in the block, the address followed by its sign alone (`DR-1` in a tool call is no direction word). `ARC_CODES` and `DIRECTION_WORD` retire; the flags say what they said on the six databases (`geometryFlags.test.ts` pins `G2` → `cw`, `G3` → `ccw`), so no extents result moves. A code with `path: 'arc'` and no flag cuts no arc the tracker knows (`CIP`, `CT` are `UNRESOLVED_ARCS` as today; Okuma `G102`/`G103` stay end points only).
5. **Arcs in absolute coordinates, whatever the convention:** an incremental centre (`arcCenterMode: 'incremental'`, a centre word left out is 0), an absolute one (`'absolute'`, `I=AC(…)`), a radius (`R`, `CR=`; negative over 180°; `centre_from_radius`, chord slack `CHORD_SLACK`), the pole (Klartext `C`, `CP`), and `TURN=` above 0 as a full circle. `Arc.helix` names the axes that move with an arc outside its plane. Not resolved (an `Unknown` `'arc'`): `UNRESOLVED_ARCS`, Okuma `L` arcs, an arc by its end point and `AR=` with no centre, and — changed — an arc by its centre and `AR=` with no end point, whose end is **not known** (§7 #294). An **absolute** centre along a diameter axis (`I=AC(…)` under `DIAMON`, always under `DIAM90`) is read as that axis is, a diameter; an incremental one is a radius (`centre_reading`, §7 #302).
6. **Unknown, never guessed.** A position is `None` while not known and an `Unknown` says why, one id of `UNKNOWN_KINDS`; it stays unknown until a block writes the axis absolutely. `WordRead.unknown` carries the same id for one word.

**The transform rules (`_nc_geom.py`, P4.2):**

```python
ARC_TOLERANCE = {"mm": Decimal("0.002"), "inch": Decimal("0.0001")}   # Q4-4
MIN_DECIMALS = {"mm": 3, "inch": 4}                                  # Q4-4, a word with a point
ROLES = {"point", "point-inc", "centre", "centre-inc", "radius", "arc-angle", "turns", "direction",
  "polar-radius", "polar-radius-inc", "polar-angle", "polar-angle-inc", "pole", "pole-inc",
  "cycle-position", "tool-axis", "length", "data", "code-arc", "code-comp", "untouched", "unknown"}
class Translate(NamedTuple): shift: Dict[axis, Decimal]; x_reading: Optional['diameter' | 'radius'] = None
class Rotate(NamedTuple): centre: (Decimal, Decimal); degrees: Decimal        # XY, ccw positive
class Mirror(NamedTuple): point: (Decimal, Decimal); degrees: Decimal         # the line through point at degrees from X
class LatheZMirror(NamedTuple): z0: Decimal = 0                               # Z' = 2·z0 − Z, ZX plane
OPS = {"translate", "rotate", "mirror", "lathe-z-mirror"}
class WordRole(NamedTuple): token; role; axis; number_class; entry = None
class Refusal(NamedTuple): line; reason; written; detail = ""
class Written(NamedTuple): text; rounded: bool
REASONS = {id: (ops, plain text)}; INFO = {…}; WARNINGS = {…}    # final; the stub holds the texts
def is_exact(op) -> bool
def roles_of(step, tracker) -> List[WordRole]
def refusals_of(step, roles, tracker, op, context) -> List[Refusal]
def transform(role, value, op) -> (value, exact)              # value: Decimal or {axis: Decimal}
def swap_partner(entry, codes, flag) -> Optional[str]         # flag: 'arcDirection' | 'compSide'
def swap_word(word, profile) -> Optional[str]                 # DR+ <-> DR-
def write_word(role, value, tracker, units, exact, context) -> Written | Refusal
def check_arcs(arcs, units) -> List[Refusal]
```

7. **Roles from data.** The profile, the database, the modal state and the tracker's `WordRead`s decide every role; the geometry vocabulary of `_nc_path` is the only list of names. A word nothing describes is `'unknown'`: under rotate and mirror it refuses its block (`word-unknown`: the Fanuc lathe's drawing-dimension `A`, Sinumerik `AP=`/`RP=`, whose pole `G110`–`G112` the database does not know); under translate a plain-letter one is left and listed (`word-left`) and an assignment word (`NAME=…`) refuses. An address the database declares for the codes of one group only (not of a motion or a cycle code) is that group's number where it is written without its code (Fanuc `D2`: the offset register; §7 #304).
8. **The operations.** `transform` works on geometric coordinates (a diameter word is halved by the caller and doubled by `write_word`); a translate's X shift is meant as `x_reading` says (the form follows the document's machine) and lands on each word in its own reading. Translate moves absolute points, absolute centres, the pole, cycle positions and tool-axis parameters, and leaves increments, incremental centres, radii and polar values. Rotate and mirror move points, centres, the pole and every incremental vector in XY; rotate adds the angle to `PA` and keeps `IPA`; mirror reflects `PA` (`2·degrees − PA`) and negates `IPA` (whole turns of the operation add nothing, the result stays in −360…+360; §7 #306); both keep radii (with their sign), `AR=`, `TURN=`, `CCA`, `LEN`, and the axes outside the plane. The lathe Z mirror negates `Z` about `z0`, an incremental Z, `W` and `K`, and keeps `X`, `U`, `I`, `R`, `CR=`, `AR=`. **Exact** (`is_exact`): translate, the lathe Z mirror, a rotation by a multiple of 90°, a mirror line parallel to an axis — decimal arithmetic, no rounding; a result that does not fit the word refuses (`resolution`). Otherwise: double precision, each word rounded half away from zero (its own decimals, at least `MIN_DECIMALS`; a point-less word to whole increments), every rounded word counted (`rounded`), and `check_arcs` on the **written** program (the tracker run over the result) refuses an arc whose radii differ by more than `ARC_TOLERANCE` (`arc-check`).
9. **Swaps from data** (AD-42): `swap_partner` finds the one entry of the code's group with the other value of the flag; none or two refuses (`swap-unknown`). The partner is written in the written style of the code it replaces (`G02` → `G03`, `G2` → `G3`). `swap_word` does the same with `addresses.arcDirectionWords`.
10. **Write-back** through `gedit_nc.write_back` in the word's own form: the address, a value function, a point or none, the leading-zero style, Klartext's `+` (the profile's `numberFormat.plusSign`, `'signed'` on Klartext: a signed word keeps a sign, so `Y-2.5` turned positive is `Y+12.5`; §7 #308) and decimal comma are kept; a word added by rotate (a block that wrote only `X` gets `Y`) takes the form of the other word of the plane in that block. With no machine chosen, a word whose result the presets would write differently refuses (`machine-dependent`), as in address arithmetic (D57); P4.2 may lift its helpers into `_nc_geom.py` (the TODO's duplicated-code item names the debt).
11. **The run** (the scripts, P4.3/P4.4): every block is judged before anything is written; a cycle and the blocks that run it, a definition and its calls, the pole and the moves around it are judged as one (a refusal of one member refuses all, address arithmetic's groups); `CYCL CALL POS X Y Z` (axis-named parameters, `position: 'other'`) are the cycle's position (`cycle-position` role) and move; any other `'other'` or unreviewed parameter refuses, and so does a call whose axis-named `'other'` parameters the block does not write (`CYCL CALL PAT`: its points are elsewhere) and a code that is nothing but `'other'` parameters (`PATTERN DEF`; §7 #301). **Resets** (§7 #299): a code that opens a frame refuses only where the modal state has a frame opened at its block, so its reset form (bare `ROT`, `CYCLE800()`, `PLANE SPATIAL SPA+0 SPB+0 SPC+0`, `CYCL DEF 10.1 ROT+0`, an empty `CYCL DEF 8.1`, `G69`) refuses nothing and leaves the blocks after it in no frame; a shift code with `shiftZeroIsNone` whose every axis value is zero (Klartext cycle 7, `G52`, `TRANS`, `ATRANS`) sets no shift; a value nobody describes in a shift block (`CYCL DEF 7.1 #5`) refuses. **Rotary axes** under rotate and mirror (§7 #300): a rotary word that leaves its axis at 0 refuses nothing; any other, and every move while a rotary axis stands away from 0, refuses (`rotary-word`). `axis-unwritten` concerns the axes a block moves incrementally (§7 #303). The Fanuc lathe's `G70`–`G76` and Sinumerik `CYCLE95` carry roles (§7 #305). Every refusal is a finding `{ line, severity: "error", message: "<as written>: <text>.", reason }`; information (`INFO`) and warnings (`WARNINGS`, the message being the text with a capital and a full stop) are findings too; a refused run hands the input back unchanged (Q4-1). The lathe Z mirror's cycle rules are the case list of §5 P4a item 5, all read from data (`pitchFeed`/`tapping` → `thread`; a `'start'` cycle with data axis words or a group holding a `contour` code → `roughing-cycle`; any other `sets.cycle` → `lathe-cycle`; a `path: 'cycle'` block with `R` → `single-pass-taper`).
12. **The settled defaults** Q4-1 to Q4-6 are the header of `_nc_geom.py` and of each script (P4.3, P4.4 copy it).

The golden format of the three scripts is `tests/fixtures/scripts/README.md`, "The geometry goldens" (§7 #293).

**Added by the review fixes (§7 #311–#323).** `Step` gains `unsure` (the axes whose position is not known after a jump, a loop or a frame code), `unwritten` (the axes the program has not written yet), `flow` (a list of `FlowEvent` with a kind `modal`, `reentry` or `target`) and `pole_line` (the line that set the pole); `ModalInterpreter.fingerprint()` (Python) says whether two states read the same. `_nc_geom.REASONS` gains `jump`, `cycle-angle` and `chamfer-sign`; `INFO` gains `blank`; `pole-unknown`, `position-unknown` and `axis-unwritten` apply as the rows say. Two refusals belong to the run and are in the scripts' `RUN_REASONS`, not in `REASONS`: `selection-start` (rotate and mirror) and `selection-unprimed` (all three), with `no-database` (all three) for a run without a code database.

### 6.2 Arc direction and compensation side (`CodeSets.arcDirection`, `CodeSets.compSide`; P4a)

Written by the P4a prelude; the data is on `p4a/base`, and G10 signs it off (every flagged entry carries `review: "pending"` until then).

```ts
// core/codes/types.ts, CodeSets
arcDirection?: 'cw' | 'ccw';     // only with path: 'arc'
compSide?: 'left' | 'right';     // only with radiusComp: 'on'
// core/codes/load.ts: SETS_VALUES.arcDirection = ['cw', 'ccw'], SETS_VALUES.compSide = ['left', 'right'];
//   a value without its partner member is dropped and reported (codes[i].sets.arcDirection / .compSide)
// core/codes/lookup.ts
export function arcDirectionOf(entry: CodeEntry | null | undefined): 'cw' | 'ccw' | null;
export function compSideOf(entry: CodeEntry | null | undefined): 'left' | 'right' | null;
// core/profiles/types.ts, Profile.addresses
arcDirectionWords?: Record<string, 'cw' | 'ccw'>;   // { "DR+": "ccw", "DR-": "cw" } on heidenhain-klartext
// core/profiles/validate.ts: a key is an address and a sign (/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9]*[+-]$/), a value
//   'cw' | 'ccw', at most one word per direction (addresses.arcDirectionWords.<word>)
```

```python
# gedit_nc (from _nc_modal.py); twins of the TypeScript readers, held to the same cases
arc_direction_of(entry) -> Optional[str]          # 'cw' | 'ccw', only with sets.path == 'arc'
comp_side_of(entry) -> Optional[str]              # 'left' | 'right', only with sets.radiusComp == 'on'
arc_direction_words_of(profile) -> Dict[str, str] # keys upper case; a malformed or second word left out
```

1. **What a direction means.** `cw`/`ccw` as the **control** defines the code: seen from the positive end of the axis normal to the plane toward its negative end, in the right-handed system (G17 from +Z, G18 from +Y, G19 from +X). On a lathe in `G18` the same arc looks the other way round when the turret is behind the centre line; that changes how it looks on the machine, not what the code means, and the flag records the code's meaning. A mirror is the same swap whatever the viewing side. Sources: FANUC Series 30i/300i/300is Model A operator's manual, common to lathe and machining centre (circular interpolation); Okuma OSP-P200L programming manual (circular interpolation, the ZX plane seen from the positive end of the orthogonal axis); SINUMERIK 840D sl programming manual, fundamentals (G2/G3); HEIDENHAIN TNC 640 Klartext user's manual (`DR+` counter-clockwise, `DR-` clockwise).
2. **What a side means.** `left`/`right` of the programmed path, seen along the direction of travel, for cutter and nose radius compensation alike (Fanuc and Okuma lathes keep `G41`/`G42` for nose radius compensation). Sources: the same manuals, radius compensation.
3. **The swap rule.** A mirror writes, for a flagged code, the one entry of the **same group** with the **other value** of the same flag; if there is none or more than one, the block is refused (`swap-unknown`). A block of a selection that runs under a direction or a side set above the selection is refused too (`selection-mode`, §7 #309): the code to swap is outside the selection. `geometryFlags.test.ts` holds every shipped database to "exactly one partner"; that is why the five-axis Fanuc compensation codes and the C-axis contour arcs of Okuma are not flagged (§7 #297).
4. **The data** (16 entries, all `review: "pending"`; the list is `tests/fixtures/codes/geometry-flags.json`, which also says why every unflagged arc and compensation code is unflagged):

| Database | `arcDirection` | `compSide` |
|---|---|---|
| `fanuc` | `G2` cw, `G3` ccw | `G41` left, `G42` right |
| `fanuc-lathe` | (inherited) | `G41` left, `G42` right (its own entries) |
| `fanuc-lathe-b` | (inherited) | (inherited) |
| `heidenhain` | — (`DR±` in the profile) | `RL` left, `RR` right |
| `okuma` | `G2` cw, `G3` ccw | `G41` left, `G42` right |
| `sinumerik` (turning and milling) | `G2` cw, `G3` ccw | `G41` left, `G42` right |

5. **Who reads them.** `_nc_path` (the arc direction, replacing the extents' `ARC_CODES`/`DIRECTION_WORD`), `_nc_geom` (the swaps), and nothing in the app: the modal interpreters, the motion colours and the inspector do not read either flag, so no TypeScript behaviour changes. Tests: `src/lib/core/codes/flagsContract.test.ts` (how the flags read), `src/lib/data/codes/geometryFlags.test.ts` (the data), `src/lib/core/profiles/validate.test.ts` (the profile member), `tests/python/test_code_flags.py` (the Python twins and the golden).

### 6.3 Expansion schemas (`CodeEntry.expand`, `CodeParam.expandRole`; P4b)

Written by the P4b prelude (2026-10-19), binding for P4.6, P4.7 and P4.9 (with the scope additions T085 and T108 of the triage, A9). The stubs carry the signatures (`src/lib/core/codes/types.ts`, `core/codes/expand.ts`, `_nc_cycles.py`, `expand_cycles.py`); where a stub and this section differ, this section wins and the stub is fixed. A rule changes only with a §7 row.

**The run frame (`_nc_run.py`, CODE-6, T014).** The prelude moved the run that `rotate.py` and `mirror.py` carried twice, and the helpers `translate.py` shared with them, into one helper module above `gedit_nc` (like `_nc_path`, §7 #292), with every Python test, script golden, exit2 golden and the fuzz file unchanged and every output byte for byte the same (§7 #520). `expand_cycles.py` (P4.7) and `time_estimate.py` (P4.9) start from its frame:

```python
# _nc_run.py: 1. the frame (every script that rewrites or reports a program)
INCH, QUOTE_LENGTH
as_dict(value), trim(value: Decimal) -> str, count_text(count, singular, plural=None), quote(text),
decimal_text(form_value) -> Optional[Decimal], entry_params(entry) -> List[dict],
splice(text, edits: [(start, end, new)]) -> str
class Scope(NamedTuple): first: int; selection: bool; fragment: bool; preceding: List[str]
scope_of(context) -> Scope                          # context.input; preceding only for a selection below line 1
class Setup(NamedTuple): context; lines; text; profile; cp; params; codes
prepare(script, params_class, errors=(ValueError, ArithmeticError)) -> int | Setup
                                                    # the profile, its compiled form, the form; 1 + stderr on failure
NO_MACHINE, NO_NUMBER_INPUT
number_reading(machine_params, inch) -> Optional[str]
machine_note(machine, x_reading=None, fallback=None) -> str   # the machine sentence, always last
unprimed_envelope(text, first, script, note, in_force, verb)  # selection-unprimed
# 2. the plane run of rotate and mirror
PLANE, CENTRE_WORD, PLANE_MAX_FINDINGS = 1000, REASON_LABEL, PLANE_RUN_REASONS = ("selection-start",),
REASON_PLACE, reason_order, number_param, decimals_param, finding_of(item, run_reasons), Block,
class PlaneRun: __init__(context, cp, params, run_reasons); walk; judge; check_arcs; refused; apply
plane_machine_note, plane_message, plane_findings, has_point_params, differs, natural, NOISE
plane_main(script, params_class, run_reasons) -> int  # rotate.main and mirror.main
```

Translate keeps its own walk on the frame (its selection start adds words, its findings are per line, its cap is 500). Address arithmetic does not import `_nc_run`: it stands on `gedit_nc` alone and would load the geometry modules for three helpers it keeps its own copies of (§7 #520).

**The data** (`core/codes/types.ts`; the loader rules are `core/codes/expand.ts`, called by `load.ts`):

```ts
CodeParam.expandRole?: ExpandRole               // only on an entry with expand
CodeEntry.expand?: CodeExpand                   // ExpandSchema | { by: <address of the 'variant' param>; cases: { <value>: ExpandSchema } }
CodeEntry.dwell?: DwellForm                     // on a dwell code: { seconds?, increments?, revolutions?, lines? }
CodeSets.cycleReturn?: 'initial' | 'r-plane'    // Fanuc G98 / G99
CodeEntry.orient?: true                         // the oriented stop the 'orient' step writes (Sinumerik SPOS=)
interface ExpandSchema {
  kind: 'drill' | 'peck' | 'bore';
  steps: ExpandStep[];                          // one hole, in order
  returnBy: 'code' | 'role';
  relativeDepth?: 'signed' | 'into-material';   // required with depth-relative or first-peck-relative
  leaves: { motion: 'before' | 'cycle'; feed: 'before' | 'cycle' };
  defaults?: Record<string, string | string[]>; // the 'default' parameters: address -> value, or a list of equivalent values
  feedOutZero?: 'feed';                         // a feed-out of 0 means the feed (Klartext 201 Q208=0; the owner, 2026-10-20)
}
type ExpandStep =
  | { step: 'rapid'; to: ExpandLevel } | { step: 'feed'; to: ExpandLevel; feed?: 'feed' | 'feed-out' }
  | { step: 'pecks'; mode: 'chip-break' | 'full-retract' | 'universal'; retract?: ExpandValue;
      back?: ExpandLevel; reentry?: ExpandValue; retractAt?: 'rapid' | 'feed-out' }
  | { step: 'dwell'; at: 'bottom' | 'top' } | { step: 'spindle-stop' } | { step: 'spindle-restore' }
  | { step: 'orient' } | { step: 'lift-off'; back?: boolean };
type ExpandLevel = 'start' | 'r-plane' | 'clearance' | 'depth' | 'return';
type ExpandValue = { role: ExpandRole } | { form: 'peckRetract' | 'peckClearance' };
// core/codes/expand.ts: EXPAND_ROLES, REPEATABLE_ROLES = ['position', 'default'], EXPAND_KINDS,
//   EXPAND_LEVELS, EXPAND_STEPS, PECK_MODES, PECK_FIELDS, FEED_OUT_ZERO = ['feed'];
//   readExpandRole, readExpand, readDwell, schemaFor
```

```python
# _nc_cycles.py (imports gedit_nc only); the lists are expand.ts's, held equal by test_nc_cycles.py
EXPAND_ROLES, REPEATABLE_ROLES, EXPAND_KINDS, EXPAND_LEVELS, EXPAND_STEPS, PECK_MODES, PECK_FIELDS,
FEED_OUT_ZERO, CYCLE_RETURNS, MOVE_KINDS, REASONS = {id: plain text}, INFO = {id: plain text}   # final
class PeckValues(NamedTuple): retract: Optional[Decimal]; clearance: Optional[Decimal]
class HoleMove(NamedTuple): kind; to: Dict[axis, Decimal]; feed; feed_role; seconds; dwell; step
class Hole(NamedTuple): line; code; kind; position; start: Decimal; levels; moves; end
class CycleRefusal(NamedTuple): line; reason; written; detail = ""
expand_of(entry), expand_role_of(param), schema_for(expand, variant), cycle_return_of(entry),
dwell_form_of(entry), roles_of(entry), needs_peck_values(schema) -> (bool, bool),
machine_motion(ctx), machine_limits(ctx)                                    # final (§6.4)
levels_of(schema, values, start, incremental, return_mode) -> Dict[level, Decimal]   # P4.7
peck_bottoms(feed_start, depth, values, step) -> List[Decimal]                      # P4.7
holes_of(step, tracker, pecks: PeckValues, context) -> (List[Hole], List[CycleRefusal])  # P4.7
```

1. **The roles** (`ExpandRole`; levels are positions on the tool axis, `Z` under `G17`; the in-feed goes toward the material, −Z, and "above" is away from it):

| Role | Meaning | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| `position` | a hole position in the plane, written in the cycle block | Fanuc `X`, `Y` |
| `r-plane` | absolute level where the feed starts; incremental: signed distance from the start level | Fanuc `R` |
| `surface` | absolute level of the surface or reference plane | Klartext `Q203`, Sinumerik `RFP` |
| `clearance` | unsigned distance above `surface` where the feed starts | `Q200`, `SDIS` |
| `depth` | absolute level of the bottom; incremental: signed distance from the r-plane | Fanuc `Z`, Sinumerik `DP` |
| `depth-relative` | the bottom measured from `surface`, read by `relativeDepth` (T108) | `Q201` (signed), `DPR` (into material) |
| `return` / `return-relative` | the level after the hole: absolute, or unsigned above `surface` | `RTP` / `Q204` |
| `peck`, `first-peck`, `first-peck-relative` | each peck's length; where the first peck ends (absolute, or from `surface`) | Fanuc `Q`, `Q202`; `FDEP`, `FDPR` |
| `peck-decrement`, `peck-minimum` | how much shorter each further peck is; the shortest peck | `Q212`, `_DAM`; `Q205`, `_MDEP` |
| `chip-breaks`, `chip-retract` | chip breaks before a full retract; the retract of a chip break | `Q213`; `Q256`, `_VRT` |
| `advance` | after a full retract, the distance above the last bottom the tool comes back down to at rapid | `_DIS1` |
| `dwell`, `dwell-top` | the dwell at the bottom; at the top (after a full retract, or at the start) | Fanuc `P`, `Q211`, `DTB`; `Q210`, `DTS` |
| `feed`, `feed-out` | the in-feed (absent from the entry: the feed in force); the feed on the way out. A value of 0 or less refuses (`value-missing`), except a `feed-out` of 0 where the schema's `feedOutZero` names the feed it means (`'feed'`: Klartext 201 `Q208=0` retracts at `Q206`, TNC 640 cycles manual §3.4; the owner, 2026-10-20) | Fanuc `F`, `Q206`, `FFR`; `Q208`, `RFF` |
| `repeat` | the repeat count | Fanuc `K` |
| `variant` | its value as written picks a case of `cases` | `VARI` |
| `spindle-direction`, `orient-angle`, `lift-off-first`, `-second`, `-axis` | the oriented stop and the lift-off of a boring cycle | `SDIR`, `POSS`, `RPA`, `RPO`, `RPAP` |
| `default` | understood only at its value in `defaults`, or at one of its values where `defaults` gives a list of values the manuals prove equivalent (the owner, 2026-10-20: `FRF` 1 and 0, `_AXN` 0 and 3), compared as numbers (`Q211=0.0`, `Q395=+0`); or empty, or left out (a Sinumerik argument left out is 0, so 0 is always one of the values) | `_GMODE`, `_AMODE`, `Q395` |

A parameter of an entry with `expand` that has **no** role is not understood: a block that writes it is refused (`param-unknown`), never expanded with the value ignored.

2. **Levels.** `start`: the tool-axis position when the block that runs the cycle starts, from the tracker (`Step.before`); not known → `start-unknown`. On Fanuc the start level of the first hole is the **initial level** and stays it for the holes after it in the same cycle (the cycle returns there under `G98`); a block that starts a cycle again sets it again. `r-plane`: the `r-plane` value, under incremental distance mode `start + value`. `clearance`: `surface + clearance`. `depth`: the `depth` value (incremental: `r-plane + value`), or for `depth-relative` `surface + value` (`signed`) or `surface − value` (`into-material`; a value below 0 refuses, `depth-direction`). `return`: with `returnBy: 'code'` the `cycleReturn` of the code in force in the group of the entries that carry it (`initial` → the initial level, `r-plane` → `r-plane`), from the program or the machine's `modalInitial`; one only assumed from the profile refuses (`return-assumed`, the rule of Q4-5 for the distance mode); with `'role'` the `return` value or `surface + return-relative`. A bottom that is not below the level where the feed starts refuses (`depth-direction`). Values are read on the machine (`gedit_nc.value_of` with the parameter's number class), so a point-less word under "none" refuses as everywhere (`machine-dependent`); a variable refuses (`expression`).

3. **Steps.** A step moves along the tool axis at the hole, from where the previous one left the tool. `rapid` and `feed` go to their level; a step that would not move writes nothing; a `rapid` to `return` that is not above the tool writes nothing. `feed` uses the `feed` value, or `feed-out` when it says so (a `feed-out` of 0 under `feedOutZero: 'feed'` is the `feed` word, or the feed in force where the block leaves the `feed` out). `dwell` writes the `dwell` (bottom) or `dwell-top` (top) value and nothing when it is absent or 0. `spindle-stop` writes the database's one entry with `sets.spindle: 'off'` in the group of the spindle code in force; `spindle-restore` writes the spindle code in force before the cycle; a `spindle-direction` value has to name that code (its number: `SDIR=3` with `M3`), otherwise the block refuses (`param-not-default`). `orient` writes the database's one entry with `orient: true` (Sinumerik `SPOS=`, which P4.6 adds) with the `orient-angle` value as written; `lift-off` moves at rapid by the lift-off values, increments along the first and second axis of the plane and the tool axis (`back`: the same move the other way). **Pecks**, from the level the tool stands at (the feed start) to `depth`, measured from `surface` where the entry has one and otherwise from the feed start (Klartext feeds to depth in one pass when `Q202` equals the depth, so `Q202` counts from `Q203`; SINUMERIK repeats the stroke of the first drilling depth, which `FDPR` measures from `RFP`; FANUC cuts `Q` from the R point; settled at the Wave A integration): the first peck ends at `first-peck` (or `first-peck-relative`) when the entry has it, otherwise one `peck` below that origin; each further peck is the previous one less `peck-decrement`, but not shorter than `peck-minimum` when that is written and above 0, else not shorter than the decrement (P4.6 confirms this reading for both controls from the manuals and records a §7 row where one differs); the last peck ends at `depth`. After every peck but the last: `chip-break` goes back by `retract` (at rapid, or at `feed-out` with `retractAt`) and feeds the next peck from there; `full-retract` goes back to `back` at rapid, dwells `dwell-top`, comes down at rapid to `reentry` above the last bottom and feeds on; `universal` makes `chip-breaks` chip breaks, then one full retract, and counts again. A peck length of 0 or less, a retract or reentry below 0, or a `reentry` that is not above the last bottom refuses (`value-missing` with the detail). `{ form: 'peckRetract' }` and `{ form: 'peckClearance' }` are the run's form fields (Q4-9): empty, the cycle refuses (`peck-retract-missing`, `peck-clearance-missing`).

4. **Which blocks drill, and the move to the hole.** The blocks that run a cycle are the tracker's (`Step.cycle`, the `cycle-position` events, the modal interpreter's `activeCycle`/`definedCycle`, §6.1); the expansion adds no rule of its own, and a block whose running the tracker cannot decide refuses. A **Fanuc** cycle block, and every block after it that runs the cycle again, becomes: its own words that are not the cycle's (codes of other groups, `M`, `S`, `T`, comments, the block number) on the first written block with the plane move to the hole **at rapid**; then the steps. The cycle code, its parameter words and the cycle's own `K` leave the program; `G80` and `G98`/`G99` stay as written (a cancel with nothing active and a return mode with no cycle do nothing). A repeat count `K` gives `K` holes (under `G91` each one the plane increment further; `K0` none). **Klartext:** the definition (`CYCL DEF 200` and its `Q` lines) stays as written (a definition does nothing without a call); a call (`CYCL CALL`, `M99` on a positioning block, every positioning block under `M89`) loses the call word and the hole's steps follow its block; `CYCL CALL POS` adds its tool-axis value to `surface` and moves to its point first as the manual's positioning logic says (P4.7 writes it from the TNC 640 cycles manual, or refuses it as `call-form` with a §7 row); `CYCL CALL PAT` refuses (`call-form`). **Sinumerik:** `CYCLE81(…)` runs at the position the tool stands at; under `MCALL` every block that moves in the plane runs it after its own move, and stays as written; the call (or the `MCALL CYCLE…` line) leaves, a bare `MCALL` stays. A cycle whose start the selection cuts off refuses (`selection-inside`).

5. **The written forms come from data.** A rapid is the motion entry with `sets.motion: 'rapid'` and no `path`; where there is none (Klartext), the straight feed entry with the profile's rapid word (`addresses.rapid`, `L Z+2 FMAX`). A feed move is the entry with `sets.motion: 'feed'` and no `path`. None or more than one → `no-code`. A code is written in the style the program writes its motion codes (`G01` or `G1`, as `_nc_geom.partner_text` does), and **only where the mode in force differs** from the one the move needs, as a post-processor writes modal codes. Moves are written in the distance mode in force at the cycle block (`G91` increments from where the tool stands); a dialect whose words carry their own mode (Klartext `I` prefix) writes absolute values. Numbers go through `gedit_nc.write_back` in the form of the cycle's word they come from (its decimals, the Klartext `+`), and a value computed from two words (a level, a peck bottom) takes the decimals of the longer and at least those of `_nc_geom.MIN_DECIMALS`; it is exact decimal arithmetic, never rounded (a result that does not fit refuses, `resolution`).

6. **Feeds and dwells.** The feed of a `feed` move is the cycle's feed word copied as written (Fanuc `F150.`), `Q206` written as `F` with its value, and no `F` where the entry has no `feed` role (Sinumerik `CYCLE81`: the feed in force). A dwell is written with the database's dwell code (the entry with `dwell`): the cycle's dwell word copied as it stands where the dwell code takes the same address and number class (Fanuc `G82 P500` → `G4 P500`), otherwise the time in seconds through `seconds` or `lines` (Sinumerik `DTB=2` → `G4 F2`, a negative `DTB` through `revolutions` as `G4 S…`; Klartext `Q211=0.5` → `CYCL DEF 9.0 DWELL TIME` / `CYCL DEF 9.1 DWELL 0.5`).

7. **What the blocks after the cycle find** (`leaves`): `motion: 'before'` writes the motion code that was in force before the cycle on the next block that moves without a motion code of its own (Fanuc: the cycle is a group of its own and `G80` leaves the motion group as it was; Sinumerik: the standard cycles restore the G codes on their return); `feed: 'before'` writes the feed in force before the cycle on the next feed move without an `F` (Klartext: `Q206` is not the program's feed). That block outside the selection refuses (`restore-outside`). `'cycle'`: nothing is written back.

8. **Block numbers** (Q4-12): the first written block keeps the block number of the block it replaces, the others get none. Klartext numbers every block: the program is renumbered from the first written block to `END PGM`, which a selection that does not reach `END PGM` cannot do (`renumber-outside`).

9. **Refusals and the run.** All or nothing (Q4-1): one refused block writes nothing and lists every refused block, one error per block (its first reason in the order of `_nc_cycles.REASONS`), as the geometry scripts do; the run's own reasons are `no-database` and `selection-unprimed` (the frame's) and `lathe` (a turning program: drilling cycles are written out on milling programs only, whatever the database inherits). A block refuses for: no schema (`no-schema`), a tapping or thread code (`tapping`), a parameter without a role or not at its default (`param-unknown`, `param-not-default`), a variant value without a case (`variant-unknown`), a value the steps need that is not written (`value-missing`), a variable (`expression`), a reading the machine decides (`machine-dependent`), radius compensation on (`radius-comp`), a plane other than XY (`plane`), a frame in force (`frame`), a start level or a return mode not known (`start-unknown`, `return-assumed`), a distance mode only assumed (`distance-assumed`), a bottom not below the feed start (`depth-direction`), a missing form value (`peck-retract-missing`, `peck-clearance-missing`), a call form not written out (`call-form`), a computed value that does not fit its word (`resolution`), and the selection rules (`selection-inside`, `restore-outside`, `renumber-outside`). Information rows: `definition-kept` (Klartext, once per run), `data-kept` (Fanuc `K0` without a position, per block). The message: `Expanded N cycles (H holes) into M blocks.`, what was left, the machine sentence last (`_nc_run.machine_note`).

10. **The loader** (`core/codes/expand.ts`, the gate; the Python readers trust the loaded form): `expand` only on an entry with `sets.cycle` `'start'` or `'define'` and never on `tapping`, `pitchFeed` or `pitchFeedAmbiguous`; a role at most once per entry except `position` and `default`; every role a step or a level needs is a role of a parameter (`levels`: `r-plane` → `r-plane`; `clearance` → `surface` and `clearance`; `depth` → `depth`, or `depth-relative` with `surface`; `return` → `returnBy: 'code'`, or `return`, or `return-relative` with `surface`); some step feeds to `depth` (a `feed` to it, or `pecks`); `pecks` need `peck` (or a first-peck role), `chip-break` a `retract`, `full-retract` a `back` and a `reentry`, `universal` all three and `chip-breaks`; `relativeDepth` with a relative role; `feedOutZero` one of `FEED_OUT_ZERO` and only with the roles `feed-out` and the one it names; every `default` parameter has its value in `defaults`, a text or a list of at least one text with no value twice; `variant` only with `{ by, cases }`; no member the schema does not know. A schema that breaks a rule is **dropped whole** and reported with its JSON path, and the roles of its entry with it (a role means nothing without a schema); the entry itself stays. `dwell` names parameters of its entry, or `lines` with `{s}`; `cycleReturn` is a `SETS_VALUES` member. Tests: `src/lib/core/codes/expandContract.test.ts` (the prelude), `tests/python/test_nc_cycles.py` (the lists, the readers).

11. **The data P4.6 writes** (`review: 'pending'` on every entry it changes, the sources by manual and section in its hand-off for G10): the schemas of Q4-8 (Fanuc mill `G73`, `G81`, `G82`, `G83`, `G85`, `G86`, `G89`; Klartext 200, 201, 203; Sinumerik `CYCLE81`, `CYCLE82`, `CYCLE83` with the parameters it understands and every other one a `default`, `CYCLE85`, `CYCLE86`, `CYCLE89`) with their roles; `cycleReturn` on Fanuc `G98`/`G99`; `dwell` on Fanuc `G4`, Sinumerik `G4` and Klartext `CYCL DEF 9`; the Sinumerik `SPOS` entry with `orient` for `CYCLE86`; and the T108 roles `depth-relative` (`Q201`, `DPR`), `surface` (`Q203`, `RFP`) on Klartext 200/201/203 and `CYCLE81`–`83`. A cycle the vocabulary cannot say gets no schema and a §7 row; the vocabulary grows only through this section. `src/lib/data/codes/expand.test.ts`: every schema loads without a problem, every role names a parameter of its entry, no tapping or thread entry carries one, the list of entries with a schema is exactly Q4-8's, and the resolved lathe databases (which inherit Fanuc entries) are refused by the script, not by the data.

12. **T108 in the extents.** With `depth-relative` and `surface` on Klartext 200/201/203 and `CYCLE81`–`83`, the extents reach the bottom of such a cycle (`surface + Q201`, `RFP − DPR`) where they count it "not resolved" today; the Wave B package P4.7b owns that change in `extents.py` (the cycle positions of the tracker), the extents goldens it moves and the X23 cases of those cycles (§7 #526), so X23's equality (the extents of the expanded program equal the cycle's) holds for them.

13. **The statistics read the same holes** (AD-45, AD-47; P4.9): `time_estimate.py` calls `holes_of` with the form's `peckRetract`/`peckClearance` and times each `HoleMove` like a written move (§6.4 rule 6); a block whose holes refuse is counted "not estimated" under its reason with its lines, and the report goes on. A missing peck value is the reason `peck-retract-missing` or `peck-clearance-missing` there too.

14. **The goldens** (`tests/fixtures/scripts/README.md`, "The drilling and time goldens"): `expand_cycles/<case>/` in the format of the geometry goldens (`input.nc`, `params.json`, `case.json`, `preceding.nc`, `expected.nc` with `envelope.json`, or `refused.json`); P4.7 writes a case per schema per return mode and distance mode, the repeat count, the refusals, a `G73`/`G83` program with and without the form's values, and the extents equality of X23 for every written case (`test_expand_cycles.py`).

### 6.4 Machine motion and limits (`MachineConfig.params.motion`, `.limits`; P4b)

Written by the P4b prelude (2026-10-19), binding for P4.8, P4.9 and P4.10 (with T085). The stubs carry the signatures (`core/machines/types.ts`, `core/machines/motion.ts` with `motion.test.ts`'s `todo` rows, `core/scripting/types.ts`, `_nc_cycles.machine_motion`/`machine_limits`).

```ts
// core/machines/types.ts (MachineParams gains both, like channels: stored in MachineConfig.params only)
motion?: MachineMotion;  limits?: MachineLimits;
interface MachineMotion {
  units?: 'mm' | 'inch';                 // of every length and rate below; required once one is set
  rapid?: Record<string, number>;        // axis letter -> per minute (linear: units; rotary, addresses.angular: degrees)
  rapidKind?: 'straight' | 'per-axis';   // missing: read as 'straight' (Q4-11), and the report says so
  maxFeed?: number;                      // top cutting feed per minute
  toolChange?: number;                   // seconds
  peckRetract?: number; peckClearance?: number;   // the G73 retract and G83 clearance: the forms' prefill only (Q4-9)
}
interface MachineLimits {
  units?: 'mm' | 'inch';                 // of travel; required once travel is set
  spindle?: Record<string, number>;      // rpm; 'main' (the plain speed word) or the speed address (S2, SB)
  travel?: Record<string, { min: number; max: number }>;   // machine coordinates (rotary: degrees)
}
// core/machines/motion.ts (P4.8 writes the bodies)
MOTION_BOUNDS = { rate: 200_000, rapidRotary: 1_000_000, toolChange: 600, peck: 100, spindle: 200_000, travel: 100_000 }
type MotionBlock = { state: 'absent' } | { state: 'valid'; motion } | { state: 'invalid'; problems };   // LimitsBlock alike
validateMotion(raw, path, id, { axes, angular }): MachineProblem[]; validateLimits(…)
motionBlock(m, path, { axes, angular }): MotionBlock; limitsBlock(…): LimitsBlock
// core/scripting/types.ts: ScriptContextV2.machine.motion?, .limits?; BuildContextInput.motion?, .limits?
```

```python
# _nc_cycles.py (final): context.machine.motion / .limits, or None (no machine, none set, a broken block)
machine_motion(ctx) -> Optional[dict]; machine_limits(ctx) -> Optional[dict]
```

1. **Where they live** (AD-46). Two optional members of `MachineConfig.params`, additive: `MACHINES_VERSION` stays 1, as M12's `channels` (D59). **Never in `EffectiveMachine.params` and never in `effectiveKey`** (§7.16 #149): a rate does not change how a program is read, so changing it rebuilds no modal index and no effective profile. `src-tauri/src/machines.rs` keeps the file as opaque JSON (it checks only that the payload is an object and its size); the TypeScript validator is the gate, as for `channels`, so P4.8 needs **no Rust change** beyond a round-trip test of a record with both blocks (§7 #522).

2. **Validation** (`validateMotion`, `validateLimits`; JSON paths `machines[2].params.motion.rapid.X`): every number finite; a rate, a feed, a peck value and a speed above 0; a tool-change time 0 or more; each at most its `MOTION_BOUNDS` (in mm; an inch value at most the bound ÷ 25.4; a rotary rate at most `rapidRotary`); a travel `min` below its `max`, both within ±`travel`; `units` present once a length or rate is set; a rate or a travel names an axis of the profile (`addresses.axes`), read in degrees where the profile lists it in `addresses.angular`; a spindle key is `main` or a speed address (`/^[A-Z][A-Z0-9]{0,3}$/`); `rapidKind` one of its two values; no member the block does not know. Plain messages: "a rapid rate is a number of mm/min above 0 and at most 200,000".

3. **A broken block costs the block** (the `channelBlock` rule, X12 d): `validateMachine` never reports either block; `motionBlock`/`limitsBlock` answer `invalid` with the problems, the Machines page lists them, the scripts get no member, and the machine stays selectable with every other parameter. An empty object is valid and empty; a missing member is `absent`.

4. **The page** (P4.8, `MachineMotionForm.svelte`, a page "Motion and limits" of the machine dialog): plain labels with units (the unit choice first, mm or inch), every field optional and empty by default, a note that every value comes from the machine's documentation and that gEdit assumes none (Q4-10); the two peck values say they only prefill the expansion and statistics forms; saving an empty field removes the member, and an empty block removes the block; an invalid block from a hand edit is shown with its problems and kept until the user changes it. Export and import carry the blocks with the record (they are members of `params`). The context gets a valid block as `machine.motion` / `machine.limits` (`buildContext`, `contract` stays 2).

5. **The forms are prefilled from the machine** (Q4-9, Q4-10). A script parameter gains the header member `prefill = "machine.motion.<member>"` (one of `peckRetract`, `peckClearance`, `maxFeed`, `toolChange`, `rapidKind`, `units`, `rapid.<axis>`); `src-tauri/src/scripts/meta.rs` accepts it (`PARAM_FIELDS`, `ParamSpec.prefill`, any other value a header warning) and `src/lib/app/scripts.ts` puts the document's machine value into the field's default when the form opens; an empty machine value leaves the field empty. The value is shown and editable for the run; nothing reads the machine behind the user's back. `expand_cycles.py` and `time_estimate.py` add their `prefill` lines when P4.8 is merged (the Wave A integration).

6. **The time estimate** (AD-47, P4.9; `time_estimate.py`, `output = "new-document"`). The form: `peckRetract`, `peckClearance`, `motionUnits` (`mm`/`inch`), `rapidX`, `rapidY`, `rapidZ`, `rapidA`, `rapidB`, `rapidC` (the profile's axes; a rotary rate in degrees per minute), `rapidKind`, `maxFeed`, `toolChange`, each prefilled (rule 5), and `format` (`table`, `csv`). Per tool, per channel where the program has channels (as the tool list), and in total: feed length, rapid length, tool changes, feed time, rapid time, dwell time, tool-change time, total time, and "not estimated" with each kind's lines. Lengths are always given; a time needs its rate (a rapid time the rapid rates, a tool-change time `toolChange`), and is "not set" without it, never a number of gEdit's. A feed move takes length ÷ feed at 100 %; a feed above `maxFeed` is clamped to it; a feed per revolution is multiplied by the speed in force; under constant surface speed the speed follows the diameter along the move, held by the clamp in force or else by `limits.spindle.main`, and a move is split where the clamp starts to hold; an inverse-time block (`G93`) takes 1 ÷ F minutes; **a travel-time block (Sinumerik `G931`, `sets.feedUnit: 'travel-time'`, T085) takes F seconds as written** (the literal value, `gedit_nc.decimal_of`; Sinumerik reads numbers as written, and the number class of `numbers.ts`/`_nc_machine.py` is not changed by P4b, those files being BL-NC-readers' in Wave X), and where length ÷ time is above `maxFeed` the move takes length ÷ `maxFeed` and is counted "clamped by the top feed". A rapid: `straight` moves all axes on one line at the rapid rate, so it takes the path length ÷ the lowest rate of the axes that move (Q4-11: the longer, safer estimate); `per-axis` moves each axis at its own rate and takes the longest of each axis's distance ÷ its rate; a missing rate on a moving axis makes that rapid's time "not set"; `rapidKind` missing is read as `straight` and the report says so. A dwell counts as written (seconds, the least time increments of the machine, revolutions at the speed in force, from the dwell code's `dwell` form, §6.3 rule 6). A drilling cycle with a schema is timed through `_nc_cycles.holes_of` (§6.3 rule 13). **Not estimated**, each counted with its lines and never guessed: `multi-pass` (lathe `G71`–`G73`, `G76`), `thread`, `contour`, `pattern` (a pocket or pattern cycle), `lap` (Okuma LAP), `per-tooth` (a feed per tooth: no tooth count), `call` (a subprogram call), `rotary-rate` (a rotary move without its rate), `cycle` (a cycle without a schema, or one whose holes refuse, under its reason), `peck-retract-missing`, `peck-clearance-missing`, `position-unknown`. One plain line says the estimate is a lower bound (100 % override, no acceleration, no block time, no look-ahead). The goldens are `tests/fixtures/scripts/statistics/<case>/`: `input.nc`, `params.json`, `case.json` as above, `expected.csv` (the CSV of `format: csv`, lengths to 0.01 mm, times to 0.1 s, computed by hand in the case's `README` line) and `envelope.json`.

7. **The limits in the program checks** (P4.10, `program_checks.py`; three new checks in `CHECKS`, each a header parameter, silent without a `limits` block or without the member it needs): `speedLimit` "Spindle speed limit" — a speed in rpm above `limits.spindle.main` (the plain speed word, `gedit_nc.names_main_spindle`) or above the limit of the spindle its address names, and a clamp (`sets.speedLimit`) above the limit: **warning** (the control clamps or stops; the program runs slower than written); `travelLimit` "Travel" — an axis position in machine coordinates (`axisWords: 'machine'`, `G53`, `SUPA`, `M91`) outside `limits.travel`: **error**; a position in work coordinates outside the travel: **information** (the work offset is not known, so it may still be inside); `travelTime` "Travel time" (T085) — a block that moves under `feedUnit: 'travel-time'` without an F of its own: **warning**, if the SINUMERIK fundamentals manual says the travel time counts for its own block only (P4.10 confirms; if F stays in force under `G931`, the check is dropped and §7 says so). The rows name the limit and the value (`S12000 is above the top speed of this machine, 10000 rpm`).

8. **Who reads them.** The app: the page (P4.8) and `buildContext`; nothing else (no modal index, no inspector). The scripts: `expand_cycles.py` and `time_estimate.py` through their forms (rule 5) and `time_estimate.py` also `machine_limits` for the constant surface speed fallback; `program_checks.py` through `_nc_cycles.machine_limits` (`_nc_cycles` imports `gedit_nc` only, so the checks load no geometry module).

### 6.5 Typing help and bookmarks (P4c)

Written by the P4c prelude (2026-10-19), binding for P4.12–P4.15, P4.D and H4c. The owner's answers A13 and Q4-12, Q4-13, Q4-22 (§9) are part of it. The stubs carry the signatures; the rules below carry what the signatures cannot say. A rule changes only with a §7 row. The section covers the whole milestone, not only its title: the typing help (rules 1–3, 9), the bookmarks (4–6), the block range and the file insert (7–8) and the print view (10).

**Settings and profile members.** `editing.autoSpace` and `editing.autoNumber`, optional booleans of the profile's `editing` object, read as `=== true`: **absent is off, which is the loader default** (A13). The loader (`validate.ts`) accepts both and names a non-boolean by path (`editing.autoSpace`); `ProfileEditing` (`core/profiles/types.ts`) carries them. **P4c writes no built-in profile file** (P4d is the sole writer of `src/lib/data/profiles/*.json`, P4.19), and no default is materialized in a resolved profile, so `tests/fixtures/resolved/effective/**` and every golden stay as they are; a test holds that no built-in profile carries either member. No new setting of `settings.json`: the print form's options are remembered in `uiState.lastParams['print']`, the block range form's in `lastParams['edit.blockRange']`. The bookmark names are the one new member of `state.json` (rule 4).

**Types and stubs** (the prelude; every body is empty, answers the safe "nothing happens" or throws its `…NotImplemented` error):

```ts
// core/nc/autoSpace.ts (P4.12)
wantsSpaceBefore(site: TypingSite, ch: string, cp: CompiledProfile): boolean;          // stub: false
// core/nc/autoNumber.ts (P4.12)
interface AutoNumberInput { line; column; lineCount; read: LineReader; inPane?: boolean }
type AutoNumberRefusal = { reason: 'noGap'; between: [number, number] } | { reason: 'overflow'; max: number }
  | { reason: 'tooManyBelow'; limit: number } | { reason: 'inPane' };
type AutoNumberAnswer = { kind: 'plain' } | { kind: 'number'; text: string; number: number; shifts: NumberEdit[] }
  | ({ kind: 'none' } & AutoNumberRefusal);
decideAutoNumber(input: AutoNumberInput, cp: CompiledProfile): AutoNumberAnswer;      // stub: plain
// core/nc/blockNumbering.ts (P4.12; P4.14 uses it)
type LineReader = (line: number) => string;  interface NumberEdit { line; startColumn; endColumn; text }
formatBlockNumber(value: number, cp): string | null;
consecutiveRenumbering(read, lineCount, fromLine, firstNumber, cp, maxLines): { ok: true; edits: NumberEdit[]; last: number } | { ok: false; reason: 'tooMany' | 'overflow' };
MAX_RENUMBER_LINES = 5000
// core/nav/bookmarks.ts (P4.13): MAX_BOOKMARKS = 200, MAX_BOOKMARK_NAME = 60, MAX_BOOKMARK_TEXT = 120, cleanBookmarkName(raw: unknown): string | null
// app/types.ts (P4.13): FileMemo.bookmarkNames?: { line: number; name: string }[];  BookmarkInfo { docId; line; name?; text };
//   BookmarkService += list(id): BookmarkInfo[]; setName(id, line, name | null): string | null; remove(id, line): boolean;
//     restore(id, marks: { line; name? }[]): void; addAllMatches(): BookmarkMatchesAnswer; onDidChange(cb): Disposable
//   BookmarkMatchesAnswer = { ok: true; marked; already } | { ok: false; reason: 'noDocument' | 'noSearch' | 'noMatch' | 'tooMany'; matches? }
// core/nav/blockRange.ts (P4.14): BlockRangeRequest { kind: 'line' | 'block'; from: string; to: string };
//   resolveBlockRange(read, lineCount, cp, request): { ok: true; startLine; endLine; blocks: { from; to } | null } | { ok: false; reason: 'notNumber' | 'outsideProgram' | 'missingBlock' | 'duplicateBlock' | 'reversed'; field?; … }
// core/nc/insertFile.ts (P4.14): InsertRequest { where: 'cursor' | 'end'; line; column; selected?; lineCount; read; lines };
//   planInsert(request, cp): { ok: true; beforeLine; lines; numberEdits; renumbered; tapeMarksLeftOut; warnings: { kind: 'afterProgramEnd' | 'afterTapeEnd' | 'programFrame'; line; … }[] } | { ok: false; reason: 'empty' | 'onlyTapeMarks' | 'secondProgramStart' | 'programEndInFile' | 'continuesBlock' | 'tooManyBelow' | 'overflow'; … }
// app/print.ts (P4.15): PrintOptions { scope; colors; lineNumbers; fontPt }, PrintSource, PrintRow, PrintPage, PrintDocument,
//   pageGeometry(fontPt), buildPrintDocument(source, options, cp), printDocumentHtml(doc), PrintOutcome;
//   PRINT_FONT_MIN/MAX/DEFAULT = 7/14/9, PRINT_MAX_LINES = 20,000, EXPORT_MAX_LINES = 100,000
```

**Typing help (P4.12)**

1. **The switches, and auto-space.** `editing.autoSpace` is the profile's flag; `edit.toggleAutoSpace` is a **session switch** like `edit.toggleForceUppercase`: it overrides the profile for every document until the app closes and is never saved (`autoSpaceOn(docId)` and `autoNumberOn(docId)` in `contrib/typing.ts` answer the switch, else the effective profile's flag; false for a document that is not an NC program, where the switch says `typing.notHere`). Both switches are in the Edit tab's Typing group (orders 151 and 152, after Upper-Case Typing's 150). The helpers act in **every editable editor** by its own document's settings (§7 #466: the channel panes, both sides of the aligned view that can be edited) and never in a read-only one.
   *Auto-space acts on a plain letter key* — the keys `uppercaseOf` accepts: no Ctrl, Alt or Cmd, no IME, no dead key. Paste, `insertText`, snippets and an accepted completion are not keys and are never touched. With `forceUppercase` on, the typed text is the capital: one `type` command carries the space and the capital, so it is **one undo step with the keystroke**, exactly as the capital alone is. Each cursor is decided on its own (the `perCursor` form of `decideUppercase`).
   *The rule.* The tokenizer of the effective profile reads the line as it reads with the letter typed (the way `keepsCase` asks it; the line above is tokenized for the continuation state only). A space goes in front of the letter when **all** hold: the letter starts a new address word (a `word` token whose address is that letter); the token that ends exactly where the letter starts is a `word` or `blockNumber` with a **complete value** (a literal, a variable reference, or a closed bracket expression such as `X[#1+1]`); the character before the letter is not white space; the letter is not the first letter of `syntax.exponentMarker` behind a word whose value is a number (Sinumerik `X1.5E`, then `X3`: it may start the exponent); and the letter is not inside a token of kind `comment`, `string`, `text`, `call`, `label`, `programMarker`, `variable`, `expression`, `operator`, `continuation` or `skip`. So typing `G1X` gives `G1 X`, `N10G1` gives `N10 G1` and `G1X5.Y` gives `G1 X5. Y`; the space is only ever put in front of the letter being typed, never behind a letter already there.
   *What never changes* (goldens on the six profiles, each a row of `autoSpace.test.ts`): a letter in a comment, a string, a Klartext program or cycle name, the name after a Klartext `SEL PGM`, `SEL CYCLE`, `SEL TABLE`, `SEL PATTERN` or `SEL CORR-TABLE` (a `syntax.freeText` row of the profile: a name is never split; the tokenizer does not read it as address words), `MSG("…")`; a letter that continues the word before it (`G` then `O` of `GOTO`, `FMAX`, `TOOL CALL`, Okuma `NLAP1`, Sinumerik `CYCLE81`, `LIMS[2]=`, `SB=1200`, `R1=R2*2`, a name with digits such as `VAR1A`); a letter after a word that has no value yet (Klartext `L` then `X`); a letter after a closed bracket that is not a word's value (`IF[…]GOTO`); a letter in a variable expression. **When the tokenizer cannot tell, the answer is "no space"** (a missing space is cosmetic, a wrong one changes the program). Klartext, where words must be separated (`wordSeparatorRequired`), reads `X+10Y` as one unknown run; there the line is read a second time with the space in front of the letter, and when that reading has the letter start an address word right after a complete word that begins where the run began, the space goes in: `X+10Y` → `X+10 Y`. Nothing else is read twice.
   *Cost.* A key reads the line and, at most, the line above; a line longer than `MAX_TYPING_LINE` is left as typed; with both options off a key costs one flag read. The keystroke budget (`typing.slow.test.ts`, 300,000 lines, 200 keystrokes, best of three): p95 below 10 ms on top of the existing upper-case path.

2. **The number of the new line.** `editing.autoNumber` and `edit.toggleAutoNumber` as in rule 1. **Plain Enter** only (no Ctrl, Alt, Cmd or Shift, no IME), **one cursor, empty selection, the cursor at the end of the line's text** (only blanks after it), and **Enter not owned by the suggestion list**; anything else is the editor's Enter, silently. The list owns Enter by the editor's live option `acceptSuggestionOnEnter` (`contrib/typing.ts`, `suggestionTakesEnter`): `'off'` never (Enter closes the list and breaks the line, so the line is numbered whatever the list shows); `'on'` and `'smart'` while the list is visible (the `suggestWidgetVisible` context key), and also when the visibility or the option cannot be read (a number is never typed over a suggestion the user may be accepting). The base block is the line the cursor is on, or, when that line continues a block (the line above ends with the continuation marker, or it starts with `syntax.continuationStart`), the head of that block (`blockHeadOf`). A base block **without a block number**, a line that is nothing but its number, a new line that would itself continue a block (the cursor's line ends with the continuation marker), and a cursor block whose next line continues it by its own leading mark (Okuma `$`: a numbered line in between would take that tail over) are the editor's Enter, silently: **a line that continues a block is never numbered**. A block-skip mark is not copied to the new line.
   *Free numbering* (`numbering.mode` `free` or absent): the candidate is `base + numbering.step`. The first numbered block below the new line within `AUTONUMBER_LOOKAHEAD = 100` lines (unnumbered lines, comments and continuation lines are skipped) is the **ceiling** when its number is not smaller than the base (a smaller one is a restart, a subprogram or another program, and is no ceiling; an equal one is a ceiling, nothing fits below it). Candidate below the ceiling: the candidate. Otherwise the number **between**: `base + floor((ceiling − base) / 2)`; when that is not above the base the answer is `noGap` (`N120`, `N121`, or two blocks with the same number: no number fits). A candidate above `numbering.max` is `overflow` — typing never wraps, only Renumber does. The text is `formatBlockNumber`: the profile's `blockNumber.prefix` (an `altPrefix` and the main-block prefix are never written for a new line), zero-padded to `numbering.digits`, then `numbering.spacesAfter` spaces (default 1); it is typed at the start of the new line (after any indentation the editor gives it) and the cursor ends behind it.
   *The status bar says why there is no number* (`noGap`, `overflow`, `tooManyBelow`, `inPane` of `AutoNumberRefusal`) in one plain sentence each, e.g. "No number fits between N120 and N121, so the new line has none. Use Renumber Blocks on the NC tab." The line break itself always happens; a refusal never swallows the key.
