3. **Consecutive numbering, one edit, limits.** With `numbering.mode: 'consecutive'` (Klartext: every block, step 1, no gaps) the new line is `base + 1` and **the blocks below take new numbers in the same edit**: `consecutiveRenumbering(read, lineCount, line + 1, base + 2, cp, MAX_RENUMBER_LINES)` runs to the program end (`END PGM` included) or the last line. Enter on the `END PGM` line itself is the plain Enter, silently: a block after it is outside the program. More than `MAX_RENUMBER_LINES` (5,000) changed lines is `tooManyBelow`: the plain Enter, the program untouched, and the status bar says so and names Renumber. The line break, the new number and every shift are **one undo step** (one `executeEdits` call between two undo stops; the test uses a real model and undoes once). In a channel pane, free numbering reads its neighbours from the pane's own lines and works; consecutive numbering is `inPane` (the blocks below belong to the document's other channels as well). Block references that follow the numbers do not exist in Klartext (labels, not block numbers), so nothing else is rewritten. Everything reads lines through a `LineReader`; no whole-text copy, so a 300,000-line program types as fast as a short one (the same budget as rule 1).

**Bookmarks (P4.13)**

4. **Names.** A bookmark is a line and, optionally, a name. In the session the name belongs to the bookmark's **decoration**, so it follows its line through edits, undo and transforms; **deleting a bookmarked line does not delete its bookmark: the bookmark and its name sit on the line that follows**; when an edit puts several bookmarks on one line (a deleted range, a join), the line keeps one bookmark, with the name of the upper one, or, when that has none, the first name among them. `bookmark.rename` ("Name Bookmark", no key by default) asks for the name of the bookmark on the cursor's line (`modals.prompt`, the current name prefilled; **the bookmark is set when the line has none**; an empty answer removes the name and keeps the bookmark; cancel does nothing). A name is **trimmed, inner runs of white space made one blank, 1 to `MAX_BOOKMARK_NAME` (60) characters, no control character** (`cleanBookmarkName`); a longer name is refused in the prompt with the limit named, never cut. Duplicate names are allowed. Toggling a bookmark off, "Clear Bookmarks", the panel's remove and Clear All remove the name with it. A document keeps at most `MAX_BOOKMARKS` (200): a new bookmark past that, by Toggle or Name Bookmark, is refused with `bookmarks.full` ("This document has 200 bookmarks, the most it keeps. Remove one first."); removing one is never refused.
   *Persistence* beside the lines, not inside them: `FileMemo.bookmarks` stays the `number[]` of M7 (an older build reads it as before and keeps `bookmarkNames` verbatim like any member it does not know), and `FileMemo.bookmarkNames` is `{ line, name }[]`, ascending by line (the plan's "`{ line, name? }`" per bookmark is replaced by this, §7 proposal below). The loader (`filesOf` in `stores/uiState.ts` and `put` in `stores/fileMemory.ts`) reads it as **untrusted**: not an array → `[]`; an entry that is not `{ line: isLine, name: cleanBookmarkName ok }` is dropped; a second entry for a line is dropped; **an entry whose line is not in `bookmarks` is dropped**; at most `MAX_BOOKMARKS` (200, equal to `MAX_REMEMBERED_BOOKMARKS`, held by a test); `KNOWN_MEMBERS` lists it. The byte cap of `state.json` counts the names (`memoBytes` measures the memo's JSON in UTF-8 bytes), so a hundred named bookmarks do not need a new constant. `session.ts` writes and restores the names with the lines (`restore`); an untitled document keeps them for the session only.

5. **The bookmark panel.** `BookmarksPanel.svelte`, a panel of the **left region** (`id: 'bookmarks'`, `order: 15`, between the Program Map's 10 and the Inspector's 20, title `bookmarks.panelTitle`), shown by `view.toggleBookmarks` (View tab, group `view.groupPanels`, like the Inspector's toggle; it shows the panel or, when it is on show, the panel it replaced). It lists **the bookmarks of every open document**, in tab order, then by line: the file (name; the path in the tip), the line, the name (empty for none) and the line's text (trimmed, at most `MAX_BOOKMARK_TEXT` = 120 characters). A click on a row **jumps**: the document comes to the front and the line is revealed and the editor focused (`editor.reveal`, the coordinate rule of §6.7 rule 3). Each row has a delete button (`bookmarks.remove`) and a way to rename (a button and a double click on the name, both through `bookmark.rename`'s prompt for that row); the panel's header has **Clear All**, which asks once (`modals.confirm`, naming the count) and removes the bookmarks and names of every open document. An open document **without a model** (a restored tab nobody has shown, session rule 3) lists the bookmarks of its waiting memo from `fileMemory` — line and name, no text — and a jump shows the tab first. The panel follows `bookmarks.onDidChange` and the documents' list, and reads the lines' text only while it is mounted. Empty: "No bookmarks yet. Toggle one with Ctrl+F2." (the key through the shortcut formatter, not literal).
   *Ribbon.* The Edit tab gets a **Bookmarks** group (`bookmarks.group`, orders 142–147, after Go To's 140 and before Typing's 150): `bookmark.toggle`, `bookmark.next`, `bookmark.prev`, `bookmark.rename`, `bookmark.addAllMatches`, `view.toggleBookmarks`. The existing four commands keep their ids and keys.

6. **Bookmark All Matches.** `bookmark.addAllMatches` (no key) puts a bookmark on every line of the active document that the **current search of the focused editor's find widget** finds: the widget's state (`editor.contrib.findController`: search string, regular expression, match case, whole word, and the "find in selection" scope when it is on) read in `monaco/bookmarks.ts`, matched with `model.findMatches` on the editor's model (in a channel pane: the pane's lines mapped to document lines, §6.7 rule 3). **All or nothing:** no search string → `noSearch` ("Search for something first: open Find and type it."); no match, or a regular expression that does not compile → `noMatch`; the distinct lines plus the bookmarks that are there already above `MAX_BOOKMARKS` → `tooMany` with the counts ("340 lines match, and a document keeps at most 200 bookmarks. Narrow the search."; when fewer lines match than the limit and the bookmarks already there tip it over, the message names both: "… already has 150 bookmarks. It keeps at most 200."), nothing marked. A line that has a bookmark keeps it and its name (`already` counts them). The cursor does not move; one status message says "Bookmarked 12 lines (3 already had one)." One call, one decoration update, so a search that finds 200 lines costs one repaint.

**Block range, insert and append (P4.14)**

7. **Block range.** `edit.selectBlockRange` and `edit.deleteBlockRange` (Edit tab, group `ranges.group`, orders 148 and 149; no key; not offered without a document, with the compare overlay open or in a locked document for the delete) open one form (`modals.form`; title the command's): `kind` (select: *Line numbers* or *Block numbers*, default Line, remembered in `lastParams['edit.blockRange']`), `from` and `to` (text). The fields start with the selection's first and last line when there is a selection (line mode), else empty. `resolveBlockRange` (pure) answers; **a refusal is shown in the form and nothing is selected or deleted**:
   - *Line numbers:* whole numbers; `notNumber` for anything else; `outsideProgram` when a number is below 1 or above the line count (the count is named); `reversed` when `from` is above `to`.
   - *Block numbers:* the typed text is a bare number (`120`), or one behind the profile's block prefix or one of its `altPrefixes` in either case (`N120`, `n 120`), behind `N` on a leading-integer dialect (Klartext, as Go to Block reads it), or behind the main-block mark where the dialect has main blocks (`:120`); any other letter (`G10`, `X10`, `O1000`) is `notNumber`, so a range never selects or deletes by a word that is not a block number. A dialect that compares sequence numbers as text (Okuma: `N0100` and `N100` are two names) is searched that way, so `100` does not find `N0100`. Each is searched in **all** block heads of the document (`blockHeadReader`; a continuation line is not a head, a number in a comment or a string is not a block number). On Klartext the `BEGIN PGM` line is **block 0** and the `END PGM` line the last block, so `0` to the last number is the whole program. None → `missingBlock` ("Block N120 is not in the program."); more than one → `duplicateBlock`, which lists up to five lines ("Block N120 is at lines 12 and 340. Use line numbers.") — **no guess, unlike Go to Block**; the first block after the second in the file → `reversed`. The range then runs from the first block's line to the **last line that continues the `to` block**, so a block is never cut in two.
   *Select* selects the whole lines (`editor.selectLines`, document lines, the focused group) and focuses the editor; the status bar says "Selected lines 12–40 (blocks N100–N200)." *Delete* removes the whole lines with their line breaks (`applyLines(id, start, end, [])`, **one undo step**), puts the cursor where the range was, and says "Deleted 29 lines: lines 12–40 (blocks N100–N200). Undo brings them back."; the range may be the whole document. The numbers are document lines in a channel pane as well (§6.7 rule 3).

8. **Insert File and Append File.** `file.insertFile` and `file.appendFile` (File tab, group `insertFile.group`, orders 80 and 81; no key; not offered without a document, in a locked document, with the compare overlay or the channel view open — the channel view re-projects and an insert could move lines to another channel, §6.7 rule 10). The file comes from `dialogs.openFiles({ multiple: false })` and is read **the way Open reads it**: its size first (`filesStat`, no answer is refused, `MAX_OPEN_BYTES`), then `fs.readFile`, `decodeFile` without `allowBinary` (UTF-8, the BOMs, UTF-16 and the Windows-1252 fallback; a binary file is refused; a stripped NUL is counted and said), the text turned to LF and written with **the document's line ending**; a final line break of the file is dropped. The dialog and the read take time, so the command asks again afterwards whether the document is still open, unlocked, in front and out of the channel view and the compare overlay (`insertFile.changed` / `notAvailable`). Pure planning is `planInsert` (`core/nc/insertFile.ts`); `contrib/insertFile.ts` reads the editor, applies one `applyLines` call and selects the result. Refused with the file's name and the reason, nothing inserted: not readable, size unknown, too large, binary, **empty**, a locked document.
   *Which lines.* A line that is only `%` (a lone tape mark, `isLoneTapeMark`: a `%` with nothing but blanks around it, in a profile whose `%` at the head of a line is the tape mark) is **left out** of the inserted lines, and the status bar says so ("Left out the % line of the file."), unless the document is empty (nothing but blank lines): then the file comes in as it is. A file of nothing but such lines is refused (`onlyTapeMarks`). A lone `%` is **not a program end** (`cp.re.programEnd` is `M30`, `M02`, Klartext `END PGM` …); the tape end has its own warning below.
   *Where.* **Insert** puts whole lines **before the cursor's line when the cursor is in column 1 with no selection, otherwise after the cursor's line** (after the selection's last line when there is a selection; a selection that ends in column 1 of a later line does not include that line). A block that continues over several lines is never cut: before its first line, after its last. **Append** puts them after the last line; when the document ends with a line break (an empty last line) the lines go **before** that empty line, so no blank line appears; and **before a closing line**: a lone `%` below a line that is not blank (the first `%` opens the tape), or, where the profile has an end record (Klartext), the `END PGM` line, in both cases from the first line of its block. **Never above or on the program's first lines:** a leading lone `%` and the program-start line (the first line after it that is neither blank nor a comment, when it matches `cp.re.programStart` or the profile's header — `O1000`, `%_N_…_MPF`, `$NAME.MIN%`, `BEGIN PGM` — with its continuation lines): an insert point at or above the last of these goes **below** them, except that a file that **starts a program of its own** (its first line that is neither blank nor a comment is a program start or header) goes in front of the program-start line, below a leading `%`. The inserted lines are then **selected** (first to last) so the user sees what came in.
   *Warnings (the insert goes ahead; the status bar says it in red).* (a) **After a program end:** the nearest program-end line above the insertion point (walking up; a program start before it ends the walk with no warning) — "The program ends at line 38. The inserted blocks come after it and do not run unless they are called." (`afterProgramEnd`; a Fanuc file with several programs, `O1 … M30 % O2 …`, does not warn when the insert is in the second, and a file that starts its own program does not warn). (b) **After the tape end:** the document's last lone `%`, when it is above the insertion point, is not the one that opens the tape, and no lone `%` lies below the point; a `%` that a program start follows (the mark between two programs) and a `%` right above lines that start a program of their own are no tape end — "The % at line 90 ends the tape. The control reads nothing after it, so it does not read the inserted lines." (`afterTapeEnd`, instead of (a)). (c) **A program frame in the file where blocks follow:** an inserted line that starts a program (`cp.re.programStart` or the profile's header in any form, Sinumerik `;%_N_…_SPF` too) or ends one (`cp.re.programEnd`) while a block of the program follows the inserted lines (going down, a block comes before the next program start; blank, comment and lone `%` lines are passed over) — "Line 42 (“M30”) ends the program in the middle: the blocks below it do not run. Check the program." / "… starts a program in the middle of this one: the blocks below it now belong to that program. Check the program." (`programFrame`; never a refusal in a profile with several programs per file).
   *Refusals (nothing inserted; the lines named are the file's own).* The file's **last line ends with the continuation marker** (Klartext `~`: the block below would become its tail; `continuesBlock`); the file's **first line starts with the continuation mark** (Okuma `$`) and the insertion point is below line 1 (`continuesBlock`, first); with `numbering.mode: 'consecutive'` (one program per file, Klartext) the file holds a **program start** (`secondProgramStart`, naming its line and, when the file has a program end too, that line: "The file starts a second program (“BEGIN PGM …” at its line 1). A program of this control holds only one: remove that line from the file first. Nothing was inserted.") or a **program end** without a start (`programEndInFile`); more than `MAX_RENUMBER_LINES` changed lines (`tooManyBelow`); a number past `numbering.max` (`overflow`).
   *Klartext numbering.* With `numbering.mode: 'consecutive'` the inserted blocks **and the blocks below take consecutive numbers in the same edit**, from the number of the nearest numbered block head above the insertion point plus 1 (`numbering.start` when there is none) — `consecutiveRenumbering` of rule 9. A line of the file without a number takes one, **comment lines included** (the control numbers every line: `12 ; text`); a blank line and a line that continues a block keep what they have. The status bar counts the blocks that took a new number ("Renumbered 12 blocks."). In every other profile the inserted blocks **keep the numbers they have**; the NC tab's Renumber is the way to change them (nothing nags). The insert and every number edit are **one undo step** (`insertReplacement`, one `applyLines` call).

9. **One numbering helper.** Rule 3's shift and rule 8's renumber are the same function, `consecutiveRenumbering`, in `core/nc/blockNumbering.ts` (P4.12's first commit; P4.14's insert starts after it). It does not call `renumber.ts` (P4d changes that file's options) and it renumbers only the numbered blocks it meets: a line that continues a block and a comment line without a number keep what they have; a Klartext `BEGIN PGM` is block 0, keeps its number and ends the walk (it belongs to the next program). (Rule 8's insert gives the numberless lines of the inserted file, comment lines included, a placeholder number before the walk, so they take their numbers from it.) `formatBlockNumber` is the one place that spells a number for a new line (prefix, digits, spaces after), also used by rule 2.

**Printing (P4.15)**

10. **The print view.** *Desk spike, 2026-10-19 (Tauri 2.11.5, wry 0.55.1):* `window.print()` works on Windows (WebView2 `window.print()`) and Linux (WebKitGTK print operation, `run_dialog`); on **macOS** Tauri's `webview` plugin injects an init script that routes `window.print()` to the native `NSPrintOperation` of the WKWebView (macOS 11+), which needs the permission **`core:webview:allow-print`** in `src-tauri/capabilities/default.json` — the one Rust-side change of P4c (a G8 security item: it grants the webview the print dialog and nothing else). No Rust command is written. The three platforms are confirmed only by the owner's manual check (§10, "print a program on paper and to PDF on each platform"); until then "Export as HTML" (below) is the fallback of Q4-22 and is built regardless.
   *Commands.* `file.print` ("Print", **`Mod+P`**, File tab group `print.group`, order 90, `global`) and `file.exportHtml` ("Export as HTML", order 91, no key). Both open one form (`modals.form`): `scope` (*Document* or *Selection*; Selection only when the selection is not empty and then the default), `colors` (*Colours* or *Black*), `lineNumbers` (check, default on), `fontPt` (7–14, default 9); remembered in `lastParams['print']`. **Neither command has an `enabled` rule**: the dispatcher swallows a key only for an enabled command, and a `Mod+P` let through would reach the webview's own print shortcut and print the application's window; without a program the command runs and says so in the status bar. The dispatcher leaves a key alone while a modal is open and in a text field, so `contrib/print.ts` adds a bubble-phase `keydown` listener on the window (`swallowPrintChord`) that calls `preventDefault` for `Mod+P` in exactly those two cases (the same conditions as the dispatcher, an event someone else already took is left) and prints nothing: the webview's own print would be the application's window.
   *The page model* (`buildPrintDocument`, pure, tested without a webview): the lines are cut into pages of `pageGeometry(fontPt).rows` rows with `.cols` columns, **cut for the smaller printable area of A4 and Letter in portrait with 15 mm margins**, so one document prints the same on either paper (the print dialog's landscape is not supported and the page then shows cut; the manual check says so). A line wider than `cols` continues on the next row (`line: null`, no number); a tab becomes blanks to the next multiple of 4. Each page has a header of two rows: the document's name, the **local date** of the print, and **"Page n of m"** (counted by the model, since a webview's CSS cannot). **Line numbers are the document's line numbers** (a selection keeps its own), not block numbers, right-aligned in a gutter as wide as the largest number. Colours: the role of each token is the one the editor's grammar gives it and the colour is `ROLE_COLORS.light` with the profile's `colors.light` overrides, **always the light palette** (paper is white, whatever theme is on); a role whose colour is under 3:1 against white prints black (held by a test over every role); `Black` writes no colour at all. A test holds the page model's role colours to the Monarch simulation (`core/grammar/monarchSim.ts`) on the corpus lines of the six dialects.
   *The view* (`components/print/PrintView.svelte`, mounted by `app/print.ts` on `document.body` as `.print-root`): hidden on screen (`@media screen { display: none }`), and in print the only visible thing (`@media print { body > :not(.print-root) { display: none } }`, `print-color-adjust: exact`, `@page { margin: 15mm }`); one `.print-page` box per page with a fixed height and `break-after: page`; the font is the editor's monospace stack. It is rendered, two animation frames are awaited, `window.print()` is called, and the view is removed on `afterprint`, at the next print, when the document closes, and after five minutes (macOS's print operation does not block, and `afterprint` is not promised there). **More than `PRINT_MAX_LINES` lines are refused** (`tooLong`, "This program has 41,022 lines; printing is limited to 20,000. Print a selection."); Export has `EXPORT_MAX_LINES`. **The rows are bounded too, not only the lines** (one very long line cuts into thousands of rows): `exceedsRowBudget(lines, textCols, limit)` counts the rows each line would take (`ceil(cells / textCols)`, at least one, trailing blanks not counted, the cell rule of the layout) without building them and stops when the budget is gone; over it the answer is `tooLong` with "This program would take more than N pages; printing is limited to N. Print a selection." (`print.tooManyPages`, `tooManyPagesExport` for the export), `N = ceil(limit / rows per page)`. A failure to open the print dialog or to read the document's machine says `print.failed` ("The print dialog could not be opened.").
   *Export as HTML* writes `printDocumentHtml(doc)` — one self-contained page, inline styles, no script, the same pages — through the save dialog (`dialogs.saveFile` with an `.html` filter; `app/dialogs.ts` gets an optional `filters` on `saveFile`, P4.15's one change there) and `fs.writeFile`, and says "Saved N pages to name.html."; a user prints that file from a browser. A cancelled dialog is `cancelled`, silently.

**Commands, keys and test ids, as proposed for §6.11** (the session adds the pins to `commandPins.test.ts` and the keys to `tests/runtime/lib/shortcuts.js` at the merge; a work package that registers a command checks its own title and key in its own test):

| Keys | Command | Title (i18n) | Where | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| (none) | `edit.toggleAutoSpace` | `typing.toggleAutoSpace` "Auto Space" | Edit tab, group `typing.group`, order 151; category `typing.category`; session switch | `contrib/typing.ts` (P4.12) |
| (none) | `edit.toggleAutoNumber` | `typing.toggleAutoNumber` "Automatic Block Number" | as above, order 152 | `contrib/typing.ts` (P4.12) |
| (none) | `bookmark.rename` | `bookmarks.rename` "Name Bookmark" | Edit tab, group `bookmarks.group`; category `bookmarks.category` | `contrib/bookmarks.ts` (P4.13) |
| (none) | `bookmark.addAllMatches` | `bookmarks.addAllMatches` "Bookmark All Matches" | as above | `contrib/bookmarks.ts` (P4.13) |
| (none) | `view.toggleBookmarks` | `bookmarks.togglePanel` "Bookmark Panel" | Edit tab group `bookmarks.group` and View tab group `view.groupPanels` | `contrib/bookmarks.ts` (P4.13) |
| (none) | `edit.selectBlockRange` | `ranges.select` "Select Block Range" | Edit tab, group `ranges.group`, order 148; category `ranges.category` | `contrib/ranges.ts` (P4.14) |
| (none) | `edit.deleteBlockRange` | `ranges.delete` "Delete Block Range" | as above, order 149 | `contrib/ranges.ts` (P4.14) |
| (none) | `file.insertFile` | `insertFile.insert` "Insert File" | File tab, group `insertFile.group`, order 80; category `insertFile.category` | `contrib/insertFile.ts` (P4.14) |
| (none) | `file.appendFile` | `insertFile.append` "Append File" | as above, order 81 | `contrib/insertFile.ts` (P4.14) |
| Mod+P | `file.print` | `print.print` "Print" | File tab, group `print.group`, order 90; category `print.category` | `contrib/print.ts` (P4.15) |
| (none) | `file.exportHtml` | `print.exportHtml` "Export as HTML" | as above, order 91 | `contrib/print.ts` (P4.15) |

`findConflicts` over the shipped shortcuts plus these rows is clean on both platforms (`Mod+P` is free: `channels.gotoPartner` is `Mod+Alt+P`, and Monaco 0.55.1 binds no `Ctrl+P`/`Cmd+P` in the standalone editor).

| `data-testid` | Element | Extra attributes |
|---|---|---|
| `bookmarks-panel` | the panel's root | `data-count` (all rows) |
| `bookmark-row` | one row | `data-doc-id`, `data-line`, `data-named` (`true` \| `false`), `data-pending` (`1` for a document without a model) |
| `bookmark-row-name` | the name cell | – |
| `bookmark-row-delete` | a row's delete button | `data-doc-id`, `data-line` |
| `bookmarks-clear-all` | the header's Clear All | – |
| `bookmarks-empty` | the empty-state text | – |
| `print-view` | the print root (`.print-root`) | `data-pages`, `data-colors` (`color` \| `black`), `data-scope`, `data-line-numbers` (`1` \| `0`) |
| `print-page` | one page box | `data-page` (1-based) |
| `print-line` | one row | `data-line` (the document line, empty for a continuation row) |

The forms of rules 7 and 8 and the print form use `modals.form`'s own field ids (`kind`, `from`, `to`; `scope`, `colors`, `lineNumbers`, `fontPt`). The harness reaches the services through `h.app.ctx.bookmarks` (the new members) and `h.app.ctx.print` (`build(options)` returns the `PrintDocument`; `print()` calls the injected function the harness replaces, since no scenario may open a system dialog).

**Owns and order** (disjoint; the prelude edited only the files it lists under "Prelude files"):

| Package | Model | Owns | Order |
|---|---|---|---|
