*Prelude files:* `core/profiles/{types,validate}.ts` and `validate.test.ts` (the two members), `app/types.ts` (`FileMemo.bookmarkNames`, `BookmarkService`, `AppContext.print`), `app/context.ts` and `context.test.ts` (`print`, the P4e pattern), the stubs named above, the `todo` test files, and the titles in `i18n/en/{typing,bookmarks,ranges,insertFile,print}.ts`. **Merge care:** BL-UI (`ribbonModel.ts` and the stored tab, `components/panels/**`): P4.13 adds one new file there and does not edit `panels.test.ts` or `ResultsPanel*`; the ribbon entries are `Contribution.ribbon` rows, never an edit of `ribbonModel.ts`; the group orders above are free today. P4d (`renumber.ts`, `references.ts`, `ncNumbering.ts`, profile JSON): untouched here; rule 9 reads `NumberingOptions` fields that exist today and P4d only adds. P4b (`app/types.ts`, `i18n/en/machines.ts`): the prelude's hunks in `types.ts` are the three named, and `app/context.ts` and `context.test.ts` each get one line per milestone prelude (a trivial conflict at the merge). `stores/uiState.ts` is also touched by BL-UI (the ribbon tab's stored state): P4.13 edits `filesOf` only. `src-tauri/capabilities/default.json` and `app/dialogs.ts` are shared with P4f and P4g: one added line each.

**The proofs.** P4.12: `autoSpace.test.ts` (the goldens of rule 1 on the six profiles; no space in each "never" case), `autoNumber.test.ts` (free: step, between, ceiling, noGap, overflow, restart, continuation; consecutive: shift, limit), `blockNumbering.test.ts`, `typing.test.ts` (the handler with a fake editor and with a real model from `monaco/realModel.ts`: one undo step, multi-cursor, panes, the session switches), `typing.slow.test.ts`. P4.13: names (clean, persistence round trip, untrusted `state.json`), the panel, all matches (cap, scope, already). P4.14: `blockRange.test.ts` (every refusal; the end of a block with continuation lines), `insertFile.test.ts` (placement, program end with one and several programs, Klartext, EOL, encoding). P4.15: `print.test.ts` (pagination, header, colours against the Monarch simulation, limits, the HTML). H4c: `p4-comfort` (typing with both switches on in a Fanuc and a Klartext program, a named bookmark through reopen, the panel, all matches, a block range, an insert and an append, the print view's DOM and the export; no system dialog is opened).

### 6.6 Renumber options and references (P4d)

Written by the P4d prelude (2026-10-19), binding for P4.18, P4.19, P4.20a, P4.20b, P4.D and H4d. The scope additions T222 (§5 P4d) and A13 (P4d is the only writer of `src/lib/data/profiles/*.json`, §9 "v1.2.0 triage") are part of it. The stubs carry the signatures; the rules below carry what the signatures cannot say. A rule changes only with a §7 row. Facts from the manuals are cited by the rule that needs them, in our own words.

**Types** (`src/lib/core/profiles/types.ts`; the stubs `core/transforms/programs.ts`, `core/transforms/otherReferences.ts`, `app/renumberOthers.ts`, `contrib/renumberOthers.ts`):

```ts
interface NumberingOptions {                      // additions; every member optional, a profile without them renumbers as before
  fitToMax?: boolean;                             // rule 1
  alignColumn?: number;                           // rule 2: 0..120, 0 = off
  everyNth?: number;                              // rule 3: 1..1000, 1 = every block
  skipContaining?: string[];                      // rule 4: texts of at most 200 characters
  skipFirst?: number; skipLast?: number;          // rule 5: 0..1,000,000 lines
  startTrigger?: string; startAfterTrigger?: boolean;   // rule 6: '' = off
  references?: ReferenceRule[];
  calls?: CallRule[];                             // rule 9
}
type ReferenceProgram = 'own' | 'called' | 'caller';
interface ReferenceRule { trigger: Pattern; addresses: string[]; rewrite?: boolean; program?: ReferenceProgram }  // default 'own'
interface CallRule { trigger: Pattern; address: string; maxDigits?: number; repeatAddress?: string }
// CompiledProfile.re.calls?: { trigger: RegExp; address: string; maxDigits: number | null; repeatAddress: string | null }[]
// ReferenceWord.program: ReferenceProgram                          (references.ts)
// TransformResult.renumbered?: RenumberOutcome                     (transforms/types.ts; only Renumber Blocks sets it)

// core/transforms/programs.ts (P4.19)
type ProgramKey = number | string;
interface ProgramInfo { segment: number; row: number; name: string | null; key: ProgramKey | null }
interface CallTarget { key: ProgramKey | null; text: string }
interface ProgramIndex { programs: readonly ProgramInfo[]; byKey: ReadonlyMap<ProgramKey, readonly number[]>;
  calls: ReadonlyMap<number, CallTarget>; callers: ReadonlyMap<ProgramKey, readonly number[]> }
type TargetRefusal = 'noCall' | 'notInFile' | 'programTwice' | 'noCaller' | 'callers';
type TargetProgram = { segment: number } | { refused: TargetRefusal; key: ProgramKey | null };
programKeyOf(name, cp): ProgramKey | null; callOn(tokens, line, cp): CallTarget | null;
indexPrograms(scan, cp): ProgramIndex; targetProgramOf(scan, index, row, word): TargetProgram;
interface RenumberedProgram { name: string; key: ProgramKey; changed: ReadonlyMap<BlockKey, string | null>;
  created: ReadonlySet<BlockKey>; duplicated: ReadonlySet<BlockKey> }
interface RenumberOutcome { byText: boolean; prefix: string; programs: readonly RenumberedProgram[] }

// core/transforms/otherReferences.ts (P4.20a)
type OtherReferenceKind = 'update' | 'gone' | 'duplicate' | 'lands' | 'callers';
interface OtherReference { line: number; start: number; end: number; text: string; newText: string | null; kind: OtherReferenceKind; program: string }
findOtherReferences(source: { cp; outcome: RenumberOutcome; callers: ReadonlyMap<ProgramKey, readonly ProgramKey[]> },
  others: readonly { lines: readonly string[]; cp: CompiledProfile }[]): OtherReference[][];
applyOtherReferences(lines, refs): { lines: string[]; changed: number };

// app/renumberOthers.ts (P4.20b): renumberOthers: { afterRenumber(docId, result); pending(): PendingOtherReferences | null;
//   update(): Promise<{ documents; references } | { refused: 'nothing' | 'changed' | 'closed' | 'locked' | 'declined' }> }
```

**The options (P4.18)**

1. **Fit to maximum** (`fitToMax`). The step is the largest whole number (at least 1) for which the last block the run numbers stays at or below `max`: `floor((max − start) / (n − 1))`, where `n` is the number of blocks the run will number (with `restartAtProgramStart`, the most of any one program in the scope; with `everyNth`, only the blocks that get a number). The `step` field is not used and the form says so in its help. Without a `max`, or when even a step of 1 does not fit, the run is **refused** (rule 7). The step is not rounded to a "round" number (open question to the owner, report of the prelude).
2. **Align to a column** (`alignColumn`). The code behind a block number the run writes starts at that column (1-based, counted in characters; the leading whitespace and the skip marks in front of the number count). A head that already reaches the column gets one space (or the profile's minimum where words are separated). It replaces `spacesAfter` for the lines it applies to, and like `spacesAfter` it does not apply where a skip mark follows the number (`N120/ G0`: the gaps are copied, the mark binds to its block). A tab in front of the code counts as one character; when the scope has one, the run says once that tabbed lines may not line up on the screen.
3. **Every Nth block** (`everyNth`). Of the blocks the run would number, the 1st, the (N+1)th, the (2N+1)th … get the next number of the sequence; the blocks between **lose** their number (the number and the gap behind it, skip marks kept, as Remove Block Numbers does it). A block that a reference of the file names (resolved, rule 10) is never left without a number: it gets the next number of the sequence and the count goes on. A removed number appears in `renumbered.changed` as `null`.
4. **Skip lines containing** (`skipContaining`). A line that contains one of the texts anywhere, read as written (comments included, the profile's case rule), keeps its text and is listed like a line of `skipStartingWith`. The form field is a space-separated list, like `skipStartingWith`.
5. **Skip the first and last lines** (`skipFirst`, `skipLast`). The first and the last that many lines of the run's scope (the selection or the document) keep their text; every line counts, empty ones too, and the trailing empty element of a final newline does not. Together at least the scope's length: nothing to renumber (the existing `nothing` summary).
6. **Start trigger** (`startTrigger`, `startAfterTrigger`). Numbering starts at the first line of the scope that contains the text (read as in rule 4), or at the line after it; the lines above keep their text and their numbers (they are not listed one by one; one information row says where numbering started). The trigger applies once per run, not per program. A text that is not in the scope **refuses** the run.
7. **What every option shares.** The order of the per-line decisions: continuation tail → blank line → program marker → `skipFirst`/`skipLast` → before the start trigger → `skipStartingWith` → `skipContaining` → `continuationStart` → block name → `onlyNumbered` → stopped at `max` → `everyNth` → number. A line an option leaves alone keeps its number, and a reference to it still resolves (the post-run uniqueness of M6, G8 M6, counts it). **A refusal writes nothing**: `run` returns the input lines unchanged with an identity `lineMap`, no `renumbered`, no warnings, and the reason as its `summary` (`ncNumbering.renumber.refused.<reason>`: `noMax`, `doesNotFit`, `noTrigger`), so the status bar says it and the document is untouched; the preflight asks the same question first where it can be known before the run. The consecutive dialect (Klartext) has no form and no option, as before. Every option is a form field (FieldSpec ids = the member names) prefilled from the profile and remembered per profile (`formKey`); goldens in `tests/fixtures/transforms/renumber/opt-*` on `fanuc-gcode`, `fanuc-lathe`, `okuma-osp` and `sinumerik`. `renumber`'s id, `run(lines, ctx)` and the consecutive path do not change (P4c's Klartext renumbering calls them).

**The reference model (P4.19)**

8. **Which program a reference names.** A `numbering.references` rule names a block of the program the reference stands in (`program: 'own'`, the default and all M6 knew), of the program the same line calls (`called`), or of the program that calls the one it stands in (`caller`). `ReferenceWord.program` carries it; when the rules that fire on one word name different programs, the word is reported and never rewritten (`rewrite: false`, refuse rather than guess). `rewrite: false` still means "never rewrite" whatever `program` says. The program checks read the same member (`program_checks.py`: a rule is a local jump only with `rewrite` not false and `program` `own`; done by the prelude, `TestReferenceProgram`), so a `caller` rule never reports a "missing" block in its own program.
9. **Calls** (`numbering.calls`). A line calls a program when a call rule's trigger matches the comment-masked line; the program is the plain number of the word `address` on that line, or a program-name token (`syntax.programNames`, `<SHAFT_T12>`) read with the profile's `program.start` patterns. Fanuc packs a repeat count into `P` (the 30i manuals: up to four digits the value is the program number; a longer value without `L` is a count followed by a four-digit program number, `M98 P21010` = O1010 twice; a program of five to eight digits is called with its count in `L`): `maxDigits: 4`, `repeatAddress: 'L'`. A variable or expression in the word, a value longer than `MAX_TARGET_DIGITS`, and two call rules that name different programs on one line give no program (`CallTarget.key` null). Program names: the `name` group of the `program.start` pattern that matched the program's first line; digits are compared as numbers where block numbers are numbers (`O0100` is `P100`), everything else as upper-case text; a segment without a name can be neither called nor resolved.
10. **Resolution, per reference** (`targetProgramOf`). `own`: the reference's own program. `called`: the one program of the file with the called name; `noCall` (no readable call on the line), `notInFile`, `programTwice` otherwise. `caller`: the one program of the file whose lines call the program the reference stands in; `noCaller` when none does or that program has no name (the 30i manuals: `M99 P` in a subprogram returns to block P of the caller, in a main program — or a subprogram started as one — it jumps to block P of that same program, and which of the two applies cannot be read from a file whose program nobody in it calls), `callers` when several do. Inside the resolved program the M6 rules apply unchanged (missing, duplicate, outside, incoming, the post-run uniqueness, the landing rows of NC-8). **A reference is rewritten only when it is resolved and provable; every other one is reported with its reason and left exactly as it is** (the M6 rule; the preflight asks before the run, as today). This is per reference and not all or nothing: a renumber of a correct program still renumbers it, and the user sees each reference it could not follow. Each refusal has its row (`ncNumbering.renumber.reference<Reason>Row`, written by the prelude).

The rules per dialect after P4d (P4.19 writes the data; G10 checks it, P08):

| Profile | Reference | Program | Before P4d | After P4d |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fanuc-gcode`, `fanuc-lathe` | `GOTO n`, `IF […] GOTO n` | own | rewritten | unchanged |
| | `M98 Q n` without `P` (local call) | own | rewritten | unchanged; its trigger excludes a `P` so it no longer fires beside the next row |
| | `M98 P p Q n` (a parameter starts the call at block `Q` of the called program; syntax-fanuc §7) | called | reported | called; reported (the called program is followed and renumbered, the `Q` is reported; `rewrite: false`, owner 2026-10-19) |
| | `M99 P n` | caller | reported | rewritten when exactly one program of the file calls this one |
| | `G70`–`G73 P… Q…` | own | lathe rewritten, mill reported (on a mill `G73 P… Q…` is a peck cycle's dwell and depth) | unchanged |
| | calls: `M98 P` (`maxDigits` 4, `repeatAddress` `L`), `G65 P`, `G66 P`, `G66.1 P`, and the `<NAME>` forms | – | – | new `numbering.calls` in `fanuc-gcode.json` (the lathe inherits it; `extends` merges objects key by key) |
| `okuma-osp` | `GOTO N…`, `IF […] N…`, `G85`–`G88 N…` (sequence names compared as text) | own | rewritten | unchanged; `CALL O…` names a program and no block, so Okuma needs no call rule |
| `sinumerik`, `sinumerik-mill` | `GOTO`/`GOTOF`/`GOTOB`/`GOTOC` to `N…` or a main block `:…` | own | rewritten | unchanged; labels are never renumbered. `REPEAT`, `REPEATB` and `CALL … BLOCK … TO …` name **labels** in the 840D programming manual (2006) and are no block-number rule; P4.19 checks the 840D sl manual (V4.92) and adds a rule only if it allows a block number there |
| `heidenhain-klartext` | – | – | – | none: blocks are numbered consecutively, `LBL`/`CALL LBL`/`FN 9 … GOTO LBL` name labels, which a renumber never touches, and `CALL PGM` names a file |

**T222.** The label branch of `mainJumpsOn` (`GOTOB:20` read as a label) is no longer reached by a built-in profile: since bug-fix round 1 a `syntax.labelAfter` jump is never a label definition, so Sinumerik reads `GOTOB:20` as the jump, an operator and a word. It still protects a user profile with `mainPrefix` and without `labelAfter`. P4.19 **keeps** the branch and proves it with such a profile (a test that fails without the branch); removing it would let that profile renumber `:20` and leave `GOTOB:20` pointing at nothing.

**References in other open documents (P4.20a pure, P4.20b in the app; AD-49, Q4-17)**

11. **When.** `contrib/ncNumbering.ts` calls `renumberOthers.afterRenumber(docId, result)` after every Renumber Blocks run (wired by the prelude). A run that wrote (a result with `renumbered`) on document A reads every other open document B whose `DocMeta.profileId` is A's (another dialect's references do not name A's programs), with B's effective profile and its current text. `renumbered` (rule 1–7's run fills it, P4.18) lists every **named** program of A in which the run changed, removed or created a block number; a program without a name cannot be called from elsewhere and is left out. A file that is not open is never read or written.
12. **What is listed** (`findOtherReferences`). In B: a `called` reference whose called program B does not hold itself and A holds exactly once; a `caller` reference whose program has a name, is called by no program of B, and is called by exactly one program among A and every other open document of the profile — that program being in A (`callers` row when several call it). An `own` reference never leaves B. Only a value whose block in A the run touched is listed: `update` (the block has a new number; `newText` is written the way the old value was, zero padding kept — or exactly the block's text where sequence numbers are names), `gone` (the run removed the number), `duplicate` (the new number names several blocks of that program), `lands` (the value named no block before and names one now). The Results panel shows the renumber's own rows (the report the transform service published, docId A) and below them one row per listed reference with its own `docId` and line, columns document, line, the line as written, and what happens to it; the status bar says how many there are and that "Update References in Other Documents" rewrites the ones marked to update.
13. **The update** (`nc.updateOtherReferences`, `renumberOthers.update()`). One confirmation that says how many references in how many documents it writes and how many it cannot. Then every document with `update` rows gets its values as **its own undo step** (`applyLines`, one edit per changed line). **All or nothing:** the references are found again first (from the stored source and the ids and versions of every document read). Rows that stay the same are written; when the rows differ the fresh list is shown and nothing is written; when the renumbered document A changed, nothing is written and the status bar says *Undo your changes in {name}, or undo the renumber and run it again*; a document that would be written and is closed or locked (AD-23) refuses the whole update. The rows marked `gone`, `duplicate`, `lands` and `callers` are never written. The pending list is dropped after a successful update, when the renumbered document is closed, and when a later Renumber Blocks run that writes replaces it (said in the status bar and in one Results row when references were not updated). A listed document that is closed or edited does not drop it: Update finds the references again.

**TypeScript and Python.** Renumbering is a transform (AD-12) and stays TypeScript only; no Python twin. Python reads the reference rules in `program_checks.py` only (rule 8, done). `_nc_lex.py` compiles the reference triggers and not the call rules, which no Python code reads. P4h's profile schema covers every new member (P4h follows P4d).

**The proofs.** P4.18: `renumberOptions.test.ts` (the prelude's rows and `todo`s), the `opt-*` goldens, and one row of `renumber.slow.test.ts` with every option on over 100,000 lines within the existing budget. P4.19: `programs.test.ts` (the prelude's rows and `todo`s), `references.test.ts`, the goldens `m98-subprogram-start`, `m99-p-caller` and `m98-p-before-call` updated on purpose with new `ref-*` cases (a call into a program of the file, a return into a single caller, two callers, a caller in no program, a packed `P21010`, a name call, a program twice); `tests/fixtures/resolved/**` regenerated with `UPDATE_RESOLVED=1`. P4.20a: `otherReferences.test.ts`. P4.20b: `renumberOthers.test.ts` (a fake editor, document store and dialogs: listing, the confirmation, one undo step per document, every refusal). H4d: `p4-renumber` (an option golden through the form; a renumber with `M98 P… Q…` and `M99 P` into the file; two open documents, the list, Update, one undo in each document) and `m6-lathe-renumber` updated on purpose (its `M99 P30` follows the updated golden: resolved into the single caller, whose `N30` keeps its number, so the value stays and the "kept" row goes).

### 6.7 Editor groups and channel panes (P4e)

Written by the P4e prelude (2026-10-17), binding for P4.21–P4.25. The owner's answers of 2026-10-17 (§9 "Channel view and order") are part of it. The stubs carry the signatures; the rules below carry what the signatures cannot say. A rule changes only with a §7 row.

**Types and the service** (`src/lib/app/types.ts`; the stubs in `monaco/editorService.ts`, `monaco/channelPanes.ts`):

```ts
export type GroupId = string;                                  // 'g1' always exists; then 'g2'…'g4'
export type GroupLayout = 'single' | 'side-by-side' | 'stacked' | 'channels';
export type GroupContent =
  | { kind: 'document'; docId: DocId; channelId?: string }     // split view; the channel view with one file per channel
  | { kind: 'pane'; docId: DocId; channelId: string };         // a channel pane of a program with sections (AD-51)
export interface EditorGroupInfo { id: GroupId; content: GroupContent | null }
export interface EditorGroupsState { layout: GroupLayout; groups: readonly EditorGroupInfo[]; focused: GroupId }
export interface PaneSource { readonly docId: DocId; readonly channelId: string;
  model(): ITextModel; docLine(paneLine: number): number; paneLine(docLine: number): number | null }
export interface GroupView { id: GroupId; editor: IStandaloneCodeEditor; docId: DocId; pane: PaneSource | null; focused: boolean }
export interface SelectionLines { startLine: number; endLine: number; empty: boolean; crossesJunction?: true }

interface EditorService {                                      // additions; everything else unchanged
  editorInstance(): IStandaloneCodeEditor | undefined;         // CHANGED meaning: the FOCUSED group's editor
  selectionLines(): SelectionLines | null;                     // CHANGED: `crossesJunction`
  readonly groups: Readable<EditorGroupsState>; groupsState(): EditorGroupsState;
  attachGroup(id: GroupId, container: HTMLElement): Promise<void>;   // attach(c) = attachGroup('g1', c)
  detachGroup(id: GroupId): void;
  openGroups(o: { layout: Exclude<GroupLayout, 'single'>; contents: GroupContent[]; focus?: number }): GroupId[];
  closeGroup(id: GroupId): void; closeGroups(): void;
  focusGroup(id: GroupId): void; focusNextGroup(dir: 1 | -1): void;
  selectLines(id: DocId, startLine: number, endLine: number): void;
  topLine(): number | null; scrollToTop(line: number): void;
  registerPane(source: PaneSource): Disposable;               // monaco/** only
  groupViews(): GroupView[];                                   // monaco/** only
  onDidFocusGroup(cb: (id: GroupId) => void): Disposable;
}
// monaco/editorService.ts: GROUP_EDITOR_OPTIONS (EDITOR_OPTIONS without `theme`), MAX_GROUPS = 4,
//   FIRST_GROUP = 'g1', EditorGroupsNotImplemented
// AppContext.channelPanes: ChannelPaneService { state: Readable<ChannelViewState>; open(id); close();
//   undo(); redo(); flush() }   (monaco/channelPanes.ts; the harness hook h.app.ctx.channelPanes)
```

**Editor groups (P4.21)**

1. **One creation path, no theme.** Every group editor is created by one function of `editorService.ts`. The first editor of the window keeps `EDITOR_OPTIONS` (its `theme` is the fallback of a build without `contrib/theme.ts`); every other one, and any editor created while another exists, is built from `GROUP_EDITOR_OPTIONS`, which has no `theme` (F118: `create()` hands `theme` to the one standalone theme service). In the same synchronous block: the shared `updateOptions` payload is replayed (as today, G8 M2), then the group's **own overrides**. The overrides are a closed list — `readOnly` with `readOnlyMessage` (the group's document's lock), and for a channel pane `lineNumbers` (the document's line numbers, rule 14) and `wordWrap: 'off'` — applied with the instance's own `updateOptions` and never through `EditorService.updateOptions`. Because a standalone editor's `updateOptions` writes every option into the shared configuration service (`standaloneCodeEditor.js`, `updateOptions` → `updateConfigurationService`), the service re-applies every group's overrides after each shared `updateOptions` call, so the last writer never decides another group's look.
2. **The focused group is the editor.** The focused group is the last one whose editor had the text focus (`onDidFocusEditorText`), or the one `focusGroup`/`openGroups` named. `editorInstance()`, `cursor()`, `selectionLines()`, `selectedText()`, `insertText()`, `insertSnippet()`, `triggerAction()`, `presetFind()`, `focus()` and `onDidChangeCursor` act on it alone; `hasFocus()` is true when any group editor has the text focus. `docs.activeId` follows the focused group's document (a pane's document is its program); a tab click shows that document in the focused group, or focuses the group that already shows it; in the channel view a tab of another document closes the view first (decision 12). `onDidFocusGroup` fires on every change of the focused group, followed by a cursor event.
3. **The coordinate rule.** Every line number the service takes or answers is a **document** line. For a `pane` group the service maps through the pane's `PaneSource`: `cursor()` and `topLine()` answer the document line of the pane line, `selectionLines()` the document lines of the first and the last selected pane line, with `crossesJunction` when those pane lines are not one run of document lines; `reveal`, `selectLines`, `scrollToTop` and `insertSnippet`'s `at.line` take a document line and act in the group that shows it — the focused group first, then display order; for a program in the channel view, the pane whose channel holds the line. A line no group shows (outside every channel, decision 7) is not revealed: `reveal` does nothing and the caller's status message stands; `channels` and `contrib/navigation.ts` need no change. A caller outside `src/lib/monaco/**` never uses `editorInstance()` with a line number: P4.21 moves the three that do (`contrib/segments.ts` → `selectLines`; `app/session.ts`'s `topLineOf`/`scrollToLine` → `topLine`/`scrollToTop`) and hooks `contrib/typing.ts` to the focused editor on `onDidFocusGroup` and `onDidAttach`.
4. **The same document in two groups** shares its one model: an edit in one shows in the other, and there is one undo history (Monaco's own). Each group keeps a view state per document (cursor, scroll, folds), saved when the group shows another document or is detached, restored when it shows the document again. Model decorations (bookmarks, AD-50) show in both by themselves.
5. **Attach and detach.** `EditorHost.svelte` renders the layout: one `EditorGroup.svelte` per group, with a splitter between them (`side-by-side` and `channels`: columns of equal width, `stacked`: rows), each group's header (a document group: the document's title; a channel pane: the channel's name and, with one file per channel, the file's), and the channel view's bar above the panes (rule 14). `EditorGroup` calls `attachGroup` on mount and `detachGroup` on destroy; `detachGroup` saves the group's view state and disposes its editor, the group itself stays in the state; `attachGroup` builds a fresh editor by rule 1 and restores the state. The compare overlay unmounts every group and mounts them again on close (AD-6), so `captureEditorViewState`/`restoreEditorViewState` of `diff.ts` become redundant; P4.25 removes them with its entry. `ready` resolves on the first attach of `g1`; `onDidAttach` fires for every group editor that is built.
6. **The decoration owners register per editor.** Each owner paints every editor of `groupViews()` and listens to `onDidAttach` and `groups` for new ones:
   - **Bookmarks** stay model decorations of the document, so every `document` group shows them. A `pane` group gets a mirror: decorations on the pane model at `paneLine(line)` of every bookmark the pane shows, set again when the bookmarks or the projection change. Toggling in a pane toggles the document's bookmark at the cursor's document line (rule 3); the glyph margin stays a shared option.
   - **Motion colours**: one decorations collection per group editor, each painted from its own visible range; for a pane the visible pane lines are mapped to document lines and read with one `statesAfter` per run of consecutive document lines, at most `MAX_LINES` lines per group and update.
   - **Compare marks**: the diff editor is not a group (it replaces the group area, AD-6); the word marks of P4.24 are decorations of its two sides.
   - **The inspector** has no editor decoration: it follows `cursor()` and reveals through rule 3, and so do the program map and the status bar's cursor and Channel items (decision 4). AD-50's list named "the inspector's highlight" (§7 #380).
7. **Commands.** `triggerAction` runs in the focused editor; `undo` and `redo` from a channel pane go to `channelPanes.undo()`/`redo()` instead (rules 11, 15). Each editor has its own find widget.
8. **Closing and caps.** `closeGroup(id)` closes one group; one group left is `single`. `closeGroups()` returns to `single` with the focused group's document (for a pane: its program, at the cursor's document line). Split view has exactly two groups; the channel view two to `MAX_GROUPS` (4). A document closed while a group shows it: the group shows the document the store makes active, and split view with the same document twice becomes `single`; in the channel view a closed channel document closes its pane (fewer than two panes: `single`), a closed program closes the view.

**The channel view (P4.22a pure, P4.22b in the editor)**

9. **Opening** (`channels.sideBySide` on the active document; `channels.fresh`): no channels, a resolution that ran out of time (`channels.problems.tooSlow`) or fewer than two channels found → refused with a status message; more than four → the first four in declared order (decision 8). *Channel sections:* a pane per channel, its model `inmemory://pane/<docId>/<channelId>` in the document's language with LF, filled with `projectedLines(projectChannel(set, id))`, registered with `registerPane` (its `PaneSource` answers through `docLineOf`/`paneLineOf`), then `openGroups({ layout: 'channels', contents })`, the focus on the pane of the channel at the cursor. *One file per channel:* the open channel documents as `document` groups with `channelId`, in declared order; a channel file found on disk but not open is offered through the file dialog first (decision 9).
10. **Edits and re-projection** (`panes.ts` rules 1–6). A pane's content event that the module did not cause is mapped (`mapPaneEdits`) and applied to the document's model with one `pushEditOperations` call, without stack elements around it (they join the document's open element, rule 11). The document's content event (from a pane, a transform, a script, an undo, a reload) re-reads the sections (`channels.fresh`) and re-projects every pane with `reprojectEdits`, applied with the model's `applyEdits` (no history of its own) under a guard that keeps the module from mapping its own edits back. A channel that is no longer in the program leaves its pane open, empty and read-only, with a note in its header; a program that has no channels any more closes the view with a status message. A locked document makes every pane read-only. **A pane edit that would move lines to another channel is refused** (review NC-2, the owner, 2026-10-17): `paneEditMovesLines(before, after, edits)` compares the channel sets before and after the mapped edit; if it moves lines, the module takes the edit back inside the same undo element, re-projects the pane and says *A pane only edits its own channel's lines. To end a section early or add lines after its end, edit the program itself.* (`channelPanes.status.movesLines`). The refused edit is no entry of any history. A line typed or pasted just above a section start goes to the end of the section above, in the same channel (`mapPaneEdits`). §7 #455.
11. **Undo with channel sections** (decision 1). Every boundary Monaco sets on a pane model (its `pushStackElement`, wrapped for the time the view is open) is set on the document as well, and the focus moving to another pane is a boundary; so one step of the document is what one step in the pane would have been. Undo and redo of a pane are taken **at the model**: the pane model's `undo` and `redo` (and, with one file per channel, each channel document's) are replaced while the view is open, because every way to undo in Monaco (the keys, Edit ▸ Undo, the context menu) ends in `editor.getModel().undo()`; they run the document's undo or redo (§7 #467; the prelude's "key handler" would have missed the menu); after it, the pane whose lines changed takes the focus and its cursor goes to the first changed line.
12. **Alignment and scrolling** (`alignPanes`, `waitKey`; decision 6, the owner's rule). The marks come from `channels.fresh` — channel sections: the program's marks of each channel, mapped to pane lines; one file per channel: each document's own — and are lined up with filler view zones (`changeViewZones`, `heightInLines`, P2 F55), never hidden areas (P2 F54). The alignment is computed again after an edit that changes a pane's line count or a mark, and only the zones that differ are replaced. Word wrap is off in a pane, so a line is one line height and an alignment in lines is exact. **Scrolling is always together** (owner): every pane listens to its `onDidScrollChange`; a vertical change the module did not make itself is set on every other pane (`setScrollTop`); horizontal scrolling stays per pane. The trailing zones make the panes equally tall, so the panes reach their ends together.
13. **Marks.** A `matched` wait line carries `gedit-wait-matched`, an `unmatched` one `gedit-wait-unmatched`, both in the line-decorations margin and in the overview ruler; their hover says in plain words with which channels the wait meets ("Waits with Turret B, line 120") or which named channel has no matching wait ("Turret C has no matching wait for "M910P123""); an unmatched or unjudged mark says why from `Alignment.reasons` (`alone`, `missing`, `unknown`, `crossing`, `elsewhere`, `loop`, `answered`, §7 #458). A `none` mark gets no decoration. The colours are theme tokens of both themes, ≥ 3:1 against the editor background (held by a test, as the motion colours are).
14. **Per pane** (decisions 4, 5, 7). A pane's gutter shows the document's line numbers (`lineNumbers: (n) => String(docLine(n))`; with one file per channel the document's own); each pane has its own minimap (the setting "Show minimap") and overview ruler; the active pane's border is `--accent`. The bar above the panes (`channel-view-bar`) names the channels not shown and the number of lines in no channel.
15. **One undo history with one file per channel** (decision 2; `viewUndo.ts`). The module tracks every pane document, calls `noteEdit` on each content event it did not cause, `close()` on the focus moving to another pane and on every `pushStackElement` of a pane document's model (wrapped while the view is open), and routes the undo and redo keys and Edit ▸ Undo/Redo of a pane to the stack; an answer `refused: 'changedOutside'` is said in the status bar ("That file was changed outside the view. Close the channel view to undo in that file."); the stack starts when the view opens (`close()` on every tracked document at once, §7 #461). On close the stack is dropped; every document keeps its own Monaco history; Save, Save All and the dirty marks are per file as always.

**Merge back (P4.23)**

16. The rules are the header of `src/lib/core/channels/merge.ts`: Split into Channel Documents records `splitRecord(…)` (the header and every channel document's projection as made); `channels.mergeBack` is offered when the active document is a program with a record or one of its channel documents; `planMerge` refuses when the program changed in the lines (or the header) of a channel it merges (`channels.merge.sourceChanged`), when a channel document's header changed (`channels.merge.headerChanged`), and when the merged text would move lines between channels (`channels.merge.sectionsMoved`); it skips closed and unchanged documents; the caller writes the answer with `applyLines(source, 1, lineCount, lines)` as one undo step and renews the record. The record lives for the session (P4f decides whether written channel files keep it). `findPartner` (Go to the Matching Mark) moves onto `waitKey`, so it pairs exactly as the view lines up.

**Word marks (P4.24)**

17. The rules are the header of `src/lib/core/compare/wordDiff.ts`: words are the non-whitespace tokens of each side's tokenizer, aligned by a longest common subsequence, a changed word marked whole, an **address word** (letter and literal value, outside an expression) that only moved not marked (review NC-9: operands, variables, comments and calls are never cancelled as moved, §7 #452), no marks over `WORD_DIFF_MAX_WORDS` words a side; in review mode on the normalized text shown. In `diff.ts` the lines of one change pair in order up to the shorter side, the marks are a decoration of each diff side (`gedit-word-changed`), and Monaco's own character marks are not drawn while word marks show.

**The proofs** (decision 14). P4.21: `editorGroups.test.ts` (the `todo` rows, a fake Monaco as in `editorService.test.ts`) and `editorGroups.typing.slow.test.ts` (two groups on one 300,000-line document: the listeners, the motion colours and the bookmark mirror of both groups and the inspector per keystroke, p95 < 50 ms on the best of three rounds, the `p3Typing.budget.slow.test.ts` pattern). P4.22a: `panes.test.ts`, `viewUndo.test.ts` (as written by the prelude). P4.22b: `channelPanes.test.ts` (the `todo` rows; real text models from `monaco/realModel.ts` for the document and the panes, fake editors) and `channelPanes.typing.slow.test.ts` (a 300,000-line two-channel program, 200 keystrokes in pane 2 near the middle: the mapping, the document edit, `channels.fresh`, the re-projection of both panes and the alignment per keystroke, the same budget). P4.23: `merge.test.ts`. P4.24: `wordDiff.test.ts` and the decorations in `diff.test.ts`. H4e: `p4-split` (both themes: a second group leaves the theme as it was), `p4-channels` (a three-channel program with sections and a three-file set: alignment, scrolling together, a pane edit undone in one step, merge back), and the two-pane rows of `p4-perf`.

### 6.8 Folder commands and file associations (P4f)

### 6.9 Keys, packages and the configuration zip (P4g)

Written by the P4g prelude (2026-10-27), binding for P4.34–P4.37, P4.D and H4g. The owner's answers Q4-18 and Q4-23 (§9) and the approval of the Rust `zip` crate (P11, 2026-10-27) are part of it. The stubs carry the signatures; the rules below carry what the signatures cannot say. A rule changes only with a §7 row. The section covers the whole milestone: the keys (rules 1–6), the packages (7–15), the configuration zip (16–36, with the security rules 22–32 that G8 and the code review read first), the language (37–42), the settings search and the dialog's pages (43–44), the commands and test ids (45–46).

**What the prelude wired** (it compiles; `npm run check` is 0 errors 0 warnings; `cargo fmt`, `clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` and the tests of the touched modules pass):

```text
Rust (src-tauri/src)                                         TypeScript (src/lib)
keybindings.rs   keybindings_load / keybindings_save (real)    core/keys/userKeys.ts       file constants, shapes, checkUserKey, noticeOf (final); parse/merge/serialize (stubs)
packages.rs      name rule, root name, manifest, caps (final)  core/packages/packages.ts   name rule, root prefix, layer order (final)
                 packages_list, package_files_list (stubs)     core/config/configZip.ts    group ids, refusal ids, default selection, trust rule (final)
configzip.rs     limits, name shape, layout, entry gate,       core/settings/search.ts     types, searchSettings (stub)
                 inventory, bounded entry read, fingerprint    i18n/locale.ts              LOCALES, tag rule, resolveLocale, fallbackChain (final); loadLocale (stub)
                 (final); export / preview / apply (stubs)     app/keys/userKeys.ts        loadUserKeys (stub)      app/language.ts   activateLanguage (stub)
paths.rs         keybindings_file, packages_dir,               platform/commands.ts        the wrappers and wire types of all seven commands
                 import_backups_dir                            components/dialogs          KeyboardPage / PackagesPage / ConfigurationPage (placeholders), the three tabs
userfiles.rs     read_regular_max and five helpers pub(crate)  core/settings            ui.language, packages.disabled (schema, merge rule, messages)
lib.rs           the seven commands registered                 app/bootstrap.ts            activateLanguage after the settings, loadUserKeys after the machines
Cargo            zip =9.0.5, minimal features, licence recorded i18n/en                    keyboardPage, packagesPage, configuration (namespaces), settings (tab and key messages)
```

**The `zip` crate** (P11, approved 2026-10-27). `zip = { version = "=9.0.5", default-features = false, features = ["deflate-flate2-zlib-rs"] }` in `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`. **Licence: MIT** (the crate's `Cargo.toml` and `LICENSE`; `licenses.json` regenerated: 464 packages, +3; `check-license-list.mjs` passes with no new expression). New crates in `Cargo.lock`: `zip 9.0.5` (MIT), `nix 0.31.3` (MIT; zip's Unix-only dependency), `typed-path 0.12.4` (MIT OR Apache-2.0), `cfg_aliases 0.2.2` (MIT; nix's build dependency, not shipped, so not in `licenses.json`). Already in the lock through Tauri: `flate2`, `zlib-rs`, `miniz_oxide`, `crc32fast`, `indexmap`, `memchr`, `libc`. **Off:** AES decryption, bzip2, deflate64, LZMA, xz, PPMd, zstd, the `time` crate and zopfli — a configuration zip is written by gEdit with deflate, and an entry in any other method is refused on import (rule 23). **Why `=9.0.5` and not the newest (9.1.2, 2026-10-26):** 9.0.1 and later carry the fixes this feature relies on (a read that exceeds the declared size fails, a symlink entry with a huge declared size, overflows in a legacy decoder, an empty archive with its directory offset past the end, `validate_header_consistency`); 9.1.x changes the extraction and writing paths gEdit does not use, and the crate publishes every few days; an exact pin keeps `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` the one decision, and moving up is a commit of its own. **The crate's `extract` is never called** (rule 17; a test reads the module's source). Edition 2024, `rust-version` 1.88, which the CI toolchain (`stable`) has.

**Keys: `keybindings.json` (AD-55; P4.34).**

1. **The file.** `<config>/keybindings.json`, a JSON object `{ "$version": 1, "bindings": { "<command id>": <value> } }`. Rust reads and writes it whole under the shared rules of `config.rs` (at most 1 MiB, atomic, stamped with `KEYBINDINGS_VERSION`, an unusable file kept as `.bak` by the next save, a newer `$version` read and never overwritten); `bindings` holds at most `MAX_USER_BINDINGS` (1000) entries. The webview calls `keybindings_load` / `keybindings_save` and never names a path. A missing file is no overrides; reading creates nothing.
2. **A value** is a key spec (both platforms; write `Mod+…`), `null` (the command has no key on either platform: a default is removed), or `{ "mac": spec|null, "other": spec|null }` (a missing member keeps that platform's default; `null` removes it there). The spec is the grammar of `core/keys/keySpec.ts`; there are no chords.
3. **Reading** (`parseUserKeys`): a bad entry costs the entry, a bad member costs the member, never the file; every problem has its JSON path (`bindings.view.splitEditor.mac`) and an English detail. A command id the registry does not know (a script that is gone, a package switched off) is **kept** and listed on the Keyboard page as unused, so that the key is back when the command is. An entry or member the reader dropped (a typo) is kept raw in `dropped` and put back by `serializeUserKeys` until that command's binding is changed; `__proto__` is never kept (§7 #614).
4. **Which keys a user may bind** (`checkUserKey`; the reasons are `KeyRefusal`): the spec parses; it has Ctrl, Cmd or Alt, unless it is an F-key (a bare letter, `Shift+K`, Enter, Tab or an arrow would take typing away); it is not in `RESERVED_KEYS` (the clipboard, undo and redo, select all, and the system's own: `Mod+Q`, `Mod+H`, `Mod+M`, `Mod+Space` on a Mac, `Alt+F4` elsewhere). A key that **may type a character** instead of running the command is allowed and gets a notice (`noticeOf`): Ctrl+Alt (`Mod+Alt`) with a printing key off macOS, because AltGr is Ctrl+Alt on Windows and on layouts such as the Swiss German one (`dispatcher.ts` already ignores an event that carries the `AltGraph` state, D21); Option alone with a printing key on a Mac. **Capturing a key** on the Keyboard page records the physical key (`event.code`) with the modifiers and refuses an event with the `AltGraph` state, saying why: a key the user pressed with AltGr is a character, not a shortcut. The owner's check on a Swiss German keyboard is §10 (P05, T067).
5. **Applying and conflicts.** The command registry gains `setKeyOverrides(bindings)`; `get` and `list` answer each command with its **keys in force** (`CommandDef.keys` replaced by the user's value per platform), so the window dispatcher, the Monaco bridge, the ribbon, the palette and the shortcut dialog follow with no change of their own, and `commands.changed` bumps. The declared keys stay available as `defaultKeys(id)`. Overrides are applied at `register` time too, so the dynamic `script.run:<id>` commands (re-registered on every rescan) get theirs. **The merge** (`mergeKeys`, per platform): a user key over another command's default gives that command's key to the user's and takes it from the other (`user-over-default`; the other shows no key and a warning); two user bindings on one key give it to neither (`user-user`) until one is changed; `Mod` and `Ctrl` are one key off macOS, so they conflict there. `findConflicts` runs over the merged set for **both** platforms whichever one is running, and the Keyboard page shows every conflict; **nothing is resolved silently** and the registry never has two commands on one key, so its `console.error` for a conflict (which fails the harness) stays reserved for defaults a contribution declares. Save is allowed with a conflict (the other platform's cannot be fixed here); a status message says so.
6. **Script keys, the page and Monaco.** A script's key binds the command `script.run:<script id>` (`RUN_COMMAND_PREFIX`; a package script's id is `pkg-<name>:…`, rule 10); the Keyboard page lists every registered command, scripts included, with title, category, key now, default key, and "changed" / "unused" / conflict marks, a search box, "Press a key" capture, reset per command and "Reset all". The shortcut reference (View ▸ Keyboard Shortcuts) shows the keys in force and marks a changed one, with a link to the page. The keys of Monaco's own commands (clipboard, multi-cursor, the editor's find) are not listed and not rebound. **P4.34 proves on Monaco 0.55.1** that a gEdit action added with `addAction` and a user key wins over a built-in rule on the same key (a unit test of the bridge and a scenario in `p4-settings`); if it does not, the bridge adds the removal rule (`addKeybindingRules`, `-<command>`) for it.

**Packages (AD-56; P4.35).**

7. **Layout.** `<config>/packages/<name>/` with `scripts/` (one level of group folders, `.py`), `profiles/*.json`, `codes/*.json` (templates ride in the code files) and an optional `package.json`. **The folder name is the identity**: `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}$`, no trailing dot, not a DOS device name (`validate_package_name`, twin `isPackageName`); anything else is listed with the reason and not read. `package.json` is a label: `name` (display, at most 80 characters), `version` (32), `description` (500); a member of the wrong type or too long is ignored with a problem (`parse_manifest`); a missing manifest is a note; unknown members are ignored. Nothing in it decides what loads.
8. **Listing** (`packages_list`): the folders of `<config>/packages` by name in byte order, at most `MAX_PACKAGES` (32; the 33rd is a problem, not read); a name starting with `.` is skipped; **a package folder that is a link is listed with an error and not followed** (a package is copied in; a link could point out of the config folder), and an entry inside a package is never followed either. A missing `packages` folder lists as empty (`ensure_dirs` creates it, so there is a place to copy into); **a `packages` folder that is itself a link lists nothing and reports one problem** (§7 #611). Each row carries the manifest, `enabled`, the counts of scripts, profiles and code files, and its errors; the answer also names the folder.
9. **The files are read as the user's own are** (`userfiles.rs`, one reader): names by `validate_name`, at most 64 files per folder, 1 MiB per file, UTF-8, not a link, a JSON object; a file that fails is listed with `text: null` and its error, never dropped. **Over all packages together at most 32 MiB** are read **in one listing** (`MAX_TOTAL_BYTES` of `packages.rs`; the cap is per call of `packages_list` or `package_files_list`, not a running total; §7 #612); a file past it is listed unread with the error `not read: the packages hold more than 32 MiB`, so one large package cannot slow the listing of the rest. Scripts follow `discovery.rs` (the header read is bounded to 64 KiB).
10. **Scripts.** A package's scripts are a root `pkg-<name>` (`ROOT_PREFIX`), ids `pkg-shop-a:facing.py` and `pkg-shop-a:finish/chamfer.py`; no other root has this prefix and a package name cannot hold `:`. The roots, in listing order: `bundled`, the packages by name, `user`, `extra<N>`. A script shadows one with the same file name in an **earlier** root (the existing rule), so the user's scripts beat a package's and a later package beats an earlier one; the losing entry stays listed with `shadowed: true`. A package's scripts are **not editable** in place (`editable: false`; `script_source_path` refuses like it does for `bundled:`; "Copy to user folder" works and the copy shadows the original). The Scripts menu lists a package's scripts under its display name.
11. **Profiles and code files are a layer** between the built-ins and the user's own files (`LAYER_ORDER`). `package_files_list(kind)` answers the enabled packages' files, packages by name, files by name; `userConfig.load` hands the registries the package files **first** and the user's files after, and the M13 rules hold across the whole list with these outcomes: a user profile with the id of a package profile **replaces** it, with a notice; two packages with one profile id: the first by name loads, the second is reported; a package profile with a built-in id is reported (as for a user file); code files are overlays laid on the built-ins package by package, then the user's, member by member (the M13 merge), every changed meaning an `info` notice; a profile may `extends` a profile of any layer below or beside it. `UserFileText` gains `package?: string` and `ProfileProblem` gains `package?: string`, so a problem says "in package shop-a: lathe.json"; `origin` stays `'user'`. The load signature includes the package files, so a switch or an edit makes the next load run.
12. **Switching off.** The setting `packages.disabled` (a list of folder names; `PACKAGES_DISABLED_KEY`; schema, merge rule and message are in the prelude) is read by Rust from `settings.json` like the `scripts.*` keys (`ScriptSettings.packages_disabled`). A disabled package is listed with `enabled: false` and contributes **nothing**: no script root (an id naming it fails "Unknown script folder"), no file in `package_files_list`. A name in the list with no folder is kept (the folder may come back). **The reload follows the switch without a restart**: the Packages page saves the setting, then runs `scripts.rescan()` and `userConfig.load()`; the `script.run:` commands of the package go with the rescan and its profiles and codes with the reload; open documents are re-detected by the existing rule.
13. **Trust.** A package is as trusted as a user script: it is the user's folder, and its scripts run with the user's rights (AD-13). The page says so in one plain line, and switching a package on is the user's act; nothing runs when a package is merely listed. A package that arrives through the configuration zip needs the trust box of rule 31.
14. **The Packages page** (`PackagesPage.svelte`): the folder path ("copy a package folder here"), one row per package (display name, version, description, the counts, switched on or off, errors and manifest problems), a "Reload" button, the problems of the last load. Plain words; names are data and stay untranslated. No download, no "install from a link" (rule 15).
15. **No network, no installer.** A package is installed by copying its folder in, or by the configuration zip; it is removed by deleting the folder. gEdit never fetches a package.

**The configuration zip (Q4-23; P4.36).** There is no relocation and no pointer file: the configuration folder stays where it is. Export and import are two commands of the Settings dialog's **Configuration** page and of the palette.

16. **The layout of the archive is the layout of the config folder**, plus a manifest. `gedit-config.json` (first entry; required on import): `{ "format": 1, "app": "gEdit", "version": "<app version>", "created": "<UTC time>", "platform": "<os>", "counts": { … }, "omitted": [ … ], "skipped": [ … ] }`. Entries: `settings.json`, `keybindings.json`, `machines.json`, `profiles/<name>.json`, `codes/<name>.json`, `scripts/[<group>/]<name>.py`, `packages/<pkg>/package.json`, `packages/<pkg>/{profiles,codes}/<name>.json`, `packages/<pkg>/scripts/[<group>/]<name>.py`. Names follow the rules of the user's files (`validate_name`, `is_safe_segment`, `validate_package_name`). `format` above 1 is refused (`newer-format`); `app` other than `gEdit` or no manifest is refused. `version`, `created` and `platform` are shown and decide nothing.
17. **gEdit reads and writes the archive itself and never calls the crate's `extract`.** Each entry is read into memory under limits that count the bytes really produced (rule 24), each file is written by gEdit's atomic writer to a path built from a fixed base and a validated name; no entry is ever a path, a link, a mode or a time on disk. Methods: stored and deflate only. Export writes deflate, no `time` crate (the entries' dates are the zip epoch, so equal content gives equal bytes).
18. **Export writes** (`export`): the manifest, then `settings.json` **without `scripts.python` and `scripts.folders`** (`MACHINE_SPECIFIC_KEYS`: they name places on one computer, and a file must not choose what runs; the manifest lists them under `omitted`), `keybindings.json`, `machines.json`, the user's `profiles/` and `codes/`, `scripts/`, and every package folder (disabled ones too, with `packages.disabled` riding in the settings). **Never exported:** `state.json` (recent files, session), `.window-state.json`, the backup and recovery folders, every `.bak`, any file outside this list. Files are read with the user-file readers (no link followed).
19. **A file the import would refuse is skipped by the export and listed** (over 1 MiB, a name outside the rules, a second name equal to an earlier one once case is folded, a link, not UTF-8 text for a JSON file, past the total of rule 24) in the manifest's `skipped` and in the answer, so exporting and then previewing the same zip shows every file as `same` (a test). An archive that would pass 16 MiB lists what it left out the same way.
20. **The destination** is a path from the **save dialog**: absolute, no `..`, no NUL, allowed by the fs scope (`dialog:allow-save` already grants the one picked path), and **not inside the configuration folder** (the export must not include itself); the zip is built in memory (at most 16 MiB) and written with `write_atomic`. Nothing is granted.
21. **The source** of an import is a path from the **open dialog**, checked as `user_file_import` checks its source: absolute, no `..` or NUL, allowed by the fs scope, a regular file that is not a link, at most 16 MiB, read through the same no-follow reader (`read_archive_file`). It is read once into memory; `preview` and `apply` work on those bytes.

**Security rules of the import (G8; these are the rules the code review and the skeptic start from).** **Rules 22–25 are final in `configzip.rs` and tested there** (the name shape, links and special files, encryption and method, sizes and counts and ratio, the layout, the bounded read of the archive file, the bounded read of an entry). **Rules 26–32 are written by P4.36 against them** (the preview, the fingerprint, the merges, the backup, the trust flag, staging and rollback); they do not loosen them.

22. **Names that leave the folder.** The shape of every entry name is checked before it is matched against the layout (`check_shape`), whatever file it would have been: not empty; at most 200 bytes and 5 segments; valid UTF-8 whose bytes are what the reader reports (an extra field that renames the entry is refused); **no leading `/`** (absolute); **no backslash** (never converted into a separator); **no `:`** (a drive letter, a data stream); **no `..` segment** and no `.` segment; no empty segment (`a//b`); no control character, NUL included; no segment Windows cannot hold (trailing dot or space, `< > " | ? *`, a device name such as `con` or `nul`) — on every platform, because a configuration is copied between machines. **A name that fails refuses the whole archive.** Only after the shape passes is a name matched against the layout (rule 25), and the target of a write is built from a fixed base plus the validated segments, never from the entry name as a path.
23. **Links, special files, encryption, methods.** An entry whose Unix mode is a **symbolic link** is refused (`link`), a device, pipe or socket is refused (`special-file`), whatever it holds; an **encrypted** entry is refused; a method other than stored or deflate is refused; a folder entry with data is refused. gEdit never creates a link, and a mislabeled entry could only ever become a plain file written to a validated path, but a refused entry stays refused: refuse rather than guess.
24. **Sizes, counts, ratio, bombs.** Limits (`configzip.rs`): the archive file at most **16 MiB**; at most **2,000 entries**; a file at most **1 MiB**; all files together at most **32 MiB**; a name at most 200 bytes; and a **compression ratio** of at most **200 : 1** for an entry of 64 KiB or more and for the archive as a whole. **The sizes in the archive are advisory:** `read_entry` reads at most the declared size plus one byte, counts the bytes really produced against the entry cap and the shared budget, and refuses an entry whose produced length differs from its declared one or whose checksum fails (`corrupt`). `ZipArchive::validate_header_consistency` runs first and refuses duplicate names, local headers that disagree with the central directory (a renaming Unicode-path field included) and **entries whose stored data overlap** (`inconsistent`). Two names equal once case is folded are refused (`duplicate`). A zip inside the zip is an entry outside the layout: ignored, never opened. Nothing is decompressed to disk; the peak memory is the 32 MiB budget. Every refusal found is listed (at most 50, then a count), so the user sees all of them at once.
25. **The layout decides what is used; the rest is ignored and listed.** An entry that passed rules 22–24 and is not a member of the layout (`classify`) is **not written** and is listed in the preview as ignored with a reason: `outside-layout`, `name-not-allowed` (a profile named `Lathe.json`, a script `x.txt`), `reserved-name` (`gedit_nc.py`, the shared library, so a zip cannot replace it, and `gedit_nc` as a script **group** folder, since `import gedit_nc` would find a folder of that name; compared ignoring case). A name with a bidi, zero-width or other format character (U+200B–U+200F, U+2028–U+202E, U+2060–U+2064, the isolates, the byte order mark, …) is `name-not-allowed`: the entry is ignored and listed, the archive is not refused (§7 #610). Files a file manager adds (`__MACOSX/`, `.DS_Store`, `Thumbs.db`, `desktop.ini`) are counted and not listed. The difference between rules 22–24 and this rule is deliberate: an unsafe entry refuses the archive because the archive cannot be trusted; an unknown entry costs only itself because nothing is written for it.
26. **The preview writes nothing and grants nothing** (`config_import_preview`). It answers `ok` with the **groups** (`settings`, `keys`, `machines`, `profiles`, `codes`, `scripts`, `package:<name>`), each with its files and for each file `new` / `replace` / `same` (compared with what is on disk now, byte for byte after the merge of rule 27), its size, and notes; the ignored entries and their count; whether any group holds code (`needsTrust`); the settings keys the import will keep (`kept_settings`); and the **fingerprint** (a keyed 64-bit hash, SipHash with a key chosen at random once per process, over **every byte** of the archive, as 16 hex digits; no new crate — change detection within one run, not a signature; §7 #607). Or `ok: false` with the refusals and nothing else.
27. **The two merged files show their lines.** `settings.json` is merged **key by key**: the zip's keys win, the keys it does not hold stay, and `scripts.python` and `scripts.folders` are **never taken** (a settings file chooses neither the interpreter nor the folders scripts run from); the preview lists each changed key with the old and the new value as text. `packages.disabled` in the settings is **joined**, not replaced: the names on this computer stay, the file's new names are added, so an import can switch a package off but never on (the preview adds a note when the file's list lacks a name of this computer's; §7 #608). `machines.json` is merged **by machine id** (the zip's machine replaces the same id, new ids are added, the user's other machines stay, `defaults` are merged key by key); a zip with a newer `MACHINES_VERSION` is refused; the preview lists the machines added and replaced. `keybindings.json` is merged **by command id**: a string or `null` of the zip replaces the entry, an object `{mac, other}` is laid **member by member** over the user's value (a string there counts as both members), so a platform the zip does not name keeps the user's key (§7 #608). Every other file replaces the file of the same name.
28. **What an import may replace** — only these, only under `<config>`: `settings.json`, `keybindings.json`, `machines.json`, `profiles/<name>.json`, `codes/<name>.json`, `scripts/**/<name>.py`, `packages/<pkg>/**` (as in the layout). **It never deletes a file** (a zip with three profiles does not remove your others), never touches `state.json`, the backup or recovery folders, window state, a `.bak`, or anything outside `<config>`, and never writes into a folder that is a link or over a path whose parent is a link (it skips that file and says why; a rename over a link replaces the link, never its target). The user chooses by **group** in the preview (`defaultSelection`: every group with a change is ticked, **code groups start unticked**).
29. **`config_import_apply` re-reads the archive** from `src`, recomputes the inventory and compares the **fingerprint** with the preview's; a difference (the file changed in between) writes nothing and says "the file changed; open it again". The selection names groups; an unknown group id is refused.
30. **A backup first, always.** Before anything is replaced, apply writes **a zip in this format of the current versions of every file it is about to replace** (the merged files as they are on disk now) to `<data>/import-backups/before-import-<yyyymmdd-hhmmss>.zip` (owner-only folder, the last 10 kept, only files of that name pattern removed; **the backup just written is never pruned**, whatever the dates of older ones say, §7 #613). **If the backup cannot be written, nothing is imported.** The answer names the backup, and importing that zip undoes the import (same preview, same rules). A file that is new has nothing to back up.
31. **Code needs the user's trust.** A selection that contains a runnable group (`scripts`, or a package with scripts; `Member::is_runnable`) is refused by Rust unless `accept_scripts` is true. The dialog shows, above the Import button, one plain sentence — *Scripts and packages run Python on your computer with your rights. Import only a file you trust.* — and a tick box; the button stays disabled until it is ticked (`selectionNeedsTrust`). A package that is only profiles and codes needs no tick.
32. **Staging, then swap, all or nothing as far as a file system allows.** Apply reads every selected entry (rules 22–24 again, on the bytes it will write), checks that each JSON file parses to an object, that a script is UTF-8 text without NUL, and builds the merged files in memory; only then does it write, with `write_atomic`, in this order: packages, scripts, code files, profiles, machines, keys, settings (settings last, so a half-done import never switches on something that is not there yet). A write that fails restores the files already replaced from the in-memory copies, removes the files the apply created and then the folders it created (deepest first, only while empty; §7 #613), reports the failure with the backup's name, and leaves the rest untouched.
33. **After a successful apply** the webview loads again, without a restart: `settings.reloadFromDisk()`, `loadUserKeys()`, `machines.load()`, `userConfig.load()`, `scripts.rescan()`; a status message names the counts (written, unchanged) and the backup.
34. **Plain texts.** The refusal messages are `configuration.refusal.<camelCase id>` (a test holds every id of `RefusalReason` to a message); the dialog names the entry and the reason in words an NC programmer reads (*This entry's name goes up a folder (..) and would leave the configuration folder.*). Rust errors are short English detail (AD-14).
35. **The limits in one place** — `configzip.rs`: `MAX_ARCHIVE_BYTES` 16 MiB, `MAX_ENTRIES` 2,000, `MAX_ENTRY_BYTES` 1 MiB, `MAX_TOTAL_BYTES` 32 MiB, `MAX_RATIO` 200 with `RATIO_FLOOR` 64 KiB, `MAX_NAME_BYTES` 200, `MAX_DEPTH` 5, `MAX_REFUSALS_LISTED` 50; `packages.rs`: `MAX_PACKAGES` 32, `MAX_TOTAL_BYTES` 32 MiB; `keybindings.rs`: `MAX_BINDINGS` 1000. A test pins each number.
36. **Tests P4.36 owes the rules** (the prelude's `todo` rows name them): a zip with a `..` name, an absolute name, a backslash name, a drive name, a symlink, an encrypted entry, a bomb (declared small, produces large), a case duplicate, overlapping entries, a renaming extra field, too many entries and an over-size file each refused with its reason and nothing written; an archive that changes between preview and apply writes nothing; a failing write mid-way restores; the backup is written first and a failing backup stops the import; scripts without the trust flag are refused in Rust; `scripts.python` in a zip's settings is kept as the user's; export then preview is all `same`; the round trip of every kind of file.

**Language (Q4-18; P4.37).**

37. **The setting.** `ui.language` (a string, default `en`; `isLocaleTag`: a language and an optional region or script, `de`, `de-CH`) in `settings.json`. A value that is not a tag is dropped with the usual warning (the default stays); a tag with no message set that ships shows **English** (`resolveLocale`: the exact tag, else the language, else `en`, case ignored). There is no "follow the system" value: with one language shipping it would mean nothing, and the owner may add it with the first translation.
38. **A message set ships when its folder and its row exist.** English (`src/lib/i18n/en/`) is complete, loaded with the app and the last of every fallback. Another language is a folder `src/lib/i18n/<tag>/` with the namespace files of `en/` (it may be partial) **and a row in `LOCALES`** (`i18n/locale.ts`: tag and the language's own name); `locale.test.ts` holds the two together, holds every key of a set to a key of `en/` with the same placeholders (and the plural forms its language needs), and flags a set that is missing keys as a count, not a failure. **Nothing ships in v1.2.0** unless the owner provides one (Q4-18).
39. **Loading and fallback.** Sets other than English are loaded lazily by `loadLocale(tag)` (a dynamic import of the folder, so English-only builds carry no second set). `activateLanguage()` (`app/language.ts`) runs in `bootstrap.ts` right after `settings.load()` and before the user files and the first render; it makes the translator look in the loaded set first and in English second. A key in neither shows the key itself and warns once in a development build (the existing `onMissing`). Plural selection uses the loaded language's `Intl.PluralRules`. A set that cannot be loaded leaves English in use and shows one notice. `i18n/index.ts` (`t`, `hasKey`) keeps its signatures; it is a hot file and P4.37's change to it is made by the session at merge.
40. **The language applies at the next start.** Commands, ribbon captions and panel titles resolve their text when they are built, so switching while the app runs would leave half of the window in the old language; the dialog says *The change applies the next time gEdit starts.* and nothing more is built (no live switch).
41. **Not translated:** profile and machine names, templates and script texts (data, AD-14), the descriptions of the code database, the Rust error details, the system items of the macOS menu, the user guide. The Settings dialog shows the language row only when `offersLanguageChoice()` (a second language ships); the schema row is `dialog: false` until then.
42. **Hot files.** Only the session edits `src/lib/i18n/index.ts`, `tests/unit/commandPins.test.ts` and this plan at merge; P4.37 and P4.34 hand their changes to those files over in their report (the prelude put everything else in files of its own: `i18n/locale.ts`, `app/language.ts`, `app/keys/userKeys.ts`).

**The settings search and the dialog (P4.37).**

43. **The search box** at the top of the Settings dialog finds a setting by its **label, help text or key** and a page by its heading, in the language in use (`searchSettings` over `SearchEntry`s the dialog builds from `SETTING_FIELDS` with the rows that are in the dialog, and from the page headings): every word of the query must occur (case and accents ignored), best first (label start, label, help, key; table order inside a class), at most 20; an empty query finds nothing. Choosing a hit opens its page and focuses the control (`data-field`); Escape clears the box before it closes the dialog. It does not search the contents of Machines, Profiles, Keyboard or Packages; their headings are entries.
44. **The dialog's pages.** The prelude wired three tabs after Machines and Profiles — **Keyboard**, **Packages**, **Configuration** (`KEYBOARD_TAB`, `PACKAGES_TAB`, `CONFIGURATION_TAB`; `PAGE_TABS` lists the five tabs that are not settings categories) — each a component of its own (`KeyboardPage`, `PackagesPage`, `ConfigurationPage`; placeholders until P4.34, P4.35, P4.36). Like Machines and Profiles they act at once on their own file or folder: the dialog's Save does not cover them and its "Reset category" is off on them. **P4.37 is the sole editor of `SettingsDialog.svelte` from here;** the three pages are edited only in their own files.

**Commands and test ids (extends §6.11; the rows are pinned `pending` in `commandPins.test.ts` by the session at merge).**

45. Commands: `config.export` — title `configuration.exportTitle` "Export Configuration…", category `configuration.category`, palette and the Configuration page, no key, `contrib/configuration.ts` (P4.36); `config.import` — `configuration.importTitle` "Import Configuration…", same. The Keyboard page and the Packages page are reached through Settings (`settings.open` with the tab); no key is assigned to any P4g command. User keys, once saved, change the keys the dialog and the harness's `shortcuts.js` table read from the registry only for a run that has a `keybindings.json`; the default table is unchanged.
46. Test ids: `settings-keyboard`, `settings-packages`, `settings-configuration` (the pages, placeholders now); P4.34 adds `keyboard-row` (`data-command`, `data-source` = `default` \| `user`, `data-conflict`), `keyboard-capture`, `keyboard-reset`, `keyboard-search`; P4.35 `package-row` (`data-package`, `data-enabled`), `package-toggle`, `package-reload`; P4.36 `config-export`, `config-import`, `import-preview` (`data-state` = `ok` \| `refused`), `import-group` (`data-group`, `data-runnable`), `import-file` (`data-action`), `import-trust`, `import-confirm`; P4.37 `settings-search`, `settings-search-hit` (`data-id`, `data-tab`). The harness reaches the services through `h.app.ctx` (`commands.defaultKeys`, `userKeys`, `packages`).

## 7. Where Phase 4 deviated from the contracts

Rows are numbered from #292 in the format of P3 §7. Every entry is additive or a narrowing, except where the row says it is a deviation (#332, #335). #311–#360 are the fixes of the P4a reviews (§5 P4a, the "As built" note on the reviews); the batches reserved blocks of numbers (B1 #311–#329, B2 #330–#339, B3 #340–#344, B4 #345–#349, B5 #350–#354) and the numbers they did not use are empty. P4e starts at #380 (the P4e prelude, #380–#392); #374–#379 are empty. The P4e packages reserved #393–#399 (P4.21), #400 (P4.22a), #410–#414 (P4.24), #420–#427 (P4.22b), #430–#436 (P4.23) and #440–#449 (P4.25); the fixes of the P4e reviews are #451–#472, those of the first runtime-suite run #473–#479; the numbers not used are empty. The pre-wave round of v1.2.0 follows: the NC fix round #480–#500, the fuzz review #501–#504 and the Brand batch #505–#512. The package rows were written afterwards from the packages' hand-over notes and commit messages (the packages proposed them in their reports, which were not kept in full). #382 changes the meaning of `editorInstance()` (the focused group's editor) and #388 the pairing of Go to the Matching Mark, both on the owner's decisions or for the groups. #518–#571 are Wave X: P4b (#518–#537, fix batches 1 to 7), P4c (#538–#544), P4d (#545–#555), round X and its fixes (#556–#561) and the backlog batches (#562–#571). #572–#576 are the later backlog rows, and #577–#605 are the fix round of the owner's manual check (2026-10-25/26; §9, "The manual check"): batches B1 to B6 and their integration (#577–#599) and the review round (#600–#605).

| # | Contract | What was done instead | Why | Step |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 292 | Scripts README: "a script imports `gedit_nc` and nothing else"; AD-48 ("`extents.py` imports it") | `_nc_path.py` and `_nc_geom.py` sit **above** `gedit_nc` (they import it, it does not re-export them); a bundled script imports them by name, and the scripts README says they are internal | Re-exporting them through `gedit_nc` would make the facade import modules that import the facade; the geometry is a layer on top of the modal state, not part of it | P4a prelude |
| 293 | §5 P4a item 3 (`machine.json` optional; `expected` or `refused.json`) | The machine is the `machine` member of an optional `case.json` (with `profile`, `machineName`, `input`), as in the address arithmetic goldens; a refused case holds `refused.json` (`{ message, findings }`) instead of `expected.nc` and `envelope.json`, and the text handed back must equal `input.nc`; `helpers.geometry_cases` and `helpers.geometry_context` read the format; the script tests exist as skipped stub runners | The generator of `tests/fixtures/resolved/` finds a machine golden by a top-level `machine` member and its profile by `profile` in a JSON file of the folder; a second file name would need a second rule in it and in the Python helpers. `helpers.script_cases` wants exactly one `expected.*`, which a refused case does not have | P4a prelude |
| 294 | AD-48 ("without changing a result") | One extents reading changes: `G2`/`G3` with a centre and `AR=` but no end point was read as a **full circle** that left the tool at its start (a synthetic check on `sinumerik-mill` gives X 15.0865 to 84.9135 for an arc of 140°); the tracker reports the arc as not resolved and the end as not known (`'arc'`). P4.1 adds the regression test (`OpeningAngleTest` in `test_nc_path.py`) | No extents golden and no owner-public program has the form, so the guard of AD-48 still holds byte for byte; a transform that trusted the old end point would move the next block from the wrong place | P4a prelude (P4.1 implements) |
| 295 | §5 P4a item 5, the proposals | Decided otherwise from the manuals: Sinumerik `AR=` **keeps** its value under a mirror (it is unsigned; the direction swaps with the code); Klartext `CCA` has no sign to follow (it stays); Sinumerik `DIAMON`/`DIAMOF`/`DIAM90` refuse **nothing** under the lathe Z mirror | §5 P4a, "Decided by the prelude": a sign change of `AR=` would write an invalid arc; the Z mirror reads no X, so the diameter mode does not matter to it | P4a prelude |
| 296 | X22 (the reasons as listed) | Reason ids beyond X22's list, each a refusal the rules of AD-41–AD-44 imply: `position-unknown` (rotate and mirror need the start of a block, AD-44), `rotary-turned` (translate while a rotary axis stands turned without tool centre point control, address arithmetic's rule), `word-unknown`, `unknown-code`, `swap-unknown`, `arc-check`, `resolution` (an exact operation whose result does not fit the word, Q4-4), `pole-unknown`, `mill-z-mirror`, `single-pass-taper`; and the warning `helix-hand` | "Every assumption is a refusal reason" (§8); X22 names the cases of the plan, the rules need the rest, and a stable id per reason is what the goldens count | P4a prelude |
| 297 | AD-42 ("`arcDirection` on the arc codes, `G2`/`G3` and their dialect forms"; "`compSide` on the radius-compensation codes") | Not flagged: Fanuc `G41.2`–`G42.6` and `G41.3`, Okuma `G102`/`G103`/`G132`/`G133`, Sinumerik `CIP`/`CT`, the Klartext arc codes; a mirror refuses a block with one (`swap-unknown`, `arc-tangential`, `arc-unsupported`), and `geometry-flags.json` says why per code | The swap rule needs exactly one partner per group: a second `left` code (`G41.2`) would leave `G42`'s partner ambiguous; the C-axis contour arcs and the five-axis compensation are refused by the transforms anyway; the Klartext direction is the `DR` word, not the code | P4a prelude |
