Clarified: `roles_of`, `refusals_of`, `notes_of`, `read_word` and `write_word` read the tracker's modal state as it stands after the step's line, so a script asks them right after `tracker.line` (the test helper `walk` stops at the line asked); a refusal from `write_word` carries line 0 for the caller to set; call arguments and Klartext `Q` parameters of a cycle are listed by `roles_of` as words made up for them (a `Token` with the value's span in the line) | `check_arcs(arcs, units)` cannot see a radius arc's `R` (the `Arc` holds the centre the tracker computed from it, so its two radii agree by construction), and the scripts need the rest to read, write and report without copying the rules; the state a step was read in is not in the `Step` (its slots are final) | P4.2 |
| 299 | §6.1 item 11 and X22 (`frame`, `coordinate-shift`: "a frame or shift in force refuses"); P10 rule 13 (`frameZeroWords`: an angle written without a value or incrementally is not described) | **A reset refuses nothing and closes what it resets.** `refusals_of` refuses a code that opens a frame only where the modal state has a frame opened **at that block** (bare `ROT`, `MIRROR`, `SCALE`, `TRANS`, `CYCLE800()`, `PLANE SPATIAL SPA+0 SPB+0 SPC+0`, `PLANE RESET`, Fanuc `G69`, `G50.1`, `G15`, `G50` close instead). Modal rule 13, both languages: a `frameZeroWords` word written without a value counts as not zero, and one written incrementally (the tokenizer's incremental word, or the word behind the profile's `syntax.incrementalPrefix`: `IROT` for `ROT`) is an increment (zero changes nothing, so a block of zero increments changes no frame). Data, `review: "pending"`: Klartext `CYCL DEF 10` `frameZeroWords: ["ROT"]` in a new group `rotation`, `CYCL DEF 8` `frameZeroWords` (the mirrorable axes) with `frameEmptyCloses: "1"` in the group `mirror` (the frames of 8, 10, 11 and 26 no longer share one group, so a reset ends only its own); a new `CodeEntry.shiftZeroIsNone` (TS type, loader, `flags.json`) on Klartext `CYCL DEF 7`, Fanuc `G52`, Sinumerik `TRANS`, `ATRANS`: a block whose every axis value is a written zero sets no shift and is not refused (`G92 X0` is not flagged: it sets the position). A shift block with a value nobody describes (`CYCL DEF 7.1 #5`, a line of the datum table) now refuses (`coordinate-shift`; it used to pass). A frame code written as a call (`CYCLE800()`) is no subprogram call. The modal golden `heidenhain-klartext/frame-resets` holds the rules in both languages; `test_klartext_plane_reset_ends_the_tilt_and_not_the_mirror` in `test_modal.py` changed on purpose (cycle 8 opens at its 8.1, not at 8.0) | CAM programs start with these resets, and they refused every such program (the main cause of refusals on the owner-public programs). | 480 | #304, #320; `CodeParam` (T009, T012) | New optional member `CodeParam.lengthAlong`: an axis letter or `'plane'`. The parameter is a length along that axis or in the working plane (a size, a diameter, a radius or a taper; never a position). Address arithmetic never changes it on a shift; a multiply or divide of its axis (either axis of the plane for `'plane'`, every axis while the plane is not known) refuses the block, and the cycle or series of passes it belongs to, as `not-scaled` while the value is written and not 0. The loader accepts it only with no `position` or `"position": "none"` | A multiply scaled the positions of a cycle and left its sizes, silently (T009 and T012 were reproduced); whether one factor on both axes is meant is not for gEdit to decide | NCF-A |
| 481 | T009 | The taper `R` of the lathe single passes (`G90`, `G92`, `G94`, system B `G77`, `G78`, `G79`) is `lengthAlong` X (turning and threading) or Z (facing). A shift lists it as `data` when `R` is chosen; a multiply or divide of its axis leaves the series of passes as written while a taper is in force. `R` stays in force for the next pass blocks until a block writes it again; the passes from the code until a move leaves it are one series (one refused block leaves all as written), and a selection may start inside a series. Kept cautious: a taper written early refuses later straight passes; `R` and `X` with the same factor are refused too | The control keeps `X`, `Z` and `R` for the passes; a multiply that scales the end point and not the taper cuts a different cone | NCF-A |

(2) **The bookmark panel lists a tab that came back with the session from its waiting memo** (`app/memoWaiting.ts`, kept by the file tracker): such a tab has its model from the start, so "has no model" never said it; a click shows the tab first and then reveals the line. | 611 | §6.9 rule 8 (a package folder that is a link is not followed) | A `packages` folder that is itself a link lists nothing and reports one problem | The link could point out of the configuration folder, as for a package folder | P4g fix round (SEC-7) |
| 612 | §6.9 rule 9 (32 MiB over all packages) | The cap is per listing (per call), not a running total over the session | Each listing reads afresh; a total over the session would starve a later listing | P4g fix round |
| 613 | §6.9 rules 18, 30, 32 | The apply's own backup is never pruned (older backups dated later cannot cost it its place); rollback removes the folders the apply created (deepest first, only while empty); the export skips a second name equal to an earlier one after case folding and lists it | A clock that ran ahead could prune the undo of the import just made; a failed import left empty folders; two names equal under case folding are refused on import | P4g fix round (SEC, SKEP-1) |
| 614 | §6.9 rule 3 (`serializeUserKeys`) | `serializeUserKeys` keeps the raw entries the reader dropped (`dropped`) until that command's binding changes; a command id `__proto__` is never kept | A typo in the file such as `Mod+Shft+K` was lost when any other key was saved; `__proto__` would change the prototype of the result | P4g P4.34 |

The plan itself departs from the design notes in six places, recorded here so the notes can be brought up to date when the work lands:

| # | Source | What changes | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| – | nc-transformations, "Geometry transforms" | The run options "arc-centre convention" and "distance mode to assume at the start" are dropped (AD-43) | Both are facts of the control: the profile's `arcCenterMode` with `AC()`/`IC()` per word, and the machine's power-on mode; a per-run choice lets a guess in |
| – | nc-transformations, "Geometry transforms", common edge cases | "Incremental words are kept" holds for translate only; rotate and mirror turn or flip incremental moves, arc vectors and `IPA` | An incremental move is a vector; rotating the path rotates it |
| – | nc-transformations, "Expand drilling cycles" | `G74`, `G76`, `G84`, `G87`, `G88` are not expanded (AD-45) | Tapping and oriented or manual boring cannot be written as plain moves without changing what the tool does |
| – | nc-transformations, "Geometry transforms" | A lathe gets a Z mirror for the counter spindle (AD-43), in the ZX plane; the design note's "first version: XY plane only" holds for mills (Q4-2, Q4-3) | The owner mirrors main-spindle programs for the counter spindle |
| – | settings-ui, "Configuration portability" | Relocation of the configuration folder is not built; export and import only (Q4-23) | The owner decided export and import are enough |
| – | P2 §2.1 (JSON Schema files cut, F6) and P2 §11 item 17 (Phase 4) | The schemas are built, and the hand-written validators stay the gate (AD-57) | The form editor needs a schema; the test that holds both to one answer keeps the old reason (one gate) intact |

## 8. Risks and mitigations

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| A transformed program runs into the part or the fixture | All-or-nothing refusal (AD-41); every assumption is a refusal reason (X22); the differential oracle and the fuzz review (§4 rule 7); exactness where possible and the arc check where not (AD-44); the owner's check on the machine's own simulation or a dry run (§10) |
| Rounding after a rotation makes an arc the control rejects | The arc check after rounding with the tolerance of Q4-4; the run is refused at that arc |
| A mirror turns climb into conventional milling unnoticed | One warning in every mirror result (AD-42) |
| An expanded cycle does something else than the cycle | Schemas only for the cycles the manuals describe step by step; extents equality per golden (X23); tapping and oriented boring never expanded; the peck retract and clearance asked on each run and required (AD-45) |
| The time estimate is read as a promise | A lower bound by design, said in the report; "not estimated" counted with lines; no built-in rate (AD-46, AD-47) |
| The extraction of the path tracker changes extents | Byte-for-byte extents goldens and the owner-public runs as the gate of P4.1 (AD-48) |
| A second editor breaks the main one (shared options, global theme) | AD-50: one creation path with the shared instance options and no `theme`; the code review names it; `p4-split` runs both themes |
| A channel pane's edit lands on the wrong lines of the document | A minimal mapped edit with tests on random edits against a full re-projection; a change of the source between split and merge refuses the merge (AD-51) |
| The folder walk exposes more of the disk than asked | A command that answers only the search, no directory grant, no link followed out, caps; the security review of G8 (AD-53) |
| Two instances overwrite each other's state once files open from the OS | The single-instance hand-over (AD-54) |
| A user key file breaks a default that a scenario or the user relies on | `findConflicts` on the merged set, conflicts shown and never resolved silently, reset per key; `commandPins.test.ts` pins the defaults (AD-55) |
| A configuration zip writes outside the config folder | Entry paths checked against the folder before anything is written; the preview shows every file; oversized entries refused |
| The JSON Schema and the validator disagree | The agreement test of AD-57 on every built-in, fixture and broken profile |
| Timing tests fail under load | Slow tests in their own group (§4 rule 6); counts where they can replace a clock |
| Eight milestones stretch the phase | Each milestone stands alone and lands with its release; the rows the owner cut on 2026-10-12 are not built |
| A lathe Z mirror leaves the tool nose direction wrong for the counter spindle | One warning in every lathe mirror result; thread, roughing and turning cycles refused (AD-43) |

## 9. Owner decisions and defaults

The owner answered all 25 questions on 2026-10-12. Each item keeps its question and the default the plan proposed, and a **Decided** line under it records the answer: as proposed, or what changed. Five answers changed the plan (Q4-2, Q4-9, Q4-14, Q4-23, Q4-24); the plan above already carries them.

**Geometry (P4a)**

- **Q4-1. A refused block in a transform.** Proposed: nothing is written and every refused block is listed (AD-41). Alternative: change what can be judged and list the rest, as address arithmetic does.
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed. A refused block means nothing is written, and every refused block is listed.
- **Q4-2. Rotate and mirror on a lathe.** Proposed: refused in Phase 4; translate only (X and Z, X as the machine reads it). Question: is a mirror in Z (a program for the counter spindle) something the owner would use, and on which machine?
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** changed. On a lathe: translate (X as the machine reads it, and Z) **and a Z mirror for the counter spindle**: Z mirrors about a Z the user gives (default Z0), `G2`↔`G3` and `G41`↔`G42` swap from data (AD-42), X is untouched, and of `U`/`W` only `W` changes sign. No rotation on a lathe; a lathe X mirror is refused. The Z mirror works in the ZX plane (`G18`), a lathe-only exception to Q4-3. What the Z mirror does to thread cycles (`G76`, `G92`, `G32`), to the roughing cycles `G71`–`G73` and to Sinumerik `DIAMON` and the turning cycles is decided from the manuals in the P4a prelude (§5 P4a, item 5); proposed: refused.
- **Q4-3. Planes.** Proposed: rotate and mirror in the XY plane only, as the design note says "at first".
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed. Rotate and mirror in the XY plane only for mills; the lathe Z mirror of Q4-2 is the one exception.
- **Q4-4. Rounding and the arc tolerance.** Proposed: a word with a point keeps its own decimals and gets at least three in mm and four in inch when a rotation or an angled mirror rounds; a point-less word stays whole increments of the machine; after rounding, start and end radius of an arc may differ by at most 0.002 mm (0.0001 in), the slack the extents already use, otherwise the run is refused. Question: does one of the owner's controls check arcs more tightly?
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed. 0.002 mm (0.0001 in); a word keeps its own decimals, at least 3 in mm and 4 in inch.
- **Q4-5. A distance mode that is only assumed.** Proposed: refused, unless the program writes `G90`/`G91` (or the dialect's form) before the first moved block or the machine sets the power-on mode.
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed.
- **Q4-6. `G28`/`G30` intermediate points.** Proposed: an incremental return of zero (`G91 G28 Z0`) is left and listed as information; an absolute intermediate point that the transform would change refuses the run; `G53`, `M91` and `SUPA` are left and listed.
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed.
- **Q4-7. The mirror's cutting direction.** Proposed: a warning only; no reversal of the path.
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed. A warning only; the path is not reversed.

**Drilling cycles and time (P4b)**

- **Q4-8. Which cycles expand.** Proposed: Fanuc mill `G73`, `G81`, `G82`, `G83`, `G85`, `G86`, `G89`; Klartext 200, 201, 203; Sinumerik `CYCLE81`, `CYCLE82`, `CYCLE83` (basic parameters), `CYCLE85`, `CYCLE86`, `CYCLE89`. Not: tapping (`G74`, `G84`, cycles 206, 207, 209, `CYCLE84`, `CYCLE840`), `G76`, `G87`, `G88`, the lathe and Okuma cycles. Question: which of the refused ones does the owner need, and for which control?
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed. The plain drill, peck and bore list.
- **Q4-9. Peck retract and clearance.** The high-speed peck (`G73`) retracts by a distance and the deep peck (`G83`) stops short of the last depth by a distance; both are parameters of the control. Proposed: they are machine values (AD-46); without them the two cycles are refused with "set the peck values of this machine". Question: can the owner read them off the machines?
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** changed. The `G73` retract and the `G83` clearance are **asked in the expand form on each run**, prefilled from the machine's motion block when it sets them. Without a value in the form the two cycles cannot run: the field is required for a program that contains them. The peck values stay in the motion block as the prefill only (AD-45, AD-46). The statistics form asks the same two fields in the same way; because it is a report and writes nothing, a `G73` or `G83` without a value is reported as "not estimated" with its lines, and the rest of the report is given.
- **Q4-10. Rates when no machine is chosen.** Proposed: lengths are always reported; times need a rapid rate and a tool-change time, from the machine or typed into the form for the run; gEdit ships no rate of its own.
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed.
- **Q4-11. The rapid kind.** Controls move rapids either on a straight line or each axis at its own rate (a parameter). Proposed: a machine value with "straight line" as the documented default, because it gives the longer (safer) estimate when the axes have equal rates. Question: which do the owner's machines use?
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed.
- **Q4-12. Block numbers of new lines** (expanded cycles, auto-number). Proposed: an expanded cycle keeps its block number on the first block and the others get none (Klartext renumbers); auto-space and auto-number are off in every built-in profile and switched on per profile or per session. Question: on or off by default for the owner's controls?
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed.

**Editor and comfort (P4c–P4h)**

- **Q4-13. Next and previous NC event.** Proposed: not built (Find All with a word condition does it).
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed. Not built. P4.16 is dropped (its number stays unused); the §2.1 row says "Not built (owner, 2026-10-12)".
- **Q4-14. The aligned diff with merge.** Proposed: not built unless the owner finds review mode too limited.
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** changed. The aligned NC-aware diff with merge **is built**, in P4e after the word marks (P4.25, size L, no longer optional; Sonnet, high for the UI). Exit criterion: X30. Design: [file-compare.md](file-compare.md#aligned-nc-aware-diff-with-merge).
- **Q4-15. A sync timeline of the channels.** Proposed: cut; it leans toward a schedule of the machine, which the scope keeps out.
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed. Cut.
- **Q4-16. Sync presets per control.** Proposed: no work until a machine's wait codes are known (D64); the presets stay generic and marked "verify".
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed. It waits on the data of the owner's machines (D64).
- **Q4-17. References from other documents after a renumber.** Proposed: listed; updated only in open documents, each as its own undo step, after one confirmation; files that are not open are never read or written.
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed.
- **Q4-18. Interface languages.** Proposed: the switch and the loading of a second message set are built; no translation ships until someone offers one. Question: does the owner want a German interface, and would they review it?
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed.
- **Q4-19. The small per-profile options.** Proposed: per-profile `tabWidth` and `rulers` (a ruler at the control's longest block); cut the per-profile completion mode, the hover delay and modifier, and the recently used tab switcher (Switch Tab exists).
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed. Per-profile `tabWidth` and `rulers` are built (P4.42); the rest is cut.
- **Q4-20. A tool-word variant for short lathe `T` words.** Proposed: closed, since the owner decided a short word is the station alone (D49) and the "last digit" choice exists.
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed. Closed; the roadmap row is removed.
- **Q4-21. File associations.** The built-in profiles also claim extensions that other programs use (`txt`, `h`, `iso`, `sub`). Proposed: register `nc`, `tap`, `cnc`, `eia`, `ncc`, `ptp`, `min`, `ssb`, `mpf`, `spf` and not `txt`, `h`, `iso`, `sub`. Question: which should gEdit open on a double-click?
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed. `nc`, `tap`, `cnc`, `eia`, `ncc`, `ptp`, `min`, `ssb`, `mpf`, `spf`; not `txt`, `h`, `iso`, `sub`.
- **Q4-22. Printing where a webview cannot print.** Proposed: "Export as HTML" there, saved with the save dialog.
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed.
- **Q4-23. Moving the configuration folder.** Proposed: export and import of the whole configuration are built; moving the folder works through a pointer file in the default folder. Question: does the owner need it (a shared network folder, a portable install), or is export and import enough?
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** changed. Export and import of the whole configuration only. **No relocation and no pointer file**; the configuration folder stays where it is (P4.36, X32).

**Releases**

- **Q4-24. Versions.** Proposed: v1.0.0 is published first; P4a's release is v1.1.0 and carries the "Unreleased" changes of bug-fix round 1; each later milestone that lands is the next minor version, each a draft the owner checks and publishes.
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** changed. **v1.0.0 is never published.** Its draft release was deleted the same day; the tag `v1.0.0` stays (the ruleset protects it). The first 1.x release is **v1.1.0, after P4a**, carrying bug-fix round 1 (CHANGELOG "Unreleased") and P4a; its CHANGELOG entry starts with one line saying it includes v1.0.0, which was not published as a release, and links the v1.0.0 entry, which stays as history. Each later milestone that lands is the next minor version, a draft the owner publishes. The Phase 1 exit check (the bundles on Windows and Linux) moves to the v1.1.0 release checklist.

**Model choice**

- **Q4-25. The P4e prelude on Opus.** Proposed: the editor-group contract by Opus, high, as an exception to the rule that non-NC work goes to Sonnet (P4e prelude).
  **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-12):** as proposed. The P4e prelude on Opus, high.

**Channel view and order (the owner, 2026-10-17)**

- **Panes:** the side-by-side channel view shows **one pane per channel**, up to four, all aligned at the wait codes (a program of the owner has three channels, one file each). AD-50 changed accordingly; split view keeps two panes.
- **Order:** **P4e follows P4a directly**; P4b, P4c and P4d come after it. The milestone letters stay as they are (they name the contents, not the order).
- **Scrolling (2026-10-17, asked by the P4e prelude):** all channel panes always scroll together, whichever pane the mouse is over; the pane under the mouse takes the wheel and every pane ends at the same place, so there is no leading pane to choose. The alignment at the wait codes decides how the other panes follow (§6.7 rule 12; §7 #385).
- **Undo (2026-10-17):** **one** undo history for the side-by-side view, not one per channel. With channel sections in one file it is the program's own history; with one file per channel the view keeps one history over the channel documents while it is open, each document keeps its own after the view closes, and Save stays per file (§5 P4e decision 2, §6.7 rule 15; §7 #386).
- **Per pane (2026-10-17):** every channel pane has its own minimap and scroll map; the status bar and the inspector show the **active channel**, the pane with the keyboard focus; the active pane has a coloured border in the theme's accent (§5 P4e decisions 4, 5).
- **Performance (2026-10-17):** no proof for four panes on 300,000 lines — programs that long come from 3D and 5-axis milling, not from two- or three-channel lathes. The 300,000-line clause of X29 and the pane budget of X34 hold for two panes and are **waived for three and four**; the single-editor typing budget must not move (§5 P4e decision 14; §7 #387).
- **Wait codes across channels (2026-10-17):** a wait code carries a `P` word that lists the channels taking part, as digits: `P12` = channels 1 and 2, `P123` = channels 1, 2 and 3. Channel 1 `M901P12` and channel 2 `M901P12` meet there (whichever arrives first waits for the other); channel 3 is not involved and does not stop. Channel 1, 2 and 3 each `M910P123`: all three meet. So a wait pairs with the same code and the same `P` set in exactly the channels the `P` names, the n-th occurrence with the n-th; a channel not in the `P` gets no alignment point for it; a mark is unmatched when a channel its `P` names has no n-th occurrence of it (or has it with another `P`) (§5 P4e decision 6, §6.7 rule 12; §7 #388). This **partly answers D64** for the owner's Fanuc-style machines: the `P` form is "path numbers as digits" (the presets' `decode: 'digits'`); which M-code range each machine uses stays the machine's setting.
- **The prelude's four questions (2026-10-17), all decided as proposed:** the channel panes follow the "Show minimap" setting (each pane always has its own overview ruler); a machine with more than four channels shows the first four in declared order and the bar names the rest; lines between sections that belong to no channel are in no pane, and the bar gives their count; **F6 / Shift+F6** move between panes, split view has no key.

**P4e review answers (the owner, 2026-10-17)**

- **NC-2. A pane edit that would move lines to another channel.** Question: may an edit in a channel pane change, add or remove a section start so that lines move to another channel? **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-17):** no; the edit is **refused** with an explanation, and a new line typed just above a section start goes to the end of the section above, in the same channel. A second round extended the refusal to typing `M30` in the middle of a section and to Enter after a section's end line. The sentence: *A pane only edits its own channel's lines. To end a section early or add lines after its end, edit the program itself.* (§7 #455).
- **NC-4. Siemens `WAITM` lists.** Question: should lists that differ per channel pair channel by channel, as Check Wait Codes does, with `SETM` answering, while the same-`P` rule stays for every wait written with a `P` word? **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-17):** yes, and the same-`P` rule holds also for a rule built on the machine page (the skeptic's correction: discriminate on the partner form, not on `samePartners`) (§7 #456).
- **NC-1. Copying a word that sits elsewhere in the other line.** Question: replace it where it is, or refuse? **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-17):** replace when its address letter occurs exactly once on each line; otherwise refuse and point to the line copy. Never a letter twice or one dropped (§7 #451).
- **NC-3, follow-up.** A wait rule built on the machine page with a `P` word should let Check Wait Codes report a `P`-less wait meeting one with `P`: **yes, as a separate small fix** after P4e lands (the NC fix round, TODO.md).
- **NC-6, NC-8.** A mode-aware copy warning (G90/G91, inch/metric) and character marks inside long marked words: **later**, roadmap ideas.
- **Words (grilling round, 2026-10-17):** `GLOSSARY.md` is the vocabulary of the interface and the docs: **channel** (not path or turret, except when a dialect is quoted), **channel view** (the command title *Channels Side by Side* stays), channel pane, split view, editor group, wait, mark, partners, matched, section, section start, header, lines outside channels, shared line, aligned view, block (one NC line), change (a group of differing lines); the cycles and time terms (drilling cycle, expand, motion data, limits, estimated time, not estimated). The undo refusal reads *…Close the channel view to undo in that file.* (§7 #471, #461).
- **Typing helpers in the aligned view (2026-10-17):** now, in every editable pane, by its own document's settings, none on the read-only side (§7 #466).
- **Typing proof (2026-10-17):** if CODE-5's quiet-machine run still misses 30 ms, fix before landing (repaint a pane only when the edit touches its lines); if it passes, the saving stays for later performance work.
- **Order (grilling round 2, 2026-10-17):** P4e lands; then a **small NC fix round on `main`** as one batch (the taper `R` bug of `G90`/`G92`/`G94` in address arithmetic, the P-word rules from the machine page of NC-3, the all-zero `PLANE SPATIAL … TURN`), with no release of its own; then the tag **v1.2.0** (the owner checks the newest draft only; v1.1.0 stays unpublished like v1.0.0 unless it is published first); then review checklists of the G10 batches as pages under `docs/planning/reviews/` (the templates first; a fully ticked page lets a mechanical batch remove "review pending"); then P4b, whose prelude starts with extracting `_nc_run.py` (CODE-6), the transforms moved onto it, the goldens unchanged. The release checklist gains a step for the manual checks of the phase plan (§10). TODO.md gets the P4e backlog. **Note (2026-10-17, later that day):** the release step moved to the end: the tag v1.2.0 comes after all remaining milestones and backlog, not after the NC fix round (see "Release scope and the waves" below); the NC fix round joins Wave X as backlog batches.


**Release scope and the waves (the owner, 2026-10-17)**

1. **No incremental minor releases.** D70 (a draft release after each milestone, §4 rule 2, P2 §5.3) is superseded. The next public release is **v1.2.0** and it contains **everything in TODO.md**: P4b, P4c, P4d, P4f, P4g, P4h, every backlog item (P4a, P4e and the older ones) and the owner's open reviews and checks. Each TODO item ends as done, closed with a reason (moved to the roadmap's Not planned) or done by the owner. v1.1.0 (tag pushed, draft built) stays unpublished like v1.0.0. Milestones still land on `main` one by one and `## Unreleased` in the CHANGELOG keeps growing until v1.2.0. No dry-run builds per milestone; one full local build and run at the end, then the tag, then the release workflow drafts all three platforms, then the owner checks and publishes. Backplot stays out (Not planned).
2. **The order of work** (replaces the earlier steps after P4e): P4e lands; then (a) the **triage** of every TODO item into a table the owner approves; (b) in parallel the owner's **checklist pages** (reviews in `docs/planning/reviews/`, machine facts, manual checks); (c) in parallel the **Brand batch** (gEdit's look follows peterbuilds.dev: one orange `#F25C19` in both modes, used sparingly; slate surfaces; a slate status bar with orange accents; warnings yellow; Space Grotesk for the few headings; the NC syntax colours kept; a new brand app icon the owner approves first); then **Wave X** (P4b, P4c and P4d side by side, plus backlog batches in untouched areas, including the NC fix round: the taper `R` in address arithmetic, the NC-3 follow-up, the all-zero `PLANE SPATIAL … TURN`), then **Wave Y** (P4f, P4g, P4h), then the final local build and run, then the tag v1.2.0. Each milestone keeps its own prelude, packages, integration, reviews and skeptic; the hot shared files (the pin test, CHANGELOG, TODO, the plan, the roadmap, the i18n index) are edited only at the merge. Working limits: about 8 agents at once, at most 3 running tests, one GitHub runtime-suite run per wave on the combined branch, then each milestone lands as its own squash commit in turn.

**v1.2.0 triage (the owner, 2026-10-17)**

The owner approved the triage of every TODO item ([v1.2-triage.md](v1.2-triage.md): 267 rows, 152 to build, 51 closed, 48 for the owner, 12 waiting on a fact, 4 umbrellas) and answered its 16 questions. Row numbers are the triage's.

- **A1. The NC fix round runs now**, beside the Brand batch and before Wave X, not as a Wave X backlog batch: it edits `address_arithmetic.py`, `translate.py`, `_nc_geom.py` and `_nc_path.py`, which P4b's prelude moves onto `_nc_run.py`, and the code databases that P4.6 owns alone. P4b's prelude branches from `main` after it has landed. It holds T009, T010, T011, T012, T013, T044 and T111; BL-NC-fuzz (T003, the read-only fuzz review of the transforms) runs beside it.
- **A2. The roadmap backlog R01–R14 stays out of v1.2.0** and stays in the roadmap's backlog. R10 and R11 (the Okuma machining-centre profile and the Klartext Q-parameter feeds) wait on a fact.
- **A3. NC-6, NC-8 and the later-version transforms stay in v1.2.0.** The transforms (T015–T019, T021) are scheduled **last in Wave Y**, each through the fuzz review and the skeptic.

T173 closes** (M12's NC-15 was never recorded). **A15. T151 closes** (no reordering of built-in rows in the template manager). All four are in the roadmap's Not planned.

T141 builds**: the ribbon tab is remembered between starts. **A14. T154 builds as the display of the unit only** (a unit beside a formula field; no check of the result's unit).

- **A10. A "default stays" tick on a machine-facts page closes that fact.** **A11. BLOCKED-FACT items only the owner can answer** (T089, T098, T099, T100, T125, R10, R11) **close at Wave Y if unanswered**; they stay fail-safe (unknown code, unknown word) until then.
- **A12. An agent may run G11 over `tests/real/`** (the transforms, the expansion and the statistics; T006), counts only, evidence in `docs/planning/` and never the programs' names or content.
- **A13. P4c's two members `editing.autoSpace` and `editing.autoNumber` are loader defaults (off), and P4c writes no built-in profile file**; P4d keeps `src/lib/data/profiles/*.json` to itself (P4.19). Q4-12 already says off everywhere.
- **A16. The NC fix round has its own NC review and skeptic** as a small milestone. The other backlog batches get one combined code review, NC review and skeptic per wave (Q3 of 2026-10-17).
- **Brand batch:** icon 4 is chosen (T050), the feed colour is violet, and Space Grotesk is not used for headings in Settings.
- **Order:** pre-wave step (Brand, the NC fix round with its review, BL-NC-fuzz, the checklist pages, these records), Wave X (P4b, P4c, P4d and the backlog batches), Wave Y (P4f, P4g, P4h and the Wave Y batches, the later-version transforms last), the final local build and run, then the tag v1.2.0.

**Review answers (P4a, the owner, 2026-10-16)**

- **Q-A. Translate with a turned table (review NC-13).** Question: with a rotary table turned and tool centre point control on, is a shift of the program a shift of the part? **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-16):** on the owner's 5-axis machines the program coordinates turn with the table, so translate and address arithmetic's shift stay allowed with the table turned. No code change (§7, the row for NC-13).
- **Q-B. The Y move under the lathe Z mirror (review NC-14).** Question: does a Y move on a lathe change sign in the mirror for the counter spindle? **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-16):** yes. Y, an incremental Y and the arc vector J change sign about the turning axis, like Z (§7 #321, #355).
- **Q-C. The time limit of the geometry scripts (review CODE-1).** Question: 300 s (X34) or more? **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-16):** 900 s (`timeout = 900` in the three headers); the scripts are optimised after (§7 #335, #350).
- **Q-D. The Klartext workpiece blank (review NC-12).** Question: what do rotate and mirror do with `BLK FORM`? **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-16):** leave it as written and say so in one information row per run (§7 #318).

**Translate and address arithmetic at the start of a selection (the owner, 2026-10-19)**

- **Q-E. A selection's first move misses a shifted axis.** **Decided:** translate adds the missing Z (the shifted axis) instead of refusing, when the position is known as a number (§7 #513).
- **Q-F. A cycle at the level of the lines above.** **Decided:** refused like `G98`, `G99` included (§7 #514).
- **Q-G. A lone `T`.** **Decided:** a lone `T` counts as a tool change on mills too, so the position is unknown after it (§7 #513).
- **Q-H. A missing diameter X.** **Decided:** stays refused; a diameter word is never added (§7 #513).
- **Q-I. Address arithmetic across a tool change.** **Decided:** the cycle is left unchanged with a warning (§7 #515).

**The manual check (the owner, 2026-10-25 and 2026-10-26)**

The agents compared what gEdit assumes with the control manuals in six areas (drilling cycles, wait codes and channels, tool words, feeds and numbers without a point, lathe cycles, tilted planes and tool centre point control: 213 assumptions in all). The owner went through the mismatches and answered each area with "go with the recommendations"; the fix round followed (§7 #577–#605). The decisions, by area:

- **1, drilling cycles.** D1-1: a Fanuc `,D` in `G73`/`G83` is refused now; reading it as the retract or clearance kept until the cycle ends is a follow-up. D1-2: a Fanuc cycle block refuses any address word that is not a code, an `M`, `S`, `T` or `F` word, a block number or a parameter of that cycle. D1-3: `CYCLE82` arguments 10 to 14 are described and stay refused. D1-4: `CYCLE83` with `_DIS1` = 0 stays refused for v1.2.0. D1-5: `CYCLE86` keeps gEdit's path order; one line in the user guide.
- **2, wait codes and channels.** D2-1: the Okuma `P-10` is a numbered wait in both turrets (verify). D2-2: the Fanuc same-`P` check compares the set as written and reports a `P` that does not name its own path (the view follows). D2-3: the guide's example is `M901 P10`, a leading 0 is not read. D2-4: Okuma checks for a spindle command at a `P` code, an `M100` met by a `P` code (a warning) and an `M100` under `G41`/`G42`. D2-5: the small checks (every mark of `SETM`/`CLEARM`, `WAITM` marks 0 to 99, a wait that shares its block, the `M198` label, first rule wins). D2-6: Okuma stays at two turrets. D2-7: presets for Nakamura-Tome and the Star SV-20 now; Citizen, Traub and WFL later.
- **3, tool words.** D3-1: the Fanuc lathe `T1`/`T12` stay "station" (verify), with one sentence in the guide; D49 closes with that wording. D3-2: an Okuma `T100` is a tool change under two-digit offsets. D3-3: the guide says plainly that the tool list of an Okuma milling program is not reliable; the real fix is the machining-centre profile (R9). D3-4: three machine choices (lathe turret or tool changer, Sinumerik turning `T` or `M6`, Fanuc mill `T` on the `M6` line loaded or next). D3-5: notes only. D3-6: the records are corrected (the five-digit `T` of syntax-fanuc is superseded by the decision of 2026-10-08, `T100` in syntax-okuma belongs to the 32/64/96 sets, "a user's lathe").
- **4, feeds and numbers without a point.** D4-1: the lathe presets count a point-less feed per revolution in 0.0001 mm/rev; the 0.01 of the Series 15 format is a choice. D4-2: the choices follow the parameters each kind of machine has. D4-3: only position words go through the distance-mode check of Address Arithmetic. D4-4: no code for a dwell per revolution; one line in the guide. D4-5: a change that rounds back says so.
- **5, lathe cycles.** D5-1: the Okuma LAP `G84` change points move with a shift. D5-2: a pass with `I` or `K` is not resolved in the extents. D5-3: system B defaults to no incremental `U`/`W`. D5-4: system C is not added; Check Program hints at it. D5-5: Check Program warns about a block that repeats a pass. D5-6: Okuma `G31` to `G33` are fixed cycles. D5-7: Q-B stays the default, with a machine setting *Counter spindle*. D5-8: the lathe drilling cycles are reviewed. D5-9: data gaps. D5-10: taper passes under the Z mirror stay refused.
- **6, tilted planes and tool centre point control.** D6-1: Sinumerik `ROTS`, `AROTS`, `CROTS`, `TOFRAME`, `TOROT`, `PAROT` as frames. D6-2: `G53.6` like `G53.1`. D6-3: `G68.2` to `G68.4` no longer conflict with tool centre point control. D6-4: Q-A stays; the translate result and the guide say that a Fanuc mill profile assumes the table coordinate system (19696 bit 5 = 0). D6-5: an all-zero `CYCLE800` closes the tilt. D6-6: cycle 19 turns the rotary axes when `19.1` writes `F` or `ABST`. D6-7: no rotate or mirror of an incremental move after a shift (`SD42440`). D6-8: wording and `SEQ`/`SYM`.
- **Follow-up (2026-10-25), D2-4c-rev.** An Okuma spindle command in a block **without** a `P` is not checked, because the control manual's own example writes them so; only a spindle command in a `P` block whose `P` the other turret lacks is reported. It supersedes the literal D2-4 (c).
- **Review round (2026-10-26).** NC-2: yes, **Pass repeated** also for a lathe block with only an `F` word or only a modal `G` code. NC-11: the system C hint stays in Check Program, not at file open. NC-9: the lathe drilling `R` label is fixed (not "always" incremental), plus an information note on *Series 15 format* lathes, no refusal.
- **Open follow-ups (TODO.md).** Reading `,D`; `CYCLE83` `_DIS1` = 0; an Okuma machine with three saddles; presets for Citizen, Traub and WFL; the Okuma machining-centre profile (R9); taper passes under the Z mirror; the system C hint at file open (it needs a system C machine choice, which gEdit does not have; Check Program carries it meanwhile, NC-11).

**Settings, keys and packages (the P4g prelude, 2026-10-27)**

The prelude's rules are in [§6.9](#69-keys-packages-and-the-configuration-zip-p4g); the zip crate is approved (P11) and its licence recorded there. The questions carry the letter g (Q4g-1…Q4g-6) because the P4f prelude numbered its own questions Q4-26…Q4-31. The owner answered all six on 2026-10-27: each as recommended, which is what the prelude built.

- **Q4g-1. Import only adds and replaces.** Question: should "Import Configuration" ever *delete* files that the zip does not hold (to make a computer equal to the zip)? Proposed: **no**. An import adds files and replaces files of the same name; a zip with three profiles leaves your other profiles alone, and a backup zip of everything it replaced is written first (§6.9 rules 28, 30). **Owner answer: accepted as recommended, 2026-10-27.**
- **Q4g-2. Code in a configuration zip.** Question: may a zip carry scripts and packages that run Python? Proposed: **yes, with care**. They are exported and shown in the preview, their groups start unticked, and Import stays off until the user ticks "I trust this file" (§6.9 rule 31). The interpreter and the extra script folders (`scripts.python`, `scripts.folders`) are never exported and never taken from a zip, so a file cannot choose what runs. **Owner answer: accepted as recommended, 2026-10-27.**
- **Q4g-3. Package scripts are not edited in place.** Question: may a package's scripts be opened and changed where they are? Proposed: **no**. Like the scripts that ship with gEdit they are copied to your scripts folder first ("Copy to user folder"), where the copy replaces the original in the menu. A package then stays exactly as its author shipped it and can be replaced by a newer copy. **Owner answer: accepted as recommended, 2026-10-27.**
- **Q4g-4. Packages are copied in, not linked.** Question: does anyone keep a package as a Git checkout elsewhere and link it into the packages folder? Proposed: **no link is followed**; a package folder that is a link is listed with a note, and the package is copied in (§6.9 rule 8). **Owner answer: accepted as recommended, 2026-10-27.**
- **Q4g-5. The language changes at the next start.** Question: must the interface switch while gEdit runs? Proposed: **no**; the setting applies at the next start (§6.9 rule 40), and there is no "follow the system" choice until a translation ships. **Owner answer: accepted as recommended, 2026-10-27.**
- **Q4g-6. Which keys can be bound.** Question: may a user bind a bare letter or Shift+letter? Proposed: **no**; a key needs Ctrl, Cmd or Alt, or is a function key, and the clipboard, undo and the system's own keys are not offered (§6.9 rule 4). Keys that may type a character on some keyboards (Ctrl+Alt on a Swiss German layout) are allowed with a notice. **Owner answer: accepted as recommended, 2026-10-27.**

## 10. Manual checks for the owner

- P4a: translate, rotate and mirror a copy of a real milling program and run it in the machine's own simulation or as a dry run above the part; translate a lathe program in X and Z with the machine chosen and check the diameter; mirror a main-spindle lathe program in Z for the counter spindle and check it on the machine's simulation (the arcs, the compensation side and the tool nose direction in the offset); read the refusal rows of a program that has a frame or a subprogram call.
- P4b: expand a drilling cycle of a real program and compare the moves with the control's own cycle on the machine's simulation; set the machine's rapid rates and tool-change time and compare the estimated time with a real run (the estimate should be lower); a program with a spindle speed above the machine's top speed.
- P4c: type a few blocks with auto-space and auto-number on; print a program on paper and to PDF on each platform.
- P4d: renumber a program with `M98 P… Q…` and `M99 P` and run it in the simulation.
- P4e: edit the channels of a real multi-channel program in the channel view (a two-turret program with sections, and the three-channel set with one file per channel) and check that the wait codes stay lined up, that the panes scroll together and that one undo takes back the last edit wherever it was made; try to type a line after a section's end and in front of a section start in a pane (refused, document unchanged); hover an unmatched wait and read why; merge a split back; compare two re-posted programs in the aligned view, copy a change, a line and a word both ways and undo.
- P4f: double-click an NC file on Windows, macOS and Linux with gEdit open and closed; search a folder of programs for a tool.
- P4g: bind a key of the owner's own on a Windows keyboard with AltGr (Swiss German) and check that it does not take an AltGr character.
- P4h: make a user profile with the form and test it on a program.
- P4a also releases v1.1.0: the release checklist with the installers on the three platforms, which includes the Phase 1 exit check (the bundles on Windows and Linux; Q4-24).
- After each release: the checklist of [P2 §13](phase-2-implementation.md#13-manual-checks-for-the-owner-cannot-be-automated-here) on the three platforms, then publish the draft when satisfied.
